# raccoon-vtt design

This document is the technical design of raccoon-vtt as built: the MVP in [features.md](features.md), with each v1 wave folded in as it shipped. The addenda keep the full reasoning for v1 waves 3 and 4 ([design-v1-maps-vision.md](design-v1-maps-vision.md)), wave 5 ([design-v1-dm-tools.md](design-v1-dm-tools.md)) and the whole-app visual pass ([design-v1-visual.md](design-v1-visual.md)).

## 1. Constraints

These come from the project owner and from the deployment target. Each one is load-bearing somewhere below.

- D&D groups playing in person, each player on a phone, and players who join from elsewhere. One server hosts many groups, a room each. 2024 rules (SRD 5.2.1). Square grid, 5 ft per cell.
- Mobile first. Phones are the main client, then tablets and desktops.
- Live sync between devices. WebSockets must reconnect on their own and keep disruption to a minimum.
- DM view and player view. Players are trusted, but a reload or a normal screen must never reveal fog, hidden things, or what is under the fog.
- Rooms have IDs and passwords. Anyone who can reach the site can create a room, and its creator becomes the DM through a DM key link.
- The DM can let players move tokens they do not own.
- Plain Node.js and a modern React. No Next.js. The server framework should be whatever makes the app most robust.
- Performant without hyper-optimizing. Nothing should use resources for no reason.
- Exposed to the internet only through an HTTPS reverse proxy or tunnel container on the `raccoon-edge` network (for example Caddy, nginx or Cloudflare's cloudflared), which may also serve other sites on the same host.
- Dice roll on the server (docs/design-v1-dice.md, v1 wave 7): the room actor draws every die with a secure random source, so everyone sees the same result and nobody can fake one, and a roll needs a connection. Anyone may still roll real dice or use the D&D Beyond app and type the total. Until docs/design-v1-roll-options.md moves it to the server, the DM's device rolls monster initiative (section 9.4).
- Image search through SerpApi's free plan.
- No AI-generated art. Every image shipped with the app comes from an openly licensed source and is credited (section 20).

## 2. Architecture overview

```
 phone / tablet / desktop browsers
   React 19 SPA (Vite build)  ── HTTPS (JSON API, image upload, assets)
          │                   ── WSS  /ws  (ops, events, ephemeral)
          ▼
 HTTPS front end ── a reverse proxy or tunnel container (for example cloudflared), in its own compose project
          │  raccoon-edge (internal), service alias "raccoon"
          ▼
 ┌──────────────── app container (one Node 24 process) ─────────────────┐
 │ Fastify 5: static SPA, /api/*, /ws (@fastify/websocket on ws)        │
 │ RoomManager ── RoomActor per loaded room (single writer, in memory)  │
 │    op: validate (zod) → dedupe → authorize → reduce (shared)         │
 │    → commit to Postgres → publish per-viewer projection diffs        │
 │ Asset pipeline (sharp, 1 job at a time) ── /data/assets volume       │
 │ Image search + URL import ── undici ProxyAgent ──┐                   │
 └───────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┘
             │ raccoon-data (internal)              │ raccoon-proxy (internal)
             ▼                                      ▼
        Postgres 17                       egress (squid, 2 ports)
                                                    │ raccoon-egress
                                                    ▼
                      serpapi.com, api.openverse.org / public image hosts
```

The key decisions:

1. **Server-authoritative state with per-viewer projection.** The server owns each room's state. Every change is checked against the sender's role, applied, committed, and then sent to each connected viewer in the form that viewer may see. Players never receive hidden tokens, DM-only fields, or tokens under the fog. CRDTs (Yjs, Automerge) were rejected because they replicate the whole document to every client and cannot keep secrets.
2. **One pure, deterministic reducer shared by the server and the client.** `applyOp(state, envelope, actor)` lives in `shared/`. The server runs it authoritatively. The client runs it on its own projected state to show edits instantly (optimistic updates). Crash recovery replays the op log through the same function. Determinism has three rules:
   - The server stamps every op with an envelope `{seq, at}` before reducing. The reducer takes time and derived IDs (`${seq}:${index}`) only from it.
   - The one non-deterministic command, undo or redo, is resolved by the server into a concrete op before the reducer runs. Only the resolved op is logged. The app's one dice roll, the DM's auto initiative for monsters (section 9.4), happens on the DM's device and reaches the server as an ordinary `encounter.setValues`, so no randomness reaches the reducer. Since v1 wave 7 the dice ops (`dice.roll`, `roll.dice`, `actor.deathRoll`) are resolved the same way as undo: the actor draws every die after the op is authorized and stores the `sys.dice` it builds, holding every face, so the reducer only copies results and replay never rolls (docs/design-v1-dice.md 4.2).
   - The reducer has no other input.
3. **One rules module shared by the server and the client.** Grid geometry, distances, area coverage, initiative order, duration ticking and HP transitions live in `shared/rules/` with unit tests. The preview on a phone and the server's answer always agree.
4. **Commit before publish.** An op is visible to anyone, including its sender's confirmation, only after its row is committed. So everything a client ever sees is durable, and "applied" and "durable" never differ. The cost is the commit latency (about 20 to 30 ms per batch), which nobody notices because drags and previews travel on the ephemeral channel.
5. **Snapshot on every connect.** A device that connects or reconnects gets a full projected snapshot of its scene, then live events. There is no event-replay path to get wrong. Projected snapshots are small and bounded (section 10.5).
6. **One process, one writer per room.** Rooms load into memory on demand and unload when idle. A per-room queue applies ops in order. This needs no locks and no distributed coordination. Three to eight people per room and a handful of rooms is far below what one process handles, and hard caps (section 10.5) keep an abusive room from changing that.

## 3. Stack

Exact versions are pinned when the project is scaffolded. These are the versions current on 2026-10-12. pnpm's default `minimumReleaseAge` of one day is kept, so a pin to a package released in the last day waits.

| Area | Choice | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Runtime | Node 24 LTS. The Docker image pins `node:24-trixie-slim` (24.21) by digest. | glibc image, because sharp and musl Node are both less well supported on Alpine. |
| TypeScript | `typescript` 7.0.2 as `tsc --noEmit` only. Node runs the server's `.ts` files directly (type stripping). | No server build step. Shared code must use erasable syntax only: no enums, no namespaces, no parameter properties, `.ts` extensions on imports. |
| Server | Fastify 5 with `@fastify/websocket` (on `ws` 8), `@fastify/static`, `@fastify/cookie`, `@fastify/rate-limit`. | The most robust option, as section 1 asks. Hooks run before the WebSocket upgrade, so auth and Origin checks reject cleanly. Body limits, logging (pino), and `inject` for tests come built in. Bare `node:http` plus `ws` was the runner-up at about 300 more lines we would own. |
| Validation | `zod` 4 schemas in `shared/protocol.ts`. | One schema validates and bounds input on the server and types the client. |
| Client | React 19, Vite 8 (bundler only), `react-konva` 19 with Konva 10, Zustand 5 (vanilla store). | Konva gives drag, transforms and pixel-accurate hit testing with a small bundle (about 56 KB gzipped). Owlbear Rodeo 1.0 used the same pairing. PixiJS is faster but has no drag, transformer or pinch, and its React binding is stale. |
| Database | Postgres 17 (`postgres:17-alpine`), `pg` 8 with hand-written SQL, `db/schema.sql` applied at boot. | A small, well-known stack: hand-written SQL and one schema file applied at boot, with no ORM. |
| Images | `sharp` 0.35, `file-type` for magic bytes. | Decode, resize, re-encode to WebP, strip metadata. |
| Passwords | `@node-rs/argon2` (argon2id, OWASP parameters m=19456 KiB, t=2, p=1). | Prebuilt binaries for every platform with no install script, and the same behavior on a development machine (Node 24.7) and in the image. Node's built-in `crypto.argon2` also exists in 24.7, but its fixes landed later in the 24 line. |
| Outbound HTTP | `undici` `ProxyAgent`, `ipaddr.js`. | Explicit egress through squid (section 10.6). |
| Room export | `yazl` and `yauzl` (zip). | Small and stable. |
| Exact geometry (v1 wave 3) | `robust-predicates` 3.0.3 (Unlicense, no dependencies). | Shewchuk's exact orientation predicate, for the wall set's split (section 9.7). |
| Tests | `node:test` with type stripping. | No test framework dependency. |
| Package manager | pnpm 11.23 through corepack, pinned in `packageManager`. | Corepack and `packageManager` give every machine the same pnpm. |

## 4. Repository layout

A flat single package, no workspaces. Node subpath imports (`"imports": {"#shared/*": "./shared/*"}` in `package.json`) replace tsconfig `paths`, which Node's type stripping ignores. Vite resolves the same imports.

```
client/                 React SPA, the Vite root (vite.config.ts sets publicDir: '../public')
  index.html  main.tsx  App.tsx
  pages/                Home.tsx (create or join), Join.tsx, Room.tsx, and (v1 wave 5) Admin.tsx
  canvas/               Stage.tsx, layers/, tools/ (select, ruler, draw, aoe, fog, builder, ping)
  panels/               TokenPanel, Tracker, Scenes, Library, Log, Settings, ImagePicker, DmDevices
  components/           shared pieces (Button, Sheet, ActionBar, ConditionChip)
  state/                store.ts (Zustand), socket.ts (connection), pending.ts (optimistic queue)
  lib/                  gestures.ts, camera.ts, theme.ts, format.ts
  features/             one folder per feature, such as builder/ (its stamps/ from v1 wave 3, heights/ from wave 4),
                        scenes/, mapexport/, (v1 wave 3) uvtt/ and phases/, (v1 wave 4) vision/, lights/,
                        movement/ and doors/, and (v1 wave 5) display/, outliner/, rolls/, party/, journal/,
                        pins/, xcard/, device/ and admin/
server/
  main.ts               boot: config, db, Fastify, routes, ws, shutdown
  config.ts             environment, validated once at boot
  db.ts                 pg pool, schema apply, query helpers
  http/                 rooms.ts, join.ts, dm.ts, assets.ts, search.ts, export.ts, health.ts, and (v1 wave 5)
                        display.ts, xcard.ts, logExport.ts, static.ts and admin.ts
  rooms/                manager.ts, actor.ts, fanout.ts, persistence.ts, undo.ts, limits.ts, explore.ts (v1 wave 4)
  auth/                 sessions.ts, passwords.ts, dmkey.ts, ratelimit.ts, clientIp.ts, csrf.ts, and (v1 wave 5)
                        displaySessions.ts and adminSessions.ts
  assets/               upload.ts, process.ts, store.ts, serve.ts, fetchPublic.ts, serpapi.ts, and (v1 wave 5)
                        openverse.ts and cleanup.ts
shared/
  model.ts              entity types
  protocol.ts           zod schemas for every client and server message and op, with bounds
  reducer.ts            applyOp, pure and deterministic
  permissions.ts        canApply(op, actor, state), including reference checks
  project.ts            projection of state and entities for a viewer (allowlists)
  migrate.ts            snapshot and op migrations between versions
  ruleHooks.ts          where permissions and projection call the movement and vision rules (v1 wave 4)
  heightCodec.ts        a build chunk's heights and stair marks, read and written (v1 wave 4)
  visionLimits.ts       the sight piece cap, apart from limits.ts since it derives walls (v1 wave 4)
  imageRef.ts           an image read either way, an upload or a shipped icon (v1 wave 5)
  referencedAssets.ts   every asset the room state names, for cleanup (v1 wave 5)
  logText.ts            the log's wording, shared by the Log panel and the log export (v1 wave 5)
  rules/                grid.ts, footprint.ts, distance.ts, aoe.ts, walls.ts, fog.ts,
                        initiative.ts, durations.ts, hp.ts, (v1 wave 3) segments.ts, autoWalls.ts,
                        (v1 wave 4) heights.ts, movement.ts, vision.ts, and (v1 wave 5) skills.ts, rollReminders.ts
  stamps.ts             the shipped map stamps' keys and footprints, written by scripts/stamps.ts (v1 wave 3)
  gameIcons.ts          the token icons' folder and authors, written by scripts/game-icons.ts (v1 wave 5)
  credits.ts            every credit for the About page, and (v1 wave 5) licenses.ts, the license names apart
                        from it, so the room's code names an icon's license without the whole list
  statBlock.ts          custom monsters in the SRD monsters' shape (section 14.6)
  srd/                  conditions.ts, statuses.ts, monsters.json, area-spells.json, xp-budget.json, NOTICE.md
db/schema.sql
public/                 icon.svg, manifest.webmanifest, generated PNG icons, textures/, icons/, stamps/ (v1 wave 3),
                        game-icons/ (v1 wave 5)
scripts/                admin.ts (DM link, list rooms, import), srd-import.ts, icons.ts, textures.ts,
                        stamps.ts and bench-walls.ts (v1 wave 3), the wave 4 gate's bench-core4.ts,
                        bench-vision.ts, bench-server4.ts and bench-sight-overlay.ts, and (v1 wave 5)
                        game-icons.ts and backup-check.ts
docker/                 squid.conf, and (v1 wave 5) backup.sh
docs/                   features.md, design.md
CREDITS.md              every third-party asset, its source and license
Dockerfile  docker-compose.yml  docker-compose.server.yml  .env.example  .dockerignore
```

Two tsconfigs, so DOM types never leak into the server and Node types never leak into the client. `tsconfig.server.json` covers `server/`, `shared/` and `scripts/` with `module: nodenext` and `types: ["node"]`. `tsconfig.client.json` covers `client/` and `shared/` with `module: esnext`, `moduleResolution: bundler`, `jsx: react-jsx`, `lib: ["dom", "es2024"]` and `types: ["vite/client"]`. Both set `strict`, `noEmit`, `allowImportingTsExtensions`, `verbatimModuleSyntax` and `erasableSyntaxOnly`. `pnpm typecheck` runs both.

Tests sit next to the code as `*.test.ts`.

## 5. Coordinates and units

- **World unit: one grid cell.** Positions are in cells, as floats. Cell (0,0) is the top-left cell of the scene. Feet per cell is a room setting, 5 by default, bounded to 1 to 100.
- **Token position:** the top-left corner of the token's footprint, in cells. Snapping puts it on whole cells (Small and larger) or half cells (Tiny).
- **Elevation:** feet, an integer, can be negative. Each token also has a height in feet, which defaults to its footprint side (5 ft for Medium, 10 ft for Large).
- **Map image placement:**
  - The asset row records the canonical size of the map: the dimensions of its `full` variant, `widthPx` by `heightPx`.
  - `scene.setMap` copies that canonical size into the scene as `map.widthPx` and `map.heightPx`, so every client can place the image before loading it.
  - The scene stores `cellPx` (canonical pixels per cell) and `originPx` (the canonical pixel on a grid intersection), both in that canonical space.
  - The image covers the world rectangle from `(-originPx.x / cellPx, -originPx.y / cellPx)` with size `widthPx / cellPx` by `heightPx / cellPx`. Every variant, `mobile` included, is stretched to exactly that rectangle. So a phone and a desktop agree with the printed grid.
  - The alignment tool only edits `cellPx` and `originPx`. A map with a grid drawn into the art and a map without one work the same way. The only difference is whether the app draws grid lines on top.
  - "Or count the squares" in the scene settings is for art whose squares run edge to edge: it sets `originPx` to 0 and `cellPx` from the counts, and resizes the scene to fit. Its fields start from the image's squares under the current grid (`widthPx / cellPx` and `heightPx / cellPx`, rounded), not from the scene's size. A Universal VTT import with a fractional origin (section 14.9) also covers the partial square before its image, so the scene's size would show one square too many on each axis and warn that the squares are not square. A grid whose first line sits before the image (a negative `originPx`, which only such an import makes) offers no counts at all, since setting the origin to 0 would slide the image half a square under the imported walls.
- **Angles:** degrees, clockwise from +x (east), matching the canvas.

## 6. Data model

All entities have `id` and `rev`. Client-created entities get a UUIDv7 from the client, so an optimistic create needs no round trip. Assets are created by the server and get random UUIDv4 IDs, so an asset ID never reveals when it was first uploaded. A create op whose ID already exists is rejected. `rev` is incremented by the reducer on every change.

The room state is a set of normalized maps. Each entity kind is small and changes on its own, so an event carries only what changed.

```ts
type RoomState = {
  meta: RoomMeta
  members: Record<MemberId, Member>
  scenes: Record<SceneId, Scene>
  buildChunks: Record<ChunkKey, BuildChunk>     // key "<sceneId>:<cx>,<cy>", 16x16 cells each
  labels: Record<LabelId, Label>
  fogTiles: Record<TileKey, FogTile>            // key "<sceneId>:<tx>,<ty>", 16x16 cells each
  fogDrafts: Record<SceneId, FogDraft>          // DM only
  tokens: Record<TokenId, Token>
  characters: Record<CharacterId, Character>
  templates: Record<TemplateId, AreaTemplate>
  drawings: Record<DrawingId, Drawing>
  encounter: Encounter | null
  encounterHistory: EncounterSnapshot[]         // DM only, last 20, for Previous
  handouts: Record<HandoutId, Handout>
  handoutDocs: Record<HandoutDocId, HandoutDoc> // DM only: PDFs saved as documents of pages (docs/design-v1-pdf-handouts.md 3.2)
  log: LogEntry[]                               // last 200 in memory, older in room_log
  monsters: Record<MonsterId, CustomMonster>    // DM only, the room's own stat blocks (v1)
  companionBlocks: Record<CompanionBlockId, CompanionBlock> // a character's stat blocks for its companions, to the DM and that character's players (docs/design-v1-companion-stat-blocks.md 3.1)
  srdImages: Record<SrdId, SrdImage>            // DM only, default images for SRD monsters (v1)
  savedEncounters: Record<SavedEncounterId, SavedEncounter>   // DM only, fights built in the encounter builder (v1)
  stamps: Record<StampId, Stamp>                // pictures on a scene's board layer (v1 wave 3)
  lights: Record<LightId, Light>                // DM only, light sources: stored in v1 wave 3, lighting scenes since wave 4
  exploredTiles: Record<TileKey, FogTile>       // v1 wave 4: what the party has seen, a second fog layer only the server writes (section 13.6)
  journal: Record<JournalPageId, JournalPage>   // v1 wave 5: the DM's journal pages, room level like the monsters (section 14.16)
  pins: Record<PinId, Pin>                      // v1 wave 5: map pins, perhaps linked to a page (section 14.16)
  rolls: Record<RollId, RollRequest>            // v1 wave 5: open roll requests, room level (section 9.11)
}

type RoomMeta = { name, settings: RoomSettings, playerSceneId: SceneId | null,
  view: { follow: boolean, blackout: boolean } }

type RoomSettings = {
  feetPerCell: number                     // 5, bounded 1 to 100
  diagonal: 'five' | 'fiveTen' | 'euclid' // 2024 default: 'five'
  aoeCoverage: 'half' | 'any' | 'center'  // 2024 default: 'half'
  aoeInclusion: 'anyCell' | 'center' | 'majority'   // default: 'anyCell'
  pcHpVisibleToParty: boolean             // default true
  monstersDieAtZero: boolean              // default true
  playersSeeAoeOfOthers: boolean          // default true
  playersMoveAny: 'none' | 'all' | MemberId[]   // who may move tokens they do not own. Default 'none' (v1, was the project owner's switch)
  playersCreateCharacters: boolean        // default true in new rooms (the project owner, 2026-10-17), and off in older rooms that lack it (section 8.1)
  playersApplyDamage: boolean             // default false
  skipDefeated: boolean                   // default true
  monsterInitScore: boolean               // default false: use 10 + Dex modifier instead of a roll
  revealHiddenOnTurn: boolean             // default true (section 9.4)
  turnTimerSec: number                    // v1: seconds a turn has before the nudge, 0 to 600. Default 0 (off), and older rooms lack it, which reads as off (section 9.4)
  playersDraw?: 'none' | 'all' | MemberId[]   // v1 wave 5: who may draw (section 8.1). Absent reads as 'all'
}

type Member = { id, name, color, kind: 'dm' | 'player', createdAt }
// kind is a display label. Authority comes only from the socket's grant (section 10.2).

type Scene = {
  id, name, order, bounds: { w, h }       // cells, at most 200 x 200
  map?: { assetId, widthPx, heightPx, cellPx, originPx: { x, y } }   // widthPx, heightPx copied from the asset row
  grid: { show: boolean, color, opacity, lineWidth }
  background: string
  fogEnabled: boolean                     // default: true for map and builder scenes, false for a blank mat
  wallsVersion: number                    // bookkeeping, only goes up (sections 7.7 and 12)
  defaultView?: { x, y, zoom }
  autoWalls?: 'all' | 'floor' | 'off'     // v1 wave 3: which painted cells make automatic walls (section 12). Absent reads as 'all'
  folder?: string                         // v1 wave 3, DM only: the Scenes panel folder, at most 40 characters
  thumb?: AssetId                         // v1 wave 3, DM only: a `thumb` asset a DM device rendered (section 14.10)
  phases?: MapPhase[]                     // v1 wave 3, DM only: at most 8, the active one included (section 14.11)
  phaseId?: string                        // v1 wave 3, DM only: the active phase, present exactly when `phases` is
  phaseGrid?: { refWidthPx, refHeightPx, cellPx, originPx: { x, y } }   // v1 wave 3, DM only: the grid in reference pixels
  blockMovement?: boolean                 // v1 wave 4: walls and ledges stop players' moves (section 9.8). Absent reads as off
  vision?: { on: boolean, light: 'bright' | 'dim' | 'dark' }   // v1 wave 4: party vision and the light level (section 13.6). Absent reads as off
}
type MapPhase = { id, name, assetId, widthPx, heightPx,   // the size is the asset row's, filled in by the server
  light?: 'bright' | 'dim' | 'dark' }     // v1 wave 4: the light level switching to it gives the scene (section 14.11)

type BuildChunk = { sceneId, cx, cy, cells: Record<string, TerrainId>, edges: Record<string, EdgeFeature>,
  walls?: FreeWall[],                     // v1 wave 3: walls off the grid lines, each in the chunk that holds its midpoint
  heights?: string,                       // v1 wave 4: base64 of 256 little-endian Int16, ground in ft, row-major. Absent when all 0
  up?: string }                           // v1 wave 4: 256 stair marks, row-major: '.' none, 'a' by the rule, 'n' 'e' 's' 'w' by hand
type FreeWall = { id, a: [x, y], b: [x, y], f: WallFeature }   // cells, every coordinate a multiple of 1/64, at most 8 cells long
type Label = { id, sceneId, x, y, text, size }
type Stamp = { id, sceneId, art: { key } | { assetId },      // v1 wave 3: a shipped picture by key, or a `token` asset of the room
  x, y, w, h, rotation,                   // center and size in cells (0.25 to 20), degrees clockwise from east
  tint?: string, flip?: boolean, z: number, visibility: 'all' | 'dm' }
type Light = { id, sceneId, x, y,         // v1 wave 3: cells, on the 1/64 lattice like free walls
  bright: number, dim: number,            // ft: the bright radius, and dim light this far beyond it, each at most 120
  color?: string, on: boolean, walls: boolean, label?: string }   // walls: walls cast shadows from it

type FogTile = { sceneId, tx, ty, bits: string }   // base64, 16x16 cells x 4x4 sub-cells, 1 = revealed
type FogDraft = { sceneId, ops: FogOp[] }         // at most 500 ops
type SightTile = { b: string } | { f: 1 | 2 | 3 } // v1 wave 4, derived, never stored: 2 bits per sub-cell, or one level for the tile (section 13.6)

type Token = {
  id, sceneId, kind: 'pc' | 'npc' | 'monster' | 'object'
  name, publicName?: string, nameHidden: boolean
  image?: ImageRef
  size: 'tiny' | 'small' | 'medium' | 'large' | 'huge' | 'gargantuan' | 'custom'
  w?: number, h?: number                  // cells, only for 'custom', 0.5 to 20
  x, y, elevation, height?, rotation
  disposition: 'ally' | 'neutral' | 'hostile'
  visibility: 'all' | 'dm'
  fogExempt: boolean                      // set by the DM: shown to players even under fog (section 13.4)
  fogExemptAuto: boolean                  // set by reveal-on-turn, cleared when the encounter ends or its entry is removed. Lifts nothing since the project owner's decision of 2026-10-14 (section 13.4)
  locked: boolean
  layer: 'object' | 'token'
  ownerIds: MemberId[]
  actor: { ref: CharacterId } | { inline: Actor }
  hpVisibility: 'hidden' | 'label' | 'bar' | 'exact'  // default 'label' for npc and monster, 'exact' for pc
  number?: number                         // auto-numbering for duplicates ("Goblin 3")
  notes?: string                          // v1: the note for everyone who can see the token. The DM's own is actor.dmNotes
  altImages?: ImageRef[]                  // v1: at most 6 other images to swap in (Wild Shape, a disguise). DM and owners only
  rangeRings?: number[]                   // ft, at most 4, nearest first (v1, section 9.2)
  path?: { turn: number, stops: PathStop[] }   // v1: the active creature's moves this turn, at most 50 stops (section 9.2)
  squeeze?: true                          // v1 wave 4: stopped squeezed in a narrow opening, part of its pose (section 9.8)
  light?: { bright: number, dim: number } // v1 wave 4: a carried light at its eye, ft, each at most 120. DM and owners only
  partyVision?: boolean                   // v1 wave 4, DM only: a creature the DM runs that lends the party its sight
}
type ImageRef = { assetId, crop: { cx, cy, zoom } }   // an asset of kind `token` and its crop (section 11.4)
  | { icon: IconKey, color: string }      // v1 wave 5: a shipped icon, "<author>/<name>", on a disk of that color (section 11.5)
type PathStop = { x, y, elevation, uncounted?: true, seen?: true, joinedUnseen?: true }   // each later stop ends a leg

type Actor = {
  hp: { cur, max, temp }
  life: 'alive' | 'dying' | 'stable' | 'dead'
  ac, speeds: { walk, fly?, swim?, climb?, burrow?, hover? }
  initBonus, initScore?: number, passivePerception, senses: string
  creatureType?, cr?, xp?, srdId?: string, sheetUrl?: string   // sheetUrl: https only, at most 500 chars
  monsterId?: MonsterId                   // v1 wave 5, DM only, never sent to owners: the room monster it was placed from (section 14.15)
  wildShape?: true                        // v1 wave 7, a character's own actor only, with srdId: the SRD creature is its Wild Shape (docs/design-v1-pc-stats.md 3.6)
  effects: Effect[]
  deathSaves: { ok: 0..3, fail: 0..3 }
  legendary?: { actionsMax, actionsUsed, resistMax, resistUsed }
  notes: string, dmNotes: string
}

type Character = { id, ownerIds: MemberId[], actor: Actor, defaultImage?, size, name,
  level?: number,                         // 1 to 20 (v1), which the encounter builder's XP budget reads
  stats?: PcStats }                       // v1 wave 7: the character's stat block (docs/design-v1-pc-stats.md 3.2)

type PcStats = {                          // at most 12 KiB serialized (LIMITS.pcStatsBytes)
  abilities: Record<AbilityKey, number>, saves: AbilityKey[], skills: { [skill]?: 'prof' | 'expert' },
  jack?: true, alert?: true, spellAbility?: AbilityKey, critOn?: 19 | 18, evasion?: true,
  adjust: { saves?, skills?, allSaves?, allChecks?, init?, passive?, spellDc?, spellAttack? },   // -20 to 20 each
  defenses: { [damageType]?: 'resistant' | 'immune' | 'vulnerable' },
  rolls: PcRoll[] }                       // at most 40: weapons, spells and other rolls with their dice
```

A character with a block has its actor's `initBonus` and `passivePerception` written from the block after every `character.create` and `character.update` (`shared/rules/pcStats.ts pcDerived`), clamped to the actor's bounds, and an actor patch that names either on such a character is refused. Every number is its derivation from the scores, the level's proficiency bonus and the proficiencies, plus a stored adjustment, so a level-up moves every number and keeps an item's bonus.

```ts
type Effect = {
  id, key: ConditionKey | StatusKey | 'custom'
  label?: string, glyph?: string, color?: string
  level?: number                                     // exhaustion 1-6, or a counter 0-99 on any other effect (v1)
  spell?: string                                     // "Concentrating: Bless"
  hiddenFromPlayers?: boolean                        // v1
  triggerShown?: boolean                             // v1, DM only: a Ready action's trigger reaches every player
  duration?: {
    remaining: number, boundary: 'turnStart' | 'turnEnd'
    anchor: { entryId: string } | { tokenId: TokenId } | { characterId: CharacterId }   // section 9.5
  }
}

type AreaTemplate = {
  id, sceneId, shape: 'sphere' | 'cylinder' | 'cone' | 'cube' | 'line' | 'emanation'
  size: number                          // ft, 5 to 1000: radius, length, side, or emanation distance
  width?: number, height?: number       // ft, 5 to 1000: line width, cylinder height
  origin: { x, y, z }                   // cells and ft, ignored when attached
  yaw: number
  pitch?: number                        // v1, cone and line only: degrees above level, -90 to 90 (section 9.3)
  faceOffset?: { u: number, v: number } // cube only: where the origin sits on the face, each -0.5 to 0.5 of the side
  anchor?: 'bottom' | 'top'             // cylinder only: which circle the origin is the center of
  attachedTo?: TokenId
  triggers?: ('enter' | 'turnStart' | 'turnEnd')[]   // v1: when the log reminds that it hits a creature again (section 9.3)
  oncePerTurn?: true                    // v1: it hits a creature only once a turn, whichever trigger comes first
  includeOrigin: boolean
  color, label?, spell?: string
  casterId?: TokenId, ownerId: MemberId
  visibility: 'all' | 'dm'
}

type Drawing = { id, sceneId, kind: 'free' | 'line' | 'rect' | 'ellipse' | 'polygon' | 'arrow' | 'text' | 'ruler',
  points: number[], style: { stroke, fill?, width, opacity, dash? }, text?, ownerId, visibility,
  anchors?: (TokenId | null)[],         // 'ruler' only (v1): the token under each stop, or null
  z?: number[] }                        // 'ruler' only: each stop's ground in feet as the measurer saw it, 0 on a token or unseen ground
  // points: at most 2,000 numbers, freehand simplified with Douglas-Peucker before sending

type Encounter = { round: number, activeEntryId: string | null, entries: InitEntry[],
                  started?: boolean,    // player projections only, never stored (section 7.3)
                  turnStart?: TurnStart }   // v1, section 9.4
type TurnStart = { entryId, tokenId, sceneId, x, y, elevation, seen?: true,   // where the active creature's turn began
  seq?: number }                        // the seq of the op that started the turn, which names it (section 9.2)
type InitEntry = { id, tokenId?, characterId?, label?, value: number | null, tieOrder: number,
  groupKey?: string, hidden: boolean, reactionUsed: boolean, inLair: boolean,
  advantage?: 'adv' | 'dis' }            // the DM's roll reminder next to the row, which auto initiative rolls by (section 9.4)
type EncounterSnapshot = { encounter: Encounter, effects: Record<TokenOrCharacterId, Effect[]>,
  revealed: { tokenId, visibility, fogExemptAuto }[], at,   // tokens revealed on that turn, restored by prev
  legendary?: { holderId, actionsUsed } }   // legendary actions the turn's creature had used, restored by prev

type Handout = { id, assetId, title, to: 'all' | MemberId[], shownAt,
  pages?: HandoutPage[] }               // every page in order, the first included, and absent for one image. assetId always names the first page (docs/design-v1-pdf-handouts.md 3.1)
type HandoutPage = { assetId, width, height }   // a handout image and the size of its full variant, so a viewer lays out every page before any loads
type HandoutDoc = { id, rev, title, pages: HandoutPage[], createdAt }   // a PDF the DM saved, kept until deleted. Showing one copies its pages into a new handout, which never points back to it

type CustomMonster = { id, rev, name,                 // v1, section 14.6
  size, sizeAlt?: size,                   // the second size of "Medium or Small"
  type, alignment, ac, initiative,        // initiative is the bonus, the score is always 10 more
  hp: { average, formula },               // "4d10 + 4" with an ASCII minus, or "" for a fixed number
  speed: string,                          // as printed: "30 ft., Fly 60 ft. (hover)"
  abilities: Record<'str' | 'dex' | 'con' | 'int' | 'wis' | 'cha', { score, save }>,
  skills, vulnerabilities, resistances, immunities, gear, senses, languages: string,
  passivePerception, cr, xp, xpInLair?,
  traits, actions, bonusActions, reactions, legendaryActions: { name, text }[],
  defaultImage?: ImageRef }               // what its new tokens start with, saved with the block
type SrdImage = { id: SrdId, rev, image: ImageRef }   // keyed and identified by the SRD id
type SavedEncounter = { id, rev, name,   // v1, section 14.8
  monsters: { srdId | monsterId, count, inLair?: boolean }[],   // one of the two ids. At most 20 kinds, 50 creatures
  party?: { count, level }[] }            // the party its budget is for. Absent: the room's characters
type LogEntry = { id, at, kind: 'chat' | 'whisper' | 'event', from?: MemberId, to?: MemberId[],
  text?: string, data?: LogEvent }
// v1, section 9.3: an area hit a creature again. `area` is its name then, `moved` the area came onto the creature.
type AreaEvent = { type: 'area', templateId, tokenId, trigger: 'enter' | 'turnStart' | 'turnEnd', area: string,
  moved?: true, seen?: true, hidden?: true }   // one of the LogEvent kinds
// An HP line (v1) also carries `left` (the HP after it), `revert` (DM only: what it replaced, section 7.7),
// and for the DM taking one back from the log `back: true` and `of` (the line taken back, DM only).
type Vitals = { hp: { cur, max, temp }, life, deathSaves: { ok, fail } }
type HpRevert = { before: Vitals, after: Vitals, added?: Effect[], removed?: Effect[] }
// A rest (v1): { type: 'rest', kind: 'short' | 'long', amount?, left?, label?, tokenId? | characterId?, seen? }
// A teleport (v1 wave 4, DM only): { type: 'teleport', tokenId, past: 'wall' | 'ledge' }, written by a "Teleport here" (section 9.8)
// A roll answer or mark (v1 wave 5, section 9.11): { type: 'roll', test, tokenId? | characterId?, choice?, dc?,
//   dcShown?: true, resultShown?: true, total?, result?, noRoll?: true }, the target as it stood after it

type JournalPage = { id, rev, title, folder?: string,   // v1 wave 5, section 14.16. Title 80 characters, folder 40
  access: 'dm' | 'all' | { member: MemberId },
  blocks: JournalBlock[] }                // at most 100
type JournalBlock = { id,                 // 12 random characters, unique in the page
  text: string,                           // Markdown, at most 4,000 characters
  secret?: { revealed: boolean } }        // only journal.reveal changes `revealed`
type Pin = { id, rev, sceneId, x, y,      // v1 wave 5, section 14.16: cells
  label?: string, pageId?: JournalPageId, color, visibility: 'all' | 'dm' }
type RollRequest = { id, rev, test: RollTest,   // v1 wave 5, section 9.11
  dc?: number,                            // 1 to 50
  dcShown: boolean, resultShown: boolean, // what players see of it
  dcHidden?: true,                        // player projections only, derived: a DC they do not see, never its number
  note?: string, at,                      // note: single line, at most 80
  targets: RollTarget[] }                 // 1 to 50, one per creature
type RollTest = { kind: 'save' | 'check', choices: { ability, skill? }[] }   // 1 or 2 choices, a skill only in a check
type RollAnswer = { choice?: 0 | 1, total?: number, result?: 'pass' | 'fail', noRoll?: true }   // total -50 to 100
type RollTarget = { tokenId? | characterId? } & RollAnswer & { by?: MemberId, previous?: RollAnswer }   // by and previous DM only
```

Notes on the model:

- **PC data lives on the character, not the token.** Tokens on different scenes point at the same character, so HP and conditions follow the PC everywhere. Monsters carry an inline actor, so each Goblin has its own HP. A token either points at one character or owns one copy, never both. Mythic Table's design notes call out drifting copies as a bug source.
- **Token notes** (features section 5, v1). A token has two: its own `notes`, for everyone who can see the token, which the DM and the token's owners write, and its actor's `dmNotes`, for the DM alone, which no player projection carries, owners included. A PC token's DM note is its character's, since the actor lives there. The token panel shows both under "Notes", the DM's only on a DM's device, and calls the actor's own `notes` (owners and the DM) "Card notes".
- **Other images** (features section 5, v1). A token keeps up to 6 images besides the one it wears, `altImages`, for a druid's Wild Shape forms or a disguise. A swap is one `token.update` of `image` and `altImages` together: the chosen image goes on and the one it wore takes its place in the list, so the same tap swaps back, and two devices swapping at once never leave one image twice. Whoever may change the token's image, the DM and its owners, adds, removes and swaps them, in the token panel or with one tap in the token's action bar. Every image in the list passes the same asset check as the image itself (section 8.2), import remaps them like it (section 16.4), and only the DM's and the owners' projections carry the list.
- **`visibility` is per entity.** `'dm'` means hidden. A per-member list is a v1 extension of the same field.
- **Concentration** is an effect with `key: 'concentrating'` and `spell`. Bloodied is computed (`hp.cur <= floor(hp.max / 2)`), never stored, so it can never be stale.
- **Custom monsters** (features section 15, v1) are stat blocks the DM enters from books the group owns. They live in the room's state and its backups only and never ship with the app. The fields follow the 2024 stat block line by line: text where only the DM reads the line, numbers where a token takes one. The speed stays text, because "40 ft. (bear form only)" has no numeric form, and a token reads its speeds from it the way the SRD import does. Legendary action uses and Legendary Resistance come from the entries that print them ("Legendary Action Uses: 3 (4 in Lair)", a trait named "Legendary Resistance (3/Day)"), as they did for the SRD. Tokens made from a custom monster are independent copies, as for SRD monsters, so editing or deleting one never touches a token. Since v1 wave 5 they keep `monsterId`, a DM-only link the DM reads the block through (section 14.15), never their numbers.
- **Monster images** (features section 15, v1). A monster can have a default image, the `ImageRef` of a token's image and a character's `defaultImage`, which every token placed from it starts with. A custom monster keeps it in its own record, so the editor saves it with the rest of the block. An SRD monster ships with the app, so the room keeps only the DM's choice, in `srdImages` under the SRD id. Setting or changing one never touches a token already placed. The DM puts a new image on those with token updates (section 14.6), so tokens stay independent copies.
- **Saved encounters** (features section 10, v1) are fights the DM builds ahead and places on a map later (section 14.8). One names its monsters, an SRD monster by its SRD ID or one of the room's own by its ID, rather than copying them, so an edit to one of the room's monsters shows in every encounter that uses it. A line may name a room monster deleted since: the builder says so and placing leaves it out. Placing sends ordinary token creates, so a saved encounter never touches a scene itself. `party` is the DM's own party for one encounter's budget, and without it the budget follows the room's characters by their `level`.
- **Every string and array in an op has a zod bound.** Names and labels at most 80 characters, chat at most 1,000, notes at most 4,000, `ownerIds` at most 16, effects at most 32 per actor.
- **Kept rulers** (features section 4, v1) are drawings of kind `ruler`: the stops are the points, and `anchors` names the token each stop stands on, as a live ruler's points do (section 7.6). `z` names the ground under each stop as the measurer saw it, one entry per stop and 0 on a token, written only when some entry is not 0. `drawing.create` refuses `z` on any other kind, at the wrong length, or not 0 where `anchors` names a token. A later repaint or fog change leaves it as kept. A stop on a token measures from where that token stands now. Keeping one as a drawing gives it what drawings already have: the owner, `visibility` for the DM's private rulers, undo on a player's own stack, removal with the scene or the member, the room limits, and export. The ruler tool makes and removes them, never the drawing tool's eraser. `drawing.clear` removes them with the other drawings.
- **The turn's starting spot** (features section 10, v1) is recorded in the encounter by `next`, and by a removal that passes the turn on, so every device and a reload draw the same marker. `prev` restores the earlier spot with the rest of the encounter. A row without a token has none. Its `seq`, the seq of the op that started the turn, names that turn and no other: `prev` puts it back with the turn, and every new turn gets a new one, the same row's next turn included.
- **The turn's path** (features section 4, v1, the project owner's plan) lives on the active creature's token, not on the encounter, so a move writes only the token, and undoing an older DM move is never blocked by later changes to the fight. It is tagged with the turn's `seq`. The turn passing writes no token, so a path whose tag is not the turn under way's counts as empty wherever it is read (`shared/rules/path.ts`). The first stop is where the turn began, and each later stop ends a leg, kept explicit so walls can judge each leg on its own later. A leg's `uncounted` mark leaves it out of the distance moved, and each stop's `seen` mark records whether players who do not own the token could see it there then, by the rule of `TurnStart.seen` (section 7.3). Section 9.2 says when legs are added.
- **Range rings** (features section 4, v1) are distances on the token, so everyone who receives the token draws them.
- **Hidden conditions** (features section 7) are effects with `hiddenFromPlayers`. Only the DM sets or clears it. Every player's projection leaves the effect out, the creature's owner included, and so do the log lines about it (section 7.3). To an owner a hidden effect is not there: changing or removing it, or creating one with its id, is answered `gone`. A change or removal gets the very answer an effect that does not exist gets, code and words alike, before the change itself is looked at, so the answer never tells a hidden effect from a removed one.
- **Counters** (features section 7) are an effect's `level`: an Exhaustion level, or a number anyone who may edit the effect sets on any other one, shown in the corner of its icon. A counter can be 0, up to 99. An Exhaustion level is 1 to 6, since a long rest counts down from it. Every op that sets one is held to that where it arrives (`canApply`, after the role's checks): adding or changing an effect (by the effect's own key, since a change does not name it) and the actor of a new token or character. The reducer does not check it, because rooms in use stored ops from before the rule, which replay must apply as they were. A backup holding a level out of range imports with it brought within 1 to 6.
- **Hold and Ready** (features section 10) is the status `readied`, with the trigger as its `label`. The project owner decided players learn only that a creature no player owns readied, so a player who does not own the creature gets such a marker without its `label`, unless the DM turned on its `triggerShown` ("Show the trigger to players", DM only, off by default). A creature a player plays, a party member or a familiar, shows its trigger to everyone, since its player typed it. That takes an owner who is a player, not a DM: a DM may own a creature, and a player made DM keeps the ids of what they owned. Owning any token of a character counts, as it does for changing the character's effects (section 8.2). The log's effect lines carry no label. Readied from the tracker, it lasts until the start of the creature's next turn (section 9.5), so the duration rules clear it, log its end and give it back on Previous. "It fired" removes it and marks the row's `reactionUsed`, as one gesture.
- **HP lines** (features section 10). Every `actor.hp` writes an `hp` line with the HP after it (`left`) and what it replaced (`revert`): the HP, life and death saves before and after, and the effects the change put on or took off (Unconscious at 0 HP, an ended concentration), with the creature behind each initiative row a removed effect's duration counts (`anchors`). The DM takes a change back from the log with it (section 7.7). Lines from before this have neither, and an imported room's lines lose `revert`, since they name effects and rows of the old room.
- **Text rules.** Names (room, member, scene, token, public name, character, handout title) need at least one character and refuse control characters, line and paragraph separators, and bidi controls (U+061C, U+200E, U+200F, U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069). A room name reaches the admin CLI's terminal, where an escape sequence would run, and a bidi control could make one roster name pass for another. Other single-line text (labels, effect labels, spells, senses, group keys) refuses control characters and line separators. Notes, chat and drawing text keep tabs and line breaks and refuse every other control character. The import schema applies the same rules, and so do the names sent over HTTP (room, DM and player names), after trimming: a player name becomes a `sys.memberJoin` op, which replay checks with the protocol schema.
- **Free walls** (features section 3, v1 wave 3, addendum 2.2) are walls, doors and windows off the grid lines, which a Universal VTT import brings (section 14.9). Each lives in the chunk that holds its midpoint and is at most 8 cells long, so it reaches at most 4 cells past its chunk. Its ends are multiples of 1/64 cell inside the scene, its border included, which the protocol checks (`Number.isInteger(v * 64)`), so every touch and side test on stored segments stays exact (section 9.7). Its `id` is 12 random characters from the client, unique within its chunk, and never says what the wall is or where it came from. Ops name a free wall by its chunk and id.
- **Stamps** (features section 3, v1 wave 3, addendum 3.5) are their own collection, drawn on the board layer (section 12). A shipped stamp is named by a key, a slug (`/^[a-z0-9-]{1,40}$/`) and never a path, drawn from `public/stamps/<key>.webp`. A key the client does not know draws as a dashed box, so a renamed stamp never breaks a scene.
- **Lights** (v1 wave 3, addendum 3.6) are stored now, so the lights a Universal VTT file brings need no placing again when lighting arrives in wave 4. They are DM only in every wave. The radii follow the SRD's wording: "Bright Light in a 20-foot radius and Dim Light for an additional 20 feet" is `{ bright: 20, dim: 20 }`. `color` is stored and not drawn. In wave 3 nothing reads a light but the DM's builder, which draws it (section 12).
- **Map phases, folders and thumbnails** (features section 3, v1 wave 3) are scene fields, all DM only. `map` stays the active phase's image, so everything that draws, projects or exports the map reads `map` alone (section 14.11). Folders and thumbnails are for the DM's Scenes panel (section 14.10).
- **Automatic walls** (v1 wave 3, the project owner's decision on question 17 of the addendum) are a scene setting, `autoWalls`. Absent reads as `'all'`, the rule every scene from before wave 3 was built under, so no existing scene changes its walls (section 12).
- **Walls block movement** (v1 wave 4, addendum 4.1) is a scene setting, `blockMovement`, which the client turns on for every scene it creates. Absent reads as off, so no scene from before wave 4 starts stopping moves. It is a permission rule, never a reducer one (section 9.8), so stored moves replay as they were made.
- **Heights and stair marks** (v1 wave 4, addendum 4.2) are map data in the build chunks, present only in chunks that have any: a chunk's `heights` is 684 characters, its `up` 256. `shared/heightCodec.ts` reads and writes them, and a cell is a stair when its terrain is Stairs or its mark is not `.` (section 9.9).
- **Party vision** (v1 wave 4, the project owner's decision on question 1 of the addendum) is a scene setting, `vision`, with the scene's light level. A token's carried `light` and a creature's senses line feed it, and `partyVision` lets a creature the DM runs lend its sight (section 9.10). Sight itself is derived on the server and never stored: projections carry it as `sight` tiles (section 7.3). What the party has seen is stored, in `exploredTiles` (section 13.6).
- **The squeeze flag** (v1 wave 4, the project owner's decision on question 12) marks a creature stopped in an opening one size too small. Every rule that reads its footprint uses the squeezed one while it is set (section 9.8).
- **Icons as images** (v1 wave 5, docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 3.12). Every image field that takes a token image (a token's image and other images, a character's portrait, a room monster's and an SRD monster's default) takes `ImageRef`: an upload, or a shipped game-icons.net icon by key on a colored disk. An icon names no asset, so it passes no asset check (section 8.2), is never in a visible-asset set, and means the same in every room. A key the client does not know draws as initials. `shared/imageRef.ts` reads either kind, and `sameImage` compares an icon by its key and color.
- **Journal pages** (features section 16, v1 wave 5, docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 3.7) are lists of Markdown blocks, and a secret is a block with a flag, never a region parsed out of text, so projection drops whole blocks and never reads Markdown. An op's block says only `secret: true`: the reducer keeps each block's revealed state from the stored block of the same id, and a new secret block starts hidden, so only `journal.reveal` reveals or hides. Removing a member turns a page private to it into a DM-only one.
- **Map pins** (features section 12, v1 wave 5, docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 3.8) are markers on a scene, each perhaps linked to a journal page. Deleting a page makes every pin linked to it DM only and unlinks it in the same op, so a deletion never widens who sees a pin and no pin names a page that is gone. A scene's deletion takes its pins, and a duplicate copies them with derived IDs, keeping their pages.
- **Roll requests** (features section 13, v1 wave 5, docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 3.3) are room state, so a request outlives a reconnect and its answers are checked by the reducer. Closing one removes it, so every request in `rolls` is open. Section 9.11 has the rules.
- **The drawing permission** (features section 12, v1 wave 5, docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 3.9) is a room setting, `playersDraw`, with the shape of `playersMoveAny`. Absent reads as `'all'`, so no existing room stops anyone drawing. It is a permission rule (section 8.1), so stored drawings replay as they were made. Removing a member drops it from the list.

## 7. Sync protocol

### 7.1 Messages

All messages are JSON objects with a `t` field, validated by zod on receipt. Inbound frames over 64 KiB are rejected by `ws` (`maxPayload`).

Client to server:

| `t` | Fields | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `hello` | `clientId`, `resumeFrom`, `sceneId?` | First message on every socket, and accepted again only once after each `resync` the server sends on that socket. Any other `hello` closes the socket with `4003`, so repeated snapshots cannot be requested at will. `clientId` is a fresh random ID for each page load, kept in memory only. `resumeFrom` is the lowest `n` the client still has pending, or its next `n` when nothing is pending. A DM's `sceneId` picks the scene that DM is viewing. Players always get the player scene. |
| `op` | `n`, `op`, `g?` | A state change. `n` is a counter that starts at 1 for each `clientId` and increases by 1 for every op. `g` is an undo group: consecutive ops with the same `g` undo as one step (section 7.7). |
| `eph` | `sceneId`, `e` | Ephemeral: drag preview, ruler, AoE preview, ping, laser, focus, and since v1 wave 5 a DM's camera for table displays (`view`, section 7.6). Never stored. At most 4 KiB. |
| `scene` | `sceneId` | DM only: view another scene. The server answers `resync`, and the client sends `hello` with the new `sceneId`, the same `clientId` and its pending ops. At most 1 per 2 s per socket and 4 per 10 s per session, and subject to the snapshot budget (section 10.4). Over the rate, the server answers `retry` with `busy`. The client keeps at most one `scene` in flight and, when it settles, sends only the latest scene the DM picked. |
| `ping` | `k` | App-level heartbeat. |

Server to client:

| `t` | Fields | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `snapshot` | `seq`, `state`, `you`, `watermark` | Full projected state for this viewer. `watermark` is this client's committed watermark (section 7.2). Since v1 wave 5 `you.role` reads `'display'` on a table display's socket (section 10.2). |
| `ev` | `seq`, `changes`, `acks?`, `rejects?`, `restored?` | Everything one committed batch changed, already projected for this viewer: `{key, value}` upserts and `{key, deleted: true}` removals. `key` names the entity in the one scheme of `shared/keys.ts`, for example `token:<id>`, `fog:<sceneId>:<tx>,<ty>` or `meta`. The copy for the client that sent ops in the batch also lists their `n`s in `acks`, and its terminal refusals in `rejects` as `{n, code, message}`. Its undos and redos that applied are also in `restored` as `{n, what}`, naming what each took back (section 7.7). |
| `retry` | `n?`, `code` | The op (or, with no `n`, the `scene` request) was not processed and must be resent later: `busy` (queue full or rate limited) or `gap` (an earlier op from this client is missing). |
| `eph` | `from`, `e` | Someone's ephemeral event, filtered for this viewer. |
| `presence` | `online`, `displays?` | The member IDs with at least one open socket, sent on every change. Since v1 wave 5 `online` never lists a table display, and DM sockets also get `displays`, how many display sockets are open. |
| `xcard` | `at` | v1 wave 5: someone tapped the X-card (section 14.16). Sent to every socket of the room, on every scene, the sender's included. It names no one, and nothing about it is stored. |
| `wait` | `ms` | The snapshot for this `hello` is delayed by the snapshot budget, by about `ms`. Sent at once and again every 2 s while the delay lasts, and stopped when the snapshot is sent, the request is replaced, or the socket closes. |
| `pong` | `k` | Heartbeat reply. |
| `resync` | `reason` | The server asks for a fresh `hello`, for example after a scene switch for players or a role change. |
| `access` | | DM sockets only: getting into the room changed outside its state, because the player password changed or a DM device was added (a claim or a redeemed invite) or signed out. The DM views that show the player link or the DM devices ask again instead of polling (section 10.2). It reaches only sockets holding a snapshot, so the client counts every snapshot after its first (a reconnect, or a resync such as a scene switch) as one too: a signal sent while it held none never arrives. |

Terminal refusal codes, which consume the op's `n`: `invalid`, `forbidden`, `gone`, `quota`. `retry` never consumes it. `n`, `acks` and `rejects` are never sent to anyone except the client that chose them.

Close codes: `4001` grant revoked (kicked, password changed, DM removed), `4003` Origin or auth failure, `4008` sustained rate abuse, `4009` room closed or deleted, `4010` room unloaded to make space (reconnect after 30 s, with backoff), `4011` server too busy to load the room (reconnect with backoff up to 60 s), `1012` server restarting (reconnect immediately).

### 7.2 Applying an op (server)

The RoomActor runs one queue. Ops, `hello` handling and room-level commands all go through it, so a snapshot and the events that follow it can never interleave wrongly.

**Watermarks.** For each `(session, clientId)` the actor keeps two numbers:

- `staged`: the highest `n` it has processed, applied or refused, including ops in the batch that has not committed yet.
- `committed`: the highest `n` whose outcome is durable.

Only `committed` is ever stored or reported to a client. It is written to `client_watermarks` in the same transaction as the batch.

**`hello`.** The actor loads the client's watermark row, or treats it as 0 if there is none. If `resumeFrom - 1` is higher, it raises the watermark to `resumeFrom - 1`, through the next batch, because the client has told it every lower `n` is settled. It then waits for that batch to commit, builds the snapshot from committed state, resets the connection's held map, and sends the snapshot with the committed watermark. `resumeFrom` is always sent, so a client can never be stranded behind a `gap`.

**Frame budget.** Before any parsing, each socket has one budget for every inbound frame of any type: 100 frames per second and 256 KiB per second, with a burst of twice that. Frames over budget are dropped unparsed, and a socket that stays over budget for 10 s is closed with `4008`. A dropped `op` is never lost: the client resends any op with no answer after 10 s (section 7.4), and the sequence rules make the resend safe. Duplicate and `gap` answers in step 2 also count toward the op rate in step 3. So no stream of frames, valid or not, can keep the process busy parsing and replying.

**Each op**, in this order:

1. **Envelope.** `n` must be a positive integer. Otherwise close the socket with `4003`, since only a broken or hostile client sends that.
2. **Sequence**, before anything looks at the payload:
   - `n <= committed`: a duplicate of a settled op. Answer an `ev` with no changes and `acks: [n]`.
   - `committed < n <= staged`: a duplicate of an op in the uncommitted batch. Send nothing. The batch's `ev` carries its ack to this client's current socket, because acks are addressed by `(session, clientId)`, not by socket.
   - `n > staged + 1`: an earlier op is missing. Answer `retry` with `gap`.
   - `n = staged + 1`: continue. Only this op can be refused in a way that consumes an `n`, so a refusal can never skip a missing op.
3. **Rate.** Over the per-socket or per-room op rate (section 10.4), answer `retry` with `busy`. The queue cap (500 ops per room) answers the same way.
4. **Payload.** Parse with the op's zod schema, including bounds. On failure, refuse `invalid`.
5. **Resolve commands.** `undo` or `redo` becomes a `sys.restore` op (section 7.7).
6. **Authorize.** `canApply(op, actor, state)` with the actor from the socket's grant (section 10.2). It checks the target and every entity, member and asset the op refers to (section 8.2). A player acting on or referring to something they cannot see gets `gone`, the same answer as a missing entity, so a refusal leaks nothing.
7. **Limits** (section 10.5): entity counts, serialized state and scene size, and the split's counting pass on every scene whose walls the op moved (v1 wave 3, section 9.7). Since v1 wave 4 also the sight piece cap on vision scenes, the lit token lights, and the explored layer's reserve (section 10.5). Since v1 wave 5 also the journal's pages and bytes, pins, open roll requests, and every op's check against the room-level part of a DM's projected size plus the largest scene's own part (section 10.5). Over a limit, refuse `quota`.
8. **Reduce.** Stamp the envelope `{seq: seq + 1, at: now}` and run `applyOp` on the staged state. It returns the new state, the touched keys, a `global` flag, log entries, and the inverse op for undo. `global` is set when settings, members, scene membership, `fogEnabled`, a scene's automatic walls rule (v1 wave 3), a scene's `vision` (v1 wave 4), fog tiles, ownership or the blackout change. A hide or a fog reset that clears explored bits sets it too, even when it changes no DM tile (section 13.6).
9. **Stage.** Set `staged = n`. A refusal in steps 4, 6 or 7 also sets `staged = n` and records `{n, code}` in the batch, so the refusal is reported only once it is durable.

**Server-built ops** skip step 4's client schema: the roster changes made over HTTP (`sys.memberJoin`, `sys.memberSetKind`, `sys.memberRemove`), the `sys.restore` that undo and redo resolve to, and since v1 wave 4 the `sys.explore` of the explore step below. Each is checked against the server op schema before it is reduced.

**Dice, receipts and admission** (v1 wave 7, docs/design-v1-dice.md 1.2 and 4.2). An op that rolls or applies carries a client `tag` (and a batch's `part`). Before anything else looks at it, a tag settled within 24 hours is answered from its receipt, the same ack or refusal, applying and drawing nothing. A new tagged op is then admitted: the room must be writable, the day's receipt caps must have room (10,000 for the room, 2,000 for each player, the players together leaving the DMs 2,000), and the member's roll budget is charged one token a roll (section 10.3). An admission refusal (`quota`) leaves no receipt. Past admission every outcome writes the op's receipt in its batch's transaction, committed with the op's seq or refused with its words. A dice op is resolved into `sys.dice` after step 6 authorizes it, so no refusal depends on the dice.

**The explore step** (v1 wave 4, section 13.6). Before the batch commits, the actor works out the party's sight on the player scene, when it has vision and fog on and no blackout is on, and appends one `sys.explore` to the same batch with every explored tile the sight adds to (`server/rooms/explore.ts`), each with the tile's new bits, in row order. It is no one's op and on no undo stack. The size guard never refuses it: the explored layer's room to grow is reserved in advance (section 10.5). Replay applies the stored bits and never works out sight again. Replay checks every stored op with the protocol schema (section 16.2), so an op that fails it is refused when it is made, never stored where it would stop the room's next load.

The batch commits in one transaction after 20 ms, or at once at 50 ops. It writes the `room_ops` rows, the `room_log` rows for their log entries, since v1 wave 7 the `roll_receipts` rows of the tagged ops it settles, and the `client_watermarks` rows. After the commit succeeds:

- the staged state becomes the committed state, and every `committed` watermark becomes its `staged` value;
- the actor sends each viewer one `ev` for the whole batch (section 7.3), with each client's `acks` and `rejects`.

Because one `ev` carries a batch's changes and all of its acks together, a client can never see an op's effect in `confirmed` while the same op is still in `pending`.

Failure handling:

- **The commit fails cleanly** (an error before `COMMIT`). Staged changes and staged watermarks are discarded, the batch is kept, and the actor retries with backoff. Nothing was published or acknowledged.
- **The outcome is unknown** (the connection dropped during `COMMIT`). Before any retry, the actor reads the room's `max(seq)` from `room_ops` and the batch's `client_watermarks` rows. If they match the batch, it committed and is published. Otherwise it is retried.
- **During a retry**, new ops wait in the queue up to its cap, then get `retry` with `busy`. `/healthz` reports `degraded` without naming the room.

### 7.3 Projection and fan-out

`shared/project.ts` exports `projectState(state, viewer)` for snapshots and `projectEntity(kind, entity, state, viewer)` for single entities. Projections are allowlists: each entity kind has a list of fields per viewer class (DM, owner, other player), so a field added later is hidden from players until someone allows it.

**Scope.** Snapshots and events are scene-scoped for everyone. A viewer receives the list of scenes it may know about, and the contents (tokens, templates, drawings, build chunks, labels, stamps, lights, fog tiles, since v1 wave 4 the explored layer and the party's sight, and since v1 wave 5 map pins) of one scene: the player scene for players, the scene a DM chose in `hello`. Room-level data (members, characters, encounter, handouts, log, custom monsters, SRD monster images, saved encounters, and since v1 wave 5 journal pages and roll requests) is filtered per viewer as below. A change on another scene sends nothing except, for DMs, scene list metadata.

**Rules for a player viewer.** A DM sees everything in its chosen scene, including `fogDrafts` and `encounterHistory`, unchanged.

- **Blackout:** while `view.blackout` is on, a player's projection covers no scene at all. The player scene and everything on it (build chunks, labels, fog tiles, tokens, areas, drawings) are withheld, and so are the encounter, which can name what is coming in a row of its own, the handouts, and the log lines about any of them. Players keep the meta, which carries the blackout for the pause screen, the members, the characters, chat and whispers, and events about characters. The ephemeral channel forwards them nothing, and the map image is not theirs to read meanwhile. So a reload or devtools shows nothing the DM sets up behind the pause screen. Turning the blackout on or off sets `global` (section 7.2), so lifting it sends players the whole table as it is then, reveals made during the blackout included. Nothing that happens behind it is marked as seen by players (the `seen` marks below, on log lines, a turn's start and a path's stops), so what the DM did meanwhile stays out of their log and trails after it lifts. DMs are unaffected.
- **Scenes:** only the player scene appears in the list. Since v1 wave 3 a player's copy of it is an allowlist: `id`, `name`, `bounds`, `map`, `grid`, `background`, `fogEnabled`, `defaultView` and `autoWalls`, and since v1 wave 4 `blockMovement` and `vision`, which players need to draw and to drag, with `rev`, `order` and `wallsVersion` sent as 0. The walls version moves with every build op, the edit of a hidden secret door or a lock included, and no client reads it, since the rules caches follow the derived wall set (section 9.7). `folder`, `thumb`, `phases`, `phaseId` and `phaseGrid` are DM only, so neither the other phases' images nor their names ("On fire") reach a player, and a thumbnail that lands in the background sends players nothing (addendum 3.3, 3.4).
- **Tokens:**
  - Removed when `visibility: 'dm'` and the viewer does not own it.
  - Removed when it is withheld under the fog by the one rule in section 13.4 (fog enabled, fully fogged, no player owner, `fogExempt` not set).
  - On a scene with vision on (v1 wave 4), removed unless it is a party member, `fogExempt` is set, or the party's sight covers its footprint (section 13.6). With fog on, sight lies inside the explored ground, so this implies the fog rule.
  - A hidden name becomes `publicName` or "Unknown creature", and its `number` is not sent, so it never shows that the creature is one of a numbered group, or which one.
  - Every player's token projection, owner or not, carries the derived `aboveFog` (true when a player owns the token, or `fogExempt` is set), computed from that same section 13.4 rule. Since v1 wave 4 a token the DM marked `partyVision` is a party member and counts as player owned here, on every scene.
  - Owners receive everything except `actor.dmNotes` and effects marked `hiddenFromPlayers`. The owner is a player, so a condition the DM hides stays hidden from the creature's owner too. A carried `light` (v1 wave 4) reaches only the DM and the owners, and `partyVision` only the DM. Other players see only the light's effect.
  - Every player's copy of a token carries rev 0, owners' included, as do their characters and their encounter (the live play review after wave 3). The rev counts every write, so a hidden condition, a DM note, a token's light or party mark (v1 wave 4) would otherwise send players an `ev` that differs only in its rev.
  - A Ready action's trigger (the `readied` effect's `label`) reaches other players only when a player, not a DM, owns the creature or a token of its character, or the DM set `triggerShown` (section 6).
  - Other players receive `id`, `sceneId`, `kind`, display name, `image`, `size`, `w`, `h`, `x`, `y`, `elevation`, `height`, `rotation`, `disposition`, `locked`, `layer`, `number` (unless the name is hidden), `notes` (the token's note for everyone), `rangeRings`, a derived `aboveFog` (true when a player owns it, or `fogExempt` is set, section 13.3), and the actor fields `effects` (minus those marked `hiddenFromPlayers`), `life` and HP per below. `ownerIds`, the exemption flags themselves and `altImages` are not sent: a disguised creature's true face would be in that list.
- **HP for players who do not own the actor:**
  - `hidden`: nothing.
  - `label`: Healthy, Bloodied, Dying or Dead from `rules/hp.ts`.
  - `bar`: `cur / max` rounded to the nearest 1/20. This hides exact numbers, but a bar still shows proportions by design, so careful watching can estimate max HP. `label` and `hidden` are the secret settings.
  - `exact`: the numbers.
  - Other players' PCs follow `pcHpVisibleToParty`.
- **Characters:** owners receive everything except `dmNotes` and hidden effects. Other players receive the name, image, size, conditions, `life` and HP per `pcHpVisibleToParty`, and not the `level`.
- **Custom monsters:** DM only. Players never receive one, in a snapshot or an `ev`, and see only the tokens made from them, under the token rules above. A DM receives all of them whatever scene it views.
- **SRD monster images:** DM only, like custom monsters and their default images. Which image the DM chose for a monster is prep: a player sees it only on a token it may see, so a default set before a fight never hints at what is coming, and the image is not in a player's visible-asset set (section 11.2) until such a token wears it.
- **Saved encounters:** DM only. A fight built ahead says what is coming, so players never receive one, in a snapshot or an `ev`, and see only the tokens it places, under the token rules above. A DM receives all of them whatever scene it views.
- **Templates:** removed when `visibility: 'dm'`, when attached to a token the viewer cannot see, or when another player's and `playersSeeAoeOfOthers` is off. A `casterId` or `attachedTo` pointing at something the viewer cannot see is sent as `null`.
- **Drawings:** removed when `visibility: 'dm'`. A kept ruler is also removed while any token it stands on is not in the viewer's projection, the live ruler's rule (section 7.6). A token that is gone counts as unseen, because the stop's own point is where that token stood when the ruler was kept. That spot may have been under fog or hidden then, so a player gets each stop on a token as the middle of the token where it stands now, the point the ruler tool gives a stop on a token. Free stops stay as kept, and the DM gets every stop as kept.
- **Build chunks:** players get the build as it would be if every undiscovered secret door were the wall they see there. On an edge where the scene's automatic walls rule puts a wall (section 12), the door is left out, since that wall is automatic and automatic walls are never sent. Anywhere else it is sent as a plain wall, like one the DM drew, so players never see a gap where a hidden door is. A locked door is sent as closed. A player's chunks carry rev 0, so an edit players cannot see sends them nothing, and a chunk left with nothing to show is withheld. Whether an edge has an automatic wall can depend on the cells across a chunk border, so a touched chunk's neighbors to the east and south are re-projected with it, and a change of the scene's rule sets `global` and re-projects them all.
  - Free walls (v1 wave 3) follow the same rules. A free wall is never automatic, so a hidden secret free door always arrives as a plain wall. A player's copy lists a chunk's free walls sorted by their coordinates, so their order never shows which one was imported as a door.
  - Heights and stair marks (v1 wave 4) are map data and reach players with the chunk, so a chunk is withheld as empty only when it has no cells, edges, free walls, heights or stair marks. A player's derived movement set is therefore exactly the server's (section 9.8).
- **Labels:** removed when the scene has fog enabled and every cell of the label's text box is fully fogged (section 13.4), so a label such as "Trap" in the dark is not in the data at all.
- **Stamps** (v1 wave 3): removed when `visibility: 'dm'`, or when the scene has fog enabled and every cell under the stamp's rotated bounding box is fully fogged, the labels' rule. So a chest in an unexplored room is not in a player's data. A stamp's library image is in a player's visible-asset set only while the stamp is in their projection (section 11.2).
- **Lights** (v1 wave 3): DM only. Players never receive the collection, and since v1 wave 4 they see only a light's effect, in the party's sight.
- **Fog:** committed fog tiles are sent. Drafts are DM only. Since v1 wave 4 a player gets one fog: each tile is the DM's tile and the explored tile at that key merged, revealed where either is, with rev 0, so a change players cannot see sends nothing. A DM gets the two layers apart, the explored layer under `explored:` keys (section 13.6).
- **Sight** (v1 wave 4): the party's sight on the viewer's scene when it has vision on, as `sight:<sceneId>:<tx>,<ty>` entities valued `{b}` or `{f}` (section 6), only tiles with a level above 0, in a `sight` map keyed `"<sceneId>:<tx>,<ty>"`. Every player of the room gets the same tiles, and DMs get them too. None during a blackout. They are derived by `projectState` and never stored.
- **Encounter:**
  - An entry is removed when it is `hidden`, or when its token or character is not in the viewer's projection.
  - `groupKey` is removed.
  - `tieOrder` is renumbered 0 to n-1 over the entries the viewer receives, in turn order. The stored numbers count hidden entries too, so a gap would tell a player that a hidden creature exists and roughly when it acts.
  - `label` follows the token projection.
  - If the active entry was removed for this viewer, `activeEntryId` is sent as `null`, and the tracker shows "Another creature's turn".
  - A player's encounter also carries `started`: whether any turn has started. Without it a player could not tell "not started" from a hidden creature's turn, since both send `activeEntryId` as `null`. It is derived, never stored, and gives nothing away: every player sees the fight's first turn start.
  - `turnStart` is sent only with the active entry the viewer receives, when its token is in the viewer's projection on the spot's scene, and either the viewer owns the token or it carries `seen`. The reducer sets `seen` when the turn starts if players who do not own the token could see it there then (not `'dm'`, not withheld under the fog or out of the party's sight on a vision scene, no blackout). So the spot where a hidden or fogged creature began never reaches players, even after it walks into the light. The mark itself is not sent.
  - `encounterHistory` is DM only.
- **A token's path** (section 9.2) reaches a player only while it is the path of the turn under way and the active row is one their encounter holds, so a path never says whose turn it is or was. Owners get every stop. Other players get only the stops marked `seen`, and a leg's `uncounted` mark only when they saw both its ends: their line between two seen stops may pass over unseen ones, and the mark would tell them how the creature moved there. Nobody but the DM gets the `seen` marks. A player's client works out the distance moved from the stops it holds and where the creature stands now, which they see, so the total leaks nothing about the legs they could not see. It can differ from the DM's, higher or lower: a line joining two seen stops is shorter than the legs it hides, and it counts in full even when part of what it hides did not count, such as a shove out of their sight. A full path's leg joined through a stop they did not see keeps its mark from them the same way (section 9.2). A server-side total was the alternative, and it would have told them how far the creature went out of sight.
- **Handouts:** only those with `to: 'all'` or a `to` list that contains the viewer, each whole, every page with its size.
- **Handout documents** (`handoutDocs`, docs/design-v1-pdf-handouts.md 3.4): DM only, as saved encounters are. No handout names the document it came from, so no id of one reaches players. A document's pages reach a player only by being shown.
- **Log:**
  - Whispers go only to the sender and the recipients. A DM sees a whisper only if the DM is a recipient.
  - Event entries are structured data that name entities by ID, never pre-rendered text, for example `{type: 'hp', tokenId, delta}`. The client renders them with its own projected names.
  - An entry that references an entity the viewer cannot see now is dropped.
  - Projection only knows the state as it is now, so an event about a creature carries two marks set when it is written:
    - `seen`, on events about a token: players who do not own the token could see it then (the token rule above, the same for every non-owner). Non-owners get only marked events, so what happened to a creature while it was hidden, under fog or on another scene stays out of their log after a reveal, and a reload spoils nothing. Owners get their own creature's whole history. Events about a character need no mark, since every player knows every character. A `turn` event carries the same mark for its initiative row: players who do not own the row's creature could see the row when the turn started (not `hidden`, and its token in their sight). So the turns a hidden row took, for example with `revealHiddenOnTurn` off, stay out of their log after the row is revealed. Entries written before the mark existed count as unseen. A creature revealed on its turn is marked by the same rule as any other, so a turn it starts in the fog and what happens to it there stay out of the log (section 9.4). Ops stored before op version 4 marked it seen wherever it stood, and replay keeps their marks (section 16.2).
