    - `hidden`, on effect and concentration events: adding, removing, expiring or pausing an effect marked `hiddenFromPlayers`, a concentration it ends, and the damage prompt when every concentration on the creature is hidden. Only the DM gets these, owners included, as with the effect itself.
  - The marks are never rewritten later. The log records what players could see when it happened: an effect hidden after it was added leaves its add line in the players' log, and one added hidden and shown later never gains an add line there. Hiding an effect players already saw writes no line, while removing one writes its remove line ("no longer Poisoned"), so players who saw it, its owner included, can tell that the DM hid it rather than that it ended. The effect leaves their view either way, and a line for the hiding would only say the same thing louder.
  - HP numbers inside an entry (`delta`, set values, temp HP, concentration DCs) and death save counts are kept only when the viewer may see that creature's exact HP. Otherwise the entry says only what the HP label allows ("Goblin 2 is Bloodied").
  - HP and rest lines are projected by an allowlist (v1): the change, the creature, `seen`, `crit`, `back`, the label unless the creature's HP is hidden, and `amount` and `left` only with exact HP. What a change replaced (`revert`) and the line a take-back names (`of`) reach only the DM, the owner included, since `revert` can hold a hidden effect.
  - A teleport line (v1 wave 4, section 9.8) goes to DMs only. No player gets it, the token's owners included.
  - A player's delete of a character they play alone writes `{ type: 'characterDeleted', characterId, memberId, name, tokens, companions, rows }`: the character's name then, and how many tokens, companions and fight rows went with it on every scene. DMs only (the project owner, 2026-10-16), since the DM cannot undo it and its counts name what players may not see. The DM's own delete writes none. The companion line a player's new companion writes is DM only the same way.
  - Member events (joined, signed out by the DM, became a DM, no longer a DM, removed) go to everyone, since every player sees the roster. They do not depend on who is on the roster now, so the lines about a member the DM removed stay, read without its name.
  - An area's reminder (v1, section 9.3) carries the token's `seen` mark and a `hidden` mark set when the area itself was hidden from players then: DM only, attached to a token players could not see, as the template rule above has it. A player gets it only when neither mark stops them and the area is in their projection now. So a player never learns that a hidden creature walked into an aura, or that a hidden trap went off.
  - A roll line (v1 wave 5, section 9.11) reaches the DM and the players who own its creature when they read it, outside a blackout, with the DC only under the line's `dcShown` and the result only under its `resultShown`. A player's own fail without rolling (`noRoll`) reaches them either way, as a total does.
- **Journal pages** (v1 wave 5, docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 3.7): a DM gets every page. A player, outside a blackout, gets a page shared with all or private to them, with every secret block not revealed dropped, the `secret` flag of revealed ones dropped too, and `rev` sent as 0, so an edit inside a block they cannot see sends them nothing and what remains shows no gap.
- **Map pins** (v1 wave 5, docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 3.8): the labels' rule plus two. A player gets a pin on the player scene that is not DM only, not on a fully fogged cell (the merged fog on a vision scene), and whose linked page, if any, they may read. So a pin linked to a DM-only page is DM only, and players never learn that a note exists there.
- **Roll requests** (v1 wave 5, section 9.11): a player gets a request only when one of its targets is a creature they own, with only those targets, `dc` only when `dcShown` and otherwise `dcHidden: true` when there is one, never its number, each `result` only when `resultShown`, `by` and `previous` dropped, `rev` sent as 0, and none during a blackout. So another player's answer sends them nothing.
- **A token's `party` flag** (v1 wave 5): every player-class copy of a token carries a derived `party`, true when a member of kind player owns the token or its character. Players already see whose tokens these are. It lets a table display, which owns nothing, fit the party (section 14.14).
- **`Actor.monsterId`** (v1 wave 5) is DM only in every projection, owners included, like `dmNotes`.
- **A table display** (v1 wave 5, section 10.2) is projected as a player who owns nothing: its viewer is `{ memberId: <display id>, role: 'player' }`, and the display id is in no `ownerIds`, no whisper's `to`, no handout's list and no page's access, so the existing rules give it exactly what such a player gets. It gets no roll requests and no roll lines.

**Fan-out.** After a batch commits, for each connected viewer, the actor finds which projected entities may have changed in the whole batch:

- every op's touched keys;
- every entity that refers to a touched key: templates attached to a touched token, kept rulers standing on a touched token, tokens that reference a touched character, encounter entries for a touched token or character (the turn's starting spot is the active entry's token), log entries, an area's reminders included for a touched template or one attached to a touched token, and every token with a path when the encounter is touched (a player's copy of a path depends on the turn under way and on its row);
- everything in the viewer's scope when any op in the batch set `global`;
- since v1 wave 4, for every write to an explored tile, the fog tile at its key, since a player's fog tile is the merge;
- since v1 wave 4, for every sight tile that changed in the batch (`changedSightKeys`), that tile and every token, label and stamp overlapping it or a tile `sys.explore` wrote (`tileDependents`), judged by a token's fog cells and its unsnapped sight rectangle. Since v1 wave 5 map pins join that list;
- since v1 wave 5, for a touched journal page, the pins linked to it, since a pin's projection depends on who may read its page.

The fan-out works out the party's sight before and after the batch for each vision scene a socket holds (the player scene unless a blackout is on, and each DM's scene) and diffs the two. So a move, a light, a door, a death, a condition or an edit of a creature's senses reaches players through the changed tiles alone and never sets `global`. Party vision makes the tiles the same for every player, so the values all players get alike (chunks, fog, sight, the scene, labels, stamps) are projected once per batch, each changed tile object hashed once, and only the hashes compared per connection.

It projects those entities from the batch's final committed state and compares each one with what that connection already holds. Each connection keeps a map from entity key to a 128-bit hash of the serialized projected value it last received, so the map costs a few dozen bytes per entity and an accidental collision is not a practical concern. A snapshot keeps the map's build chunks, fog tiles and sight tiles as the values themselves until a batch first compares them (v1 wave 4): each is the room state's own object or one projection keeps per object, so it costs no memory the room does not hold already, a snapshot hashes none of the map, and a value a batch projects as the very same object is never hashed at all. Only differences go into the batch's `ev`: an upsert when the value is new or changed, a removal when the connection held it and the projection is now `null`. A change that projects to the same value for a viewer sends that viewer nothing, so a DM's fog draft or a hidden token's move costs players nothing. A viewer with no differences, no acks and no rejects gets no `ev`.

**Dice lines** (v1 wave 7, docs/design-v1-dice.md 5.2). A DM gets every `dice` line, and a table display none. A player keeps each roll that names no creature, names a character, or names a token they may have the events of, and with none kept gets no line. Open to everyone, or the player's own, the line comes in full, its `tag` only to its roller. Someone else's secret roll comes as the bare line `{ by, to: 'dm', rolls: [] }`, only when the same roll made public would have reached the player. A rolled request answer keeps the `roll` line's rule, with its dice. The fan-out re-projects every dice line that names a touched token or character.

**Saves that end an effect** (v1 wave 7, docs/design-v1-roll-options.md 6.1). An effect's `save` reaches players without its `dc`, owners included, so they read which save ends it and never the number. Its `due`, the pending occurrence, is the DM's in every projection. The `saveEnds` line a turn end writes is DM only, as its DC is. A request's `ends` (the effect and occurrence a pass settles) is the DM's, and its public `cause` goes with the request to the players it asks. The DM's `actor.saveDue` and `actor.outcome` are refused for players.

The held map is rebuilt from scratch on every snapshot, including after `resync`.

The correctness invariant, tested with random states and op sequences: for every viewer, applying the events it received to its snapshot gives exactly `projectState(finalState, viewer)`.

### 7.4 Optimistic client

The client keeps three things in its store:

- `confirmed`: the last projected state from the server (snapshot plus `ev`s).
- `pending`: ops sent or queued but not yet acknowledged, in order, each with its `n` and the time it was made.
- `view`: `confirmed` with every optimistic pending op replayed through `applyOp`. Components read only `view`. It is recomputed when either input changes, and only the touched entities get new object identities, so React re-renders only what changed.

**Acknowledgment.** When an `ev` arrives, the client applies its changes to `confirmed` and removes every op listed in `acks` and `rejects` from `pending`, in one store update. A relative op such as "deal 7 damage" is therefore never counted twice: before its batch's `ev` it lives only in `pending`, and after it lives only in `confirmed`. A refusal recomputes the view (a token snaps back), and a toast explains it when it was not a race. A `quota` refusal says itself what is full, so the toast shows it after "Not saved.": a room-wide limit says the room is full ("The room is full: it can hold at most 50 scenes."), and a scene at one of its caps speaks of the scene ("This scene can hold at most 1,000 pillars."), with counts written with thousands separators.

**`retry`.** On `busy` or `gap` for an op, the client stops sending, waits with the reconnect backoff, then resends every pending op in order from the oldest. A `retry` with no `n` concerns only a `scene` request: the op queue is left alone, the scene deadline (section 7.5) stops, and after 2 s (the scene rate's window) the client sends the latest scene the DM picked, starting the deadline again. A pending op with no answer at all for 10 s (for example, a frame the server dropped over budget) is resent the same way. The duplicate rules in 7.2 make a resend harmless, even during a database retry.

**Which ops are optimistic.** A projection has parts removed, so not every op can be predicted from it. Before replaying a pending op, the client asks the shared reducer which entities the op reads (`reads(op, state)`). The op is replayed optimistically only if every one of them is present in the client's view with every field the op needs. Otherwise it waits for the server's `ev`, with a small spinner on what it affects. A view cannot see what was removed from it, so ops that scan a whole collection (`encounter.next`, `encounter.prev`, the ordering in `encounter.add`, and auto-numbering in `token.create`) declare a "collection complete" read. Only a DM viewing the scene of every entity in that collection satisfies it. Players always wait for those ops. This rule applies to every role. Examples:

- A player moving their token, editing their own character, drawing, or placing their own template: optimistic.
- A player's End Turn (always, because it scans the encounter), damage to a creature whose HP the player sees only as a label or bar or not at all, and fog publish: wait.
- A DM's `encounter.next` when an entry's token is on another scene than the one the DM is viewing: wait.
- `undo` and `redo`: always wait, because the server decides their result.
- A player's `roll.answer` (v1 wave 5): always waits, since their view of a request lacks a hidden DC and the other targets. The card reads "Sending…" until the answer.
- Every dice op (v1 wave 7) and `sys.dice`: always wait, since the server rolls. The control that rolled shows its answer in place (docs/design-v1-dice.md 8.7).
- A pending op whose target is gone from `confirmed` is skipped in `view`, and the server's answer settles it.

**Reconnect.** The client drops pending ops older than 12 hours, with a notice ("3 offline edits were too old to save"). Those are always the oldest. It sends `hello` with `resumeFrom` set to its lowest remaining pending `n`, or its next `n` if none remain. When the snapshot arrives, it drops every pending op with `n <= watermark`, because the snapshot already includes it, then resends the rest in order.

Watermark rows are kept for 7 days after their last use, much longer than the 12 hours a client keeps an op, so a resend is always recognized. A `clientId` lives only for one page load, because the pending queue lives in memory and a reload drops it anyway, so a fresh page never reuses an `n`.

Conflicts: last writer wins per field, in server order. Relative ops (damage, healing) compose. A refused op is never retried automatically.

### 7.5 Connection lifecycle (client)

- States: `connecting`, `open`, `reconnecting`, `offline`.
- Heartbeat: the client sends `ping` every 25 s. No `pong` within 10 s means the socket is dead (iOS can leave half-open sockets that still report open after a suspend). The client closes it and reconnects. The server also sends a protocol-level `ws.ping()` every 30 s and terminates a socket that misses a pong. Both intervals sit well under Cloudflare's 125 s proxy read timeout.
- Reconnect: exponential backoff from 250 ms, doubling to a 5 s cap, with ±30% jitter. `visibilitychange` to visible, `pageshow` and `online` all trigger an immediate attempt and reset the backoff. Close code `1012` reconnects at once.
- While disconnected, edits keep queueing in `pending` and show at once.
- UI: a small dot in the top bar (green, amber, red). A banner appears only after 3 s without a connection. A socket that is open but has not delivered its snapshot within 3 s counts as not connected for the dot and the banner, so a database retry on the server never looks like an empty room.
- Snapshot deadline: the client starts a 5 s timer when it sends a `hello` (first, or after a `resync`) and when it sends a `scene`. For a `scene`, the timer covers the whole exchange: `scene`, the server's `resync`, the new `hello`, and the snapshot. Each `wait` moves the deadline to `ms` plus 5 s from its arrival, and a `retry` for the `scene` stops the timer until the scene is sent again. If the deadline passes with no snapshot, the client closes the socket and reconnects through the normal backoff, with the scene it wants, the same `clientId` and its pending ops. Control messages the frame budget dropped therefore never leave a client stuck while heartbeats keep working. During a database retry the reconnect simply waits again, and the banner stays up.

### 7.6 Ephemeral channel

Drag previews, rulers, AoE previews, pings, the laser pointer and DM "look here" events go over `eph`.

- Every `eph` names its `sceneId`. The server forwards it only to viewers on that scene.
- When an `eph` concerns a token (a drag, or a ruler anchored on a token), the server checks that token with `projectEntity` for each receiver. A drag of a hidden token, or of a token under fog, goes only to DMs and its owners. The same applies to a ruler anchored on one.
- On a scene with fog enabled, the server also checks each frame's positions against the committed fog before sending it to players:
  - For a drag, every frame (`dragStart`, `dragMove`, `dragEnd`) and the measuring line that follows it. A frame whose preview footprint is fully fogged is not sent to players unless the token is `aboveFog`.
  - For a shared ruler, every point that is anchored on a token (a ruler snaps to a token when started or ended on one). If any anchored token is not visible to a player (hidden, or withheld under the fog), the frame is not sent to that player. Free points are sent as drawn, because they show only where the measurer pointed. A free point carries `z`, the ground the measurer saw under it in feet, left out at 0, and a point on a token carries none (the protocol refuses both together). A player's own ground under their fog counts as 0, so a ruler cannot probe the dark (docs/design-v1-heights-readout.md section 3). The server forwards `z` untouched.
  - Instead of a dropped frame, a player who had the preview gets a bare `dragCancel` with no coordinates.
  - So dragging a monster into the fog, or measuring to a creature hidden in it, never shows players where it is. Clients draw other creatures' drag previews below the fog.
- **Sight** (v1 wave 4). On a scene with vision on, a drag frame whose preview footprint the party's sight does not cover is not sent to players, unless the token is `aboveFog` or a party member, and a player who had the preview gets the bare `dragCancel`. The check reads the cached sight with the one footprint predicate (section 13.6). The server judges a preview's footprint by its own rule, whatever the frame says: where the creature would squeeze (section 9.8, checks 1, 3 and 4, with no cell alignment and the narrow space judged around the core), by the squeezed footprint centered on the preview point, else the full one. That rule applies on fog-only scenes too, so a Large creature dragged along a corridor out of sight is withheld at every fractional point, though its full-size preview would reach into lit ground. Frames may carry `squeeze`, for drawing only.
- **Drag paths** (v1 wave 4). Players get `dragMove` frames without their `path` whenever the scene has fog or vision on, so a monster dragged through darkness back into sight never shows its route.
- Players' rulers are shared by default. The DM's rulers are private until the DM shares one with a tap (the project owner's choice). A shared ruler still follows the anchored-token rule above. AoE previews are private by default for everyone, and sharing one is an explicit tap.
- A double tap with the ruler tool, or its Keep button, keeps the ruler on the map (v1). It leaves the ephemeral channel and becomes a drawing of kind `ruler` in the room state (section 6), shared exactly when the live ruler was: always for a player, and for the DM only once shared. The first tap of the pair would have added a waypoint, so a double tap keeps the ruler as it was before it.
- Shared AoE previews carry geometry only: shape, origin, yaw, size.
- A DM "look here" moves the camera only of players on that scene.
- **Table displays** (v1 wave 5, section 10.2). A display's socket keeps the role `'player'`, so it gets exactly the fog checks, the path stripping and the `focus` delivery a player gets, and a DM's drag of a hidden monster never reaches it. It sends nothing: an `op`, a valid `eph` or a `scene` from it closes it with `4003`. A DM device with "Show my view on the table display" on sends its camera as `{k: 'view', x, y, w, h}`, the center and size of its visible map area in cells, at most every 120 ms while it moves, again when the display count changes, and only while it views the player scene and a display is connected. `view` reaches only display sockets on that scene, and only from a DM.
- The client coalesces `eph` to one per animation frame and caps it at 30 per second. The server limits it per socket (token bucket: 40 per second, burst 80), and caps it at 4 KiB.
- A drag commits as one `token.move` op on drop. Remote drags animate with interpolation, then stop animating. Each remote drag preview carries a tag with the dragger's name in their color (features 5), drawn with the preview below the fog, so a tag never shows where a creature in the dark is going.
- **Preview lifecycle.** A drag sends `dragStart`, `dragMove` frames, and `dragEnd` or `dragCancel`. A receiving client clears another member's preview of a token when any of these happens: `dragEnd` or `dragCancel` arrives, an `ev` settles that token, the token is removed, the scene changes, a snapshot replaces the state, the sender goes offline (from `presence`), or 3 s pass without a frame. When the server stops forwarding a drag to a player because the preview went under fog (above), it sends that player a bare `dragCancel` with no coordinates. The same rules apply to rulers and AoE previews.

Snapshot size is bounded by the room limits (section 10.5): about 2.5 MB uncompressed at the worst, enforced by the 3 MiB check, and 100 to 300 KB for a typical scene. `permessage-deflate` is enabled for messages over 1 KB, and stage 3 verifies that it survives the Cloudflare tunnel (section 18). The budget does not count on it.

### 7.7 Undo

- The reducer returns an inverse for every op. Inverses are absolute restores of the previous field values. They never compute "heal 7" to undo "damage 7", because temporary HP makes that inexact.
- **Guards compare values, not revs.** Each entry expects the entity to still hold what the op left there: absent for a removal, otherwise the hash of its value with its rev left out, and for a scene its walls version and its thumbnail (v1 wave 3) left out too. A restore writes a new rev like any write, so a rev guard refused the next undo of the same entity although nobody else had touched it: two moves of one token could not both be undone, nor a move after undoing the delete that followed it. A value guard chains, because each restore puts back exactly the value the older entry expects. It still refuses whenever someone else changed the entity since. The one thing it lets through is a change made and then reverted by someone else, where the entity holds what this member left and the undo is safe. Remembering the rev each restore produced would also chain, but it means rewriting the guards of entries still on the stack, and it would still put a scene's walls version in every build undo's guard. The meta and the encounter history have no rev, so their hash is the plain value hash, the same one older stored restores were guarded with. A stored restore with a rev guard replays with that rev guard, so rooms from before value guards load unchanged.
- **Walls versions only go up.** A build op moves its scene's walls version forward as bookkeeping (`Tx.bumpWalls`), not as an edit, so the scene stays out of the op's inverse and its guard. Two DM devices painting different chunks of one scene never block each other's undo. Painting within the same 16x16 chunk changes the same entity, so that undo is refused, even when the cells differ. A restore that writes chunks moves their scene's walls version forward again, and so does a restore that changes the scene's automatic walls rule (v1 wave 3). A restored scene keeps the higher of its old and current version. Before wave 3 the rules caches were keyed on the version, and a version that went back would have served walls that are gone. Since wave 3 they follow the identity of the scene's chunk objects instead (section 9.7), and the version marks which scenes an op's walls moved, for the split's counting pass (section 9.7).
- **A thumbnail is bookkeeping too** (v1 wave 3, addendum 3.3). A DM device uploads a scene's thumbnail in the background whenever it likes, so `scene.update` with only `thumb` goes on no undo stack, guards leave `thumb` out, and a restore of a scene keeps the thumbnail it has now. A thumbnail that lands between a rename and its undo never makes the undo refuse, and an undo never puts back a stale picture.
- **Undoing a duplicate** (v1 wave 3) removes the copy and everything copied with it. The copy goes right after the original, so the op moved every later scene down one in the order, as a reorder moves scenes, and its inverse puts back each of those scenes under its value guard. So the undo refuses once any later scene in the list was edited since, such as a rename, which the addendum's plan did not name. A thumbnail landing on one does not count, since guards leave it out. The other way round holds too, as after a reorder: an older undo step on a later scene, such as a rename made before the duplicate, refuses once the duplicate has moved that scene, since the scene no longer holds the order the step left. Undone newest first, as the stack does, the duplicate goes back first and the older step then applies. The copy still goes beside the original, where the DM looks for it, rather than at the end of the list, where it would move nothing.
- **An undo or redo past the split's caps** (v1 wave 3) is refused like the op would be, with "could not undo <what>: " and the split's refusal (section 9.7).
- **Wave 4** (addendum 4.1 to 4.3.9):
  - A DM's `door.set` is on the DM's stack as a chunk restore. A player's is on no stack, since a second tap reverses it.
  - The squeeze flag is part of a move's pose (`Place`, `Pose`, `samePose` and `putBack` in server/rooms/undo.ts, and `POSE_FIELDS` in shared/permissions.ts), so undoing a move into a corridor takes the squeeze off, and undoing a move out puts it back, while damage dealt since stays.
  - A teleport is a move, so it goes on the player's stack and "Undo move" takes it back. Its step records that it was a teleport, so the redo the server builds skips the wall and ledge test as the teleport did, in a fight and outside one. Undo and redo write no new log line.
  - Every other redo of a player's move is tested like a new move. An undo of one is never stopped by a wall: it retraces a move that was allowed when made.
  - A height paint lifts the tokens standing on the ground there in the same op, so its undo puts them back, and is refused, like any restore, when one of them was moved since.
  - Vision settings, lights and a token's light undo as ordinary edits, and a settings undo sets `global` like the edit. Their steps say what they did: "turning on party vision on the scene Crypt", "making the scene Crypt dark", "giving Thorn a light", "putting out the light of Thorn", "letting Bree share its sight with the party", and for a teleport "teleporting Thorn".
  - The explored layer has no undo and no guards (section 13.6). A DM's fog step puts back the DM's layer only, so exploration never changes what its guard expects, and whatever the party explored since stays explored. `sys.explore` is no one's step, and undoing a party move does not take back what it explored.
- **Wave 5** (docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 2.6):
  - `token.updateMany`, `token.deleteMany` and `actor.effects` apply whole or not at all, and each is one step named by its tally ("changing 6 tokens"). The group HP entry goes out as one undo group, so one entry for six creatures is one step.
  - Every journal op restores the whole page under its value guard. So a reveal undoes as hiding the block again while the page stands as the reveal left it ("revealing a secret on the page Old map"), and is refused once the page was edited since.
  - `roll.ask` undoes as removing the request while nobody has answered, and is refused after. `roll.close` undoes by bringing the requests back, and a DM's mark undoes like any DM op. A player's `roll.answer` goes on no stack: the player answers again.
  - **A restore never brings back an image that was deleted.** The actor loads asset facts for the asset IDs a `sys.restore` would add, as for a client op, and permissions refuse a restore that adds a reference to an asset that is not this room's, of the kind that place takes: "could not undo deleting Goblin 2: its image was deleted since". Thumbnails are bookkeeping, so a restore keeps the current one and never needs an old one back. Replay does not run permissions, so stored restores replay as they were.
  - A player's undo or redo that would put a drawing back (any kind but a kept ruler) needs the drawing permission, as making one does (section 8.1). Taking a drawing away stays allowed.
  - A restore that would remove a journal page a pin still names is refused as other work depending on it, and one that would put back a pin whose page is gone is refused as what it belongs to being deleted.
- The server keeps a per-member undo stack in memory, the last 50 steps. The stacks are lost on restart, and that is acceptable.
- **One gesture is one step.** An `op` message may carry an undo group `g` (section 7.1). The client sends one fresh group with every op of one gesture that goes out as several: the flushes of one brush stroke, a room too big for one op sent in bands, an edge run, a large group of tokens moved together. The server merges an op's inverse into the newest step when that step has the same group: each entity goes back to its value before the first op, guarded by what the last op left. That holds only while nobody else changed an entity both ops touched, so an op that finds such an entity no longer what the step's guard expects starts a new step instead. Otherwise undoing the merged step would restore over another DM's paint in the same chunk without a word. Split, the older step's own guard refuses it. Only the newest step merges, so a gesture interrupted by another op of this member's starts a new step. A merged step that would pass the restore size cap starts a new one.
- `undo` takes the newest step and turns it into a `sys.restore` op before the reducer. An `undo` with `of` takes it only when that op of the same client made it (the token bar's "Undo move", section 7.7 below), and otherwise leaves the stack as it is. Only the `sys.restore` op is logged, so replay never depends on the stack. A redo stack mirrors it.
- A `sys.restore` op applies only if every guard still matches. Otherwise it answers "could not undo <what>: it was changed since" and the step is dropped. So undoing a rename never reverts a player scene change or a blackout that another DM made since. The text does not say who changed it: it may have been another DM, or this DM on another device.
- **A restore never strands anything.** Guards cover only what the step touched, not what refers to it. So a restore is also refused when it would remove a scene that a build chunk, label, token, area, drawing, fog tile, fog draft or the player scene still names, a character that a token or an initiative row still uses, or a token that an initiative row or an area (attached to it or cast from it) still names: "could not undo <what>: other work depends on it". Another DM may have painted the new scene, placed the new character's token or rolled the new token's initiative since. The other way round, it is refused when it would put back a token, an initiative row, an area or anything on a scene whose scene, character or token was deleted since: "what it belongs to was deleted since". A step that takes a scene back together with everything on it, such as undoing a scene's deletion, passes. Only references the restore adds are checked, so an entity that already pointed at something gone, which only a restore stored by an older build leaves, does not block later undos of it. Effect durations anchored to a token are not checked: deleting a token keeps them paused, and a restore that takes a token away leaves them the same way.
- **Only restores the server marks check references.** The server marks every `sys.restore` it builds from an undo or redo with `deps: true`, and the marked op is what goes into the op log, so a live undo and its replay run the same checks. The mark is optional in the schema, and a client cannot send a `sys.restore` at all. A restore stored by a build from before these checks has no mark and replays under the rules it was accepted under, even when it stranded work. Refusing it on replay would stop the room's load at that op and leave the room read-only (section 16.2), which is worse than the stranded work.
- **Undo says what it did.** Each step keeps a short phrase for what its op did, built on the server from the op and the state before it ("moving Goblin 2", "painting floor", "closing the handout Letter"). A merged step whose ops added, moved or changed more than one token between them names how many instead ("adding 6 tokens" for a placed encounter), since its first op's phrase names only one. The `ev` that acknowledges an undo or redo carries it in `restored`, for that client only, and the client shows "Undid moving Goblin 2." or "Redid ...". An empty stack answers "Nothing to undo." A refusal of an undo or redo reads as its own sentence, never as "Not saved.", since nothing was being saved, and a permission refusal of the restore reads the same way ("could not undo moving Goblin 2: that is not here"). A player's stack holds only their own drawings and token moves, and a player's phrase names a token as that player's projection showed it (a hidden name as the name players see, without its number), so a phrase never names something its reader could not see when they acted.
- **Taking an HP change back from the log** (features section 10, v1). The DM's `undo` may name a log line (`log`) instead of the stack. The server builds the restore from that line and the creature as it stands, the way a player's move undo is built: only the HP, life, death saves and the effects the change put on or took off go back, and everything else about the creature stays, its place and newer conditions included. A removed effect whose duration counted a row that has left the fight since comes back paused on that row's creature, as removing the row would have left it (section 9.5), or counting the creature's new row if it has joined the fight again. It is refused when the creature's HP state is not what the change left ("could not undo damaging Goblin 2: it was changed since"), when a removed condition's kind is back (a new concentration), when the line was already taken back, when the line is gone from the log the room holds or predates this, and when the conditions coming back, with those added since kept, would pass the 32 a creature holds ("at most 32 effects per creature"). The restore is guarded by the creature's value then, marked `deps`, and carries `undid`, the line's id, so the reducer writes a line saying the DM took the change back, and replay does the same. Its inverse goes on no stack: a redo would put the change back with no line in the log, and the line would then read as taken back for good. The DM deals the change again instead, and the log records it. Players never take a line back, and their projection never carries what one replaced.
- Ctrl+Z or Cmd+Z undoes, and with Shift redoes. They do nothing while typing in a text field, where the field's own undo is meant, and a held key's repeats are ignored. Since v1 wave 5 they live in the one key registry (section 14.17), which ignores them inside "View as a player", where they used to undo the DM's real last change.
- A `sys.restore` op passes `canApply` as the member who asked for it.
- No roll goes on a stack (v1 wave 7): the stored op is `sys.dice`, which no stack records. The DM reopens, marks or replaces an answer instead.
- The DM's stack records every DM op. A player's stack records only their own drawing ops and their token moves (`token.move`, `token.moveMany`, a `token.update` of nothing but elevation and facing, and `token.countLeg`).
- **A player's move undoes as a move** (features 13). A change of a token's elevation or facing alone counts as a move, so a player who drags a flyer and then raises it undoes the height first and then the drag, and neither reads as someone else moving it. So does marking whether its last move counts (`token.countLeg`, section 9.2). Such a step also keeps where it left each token. Its undo puts each token back where the step found it, with the path of the turn it had there, so the leg a move added goes with it, and leaves everything else about the token as it is now, so damage the DM dealt since survives the undo. It is refused when a token stands anywhere else, because someone moved it since: "could not undo moving Goblin 2: it was moved since". The path is part of the check and answers the same way: a token moved away and back stands where the step left it, but with later legs that putting back the old path would erase, and a mark the DM made since would be lost the same way. Damage and the turn passing change no path, so they never block it. A token the DM sent to another scene counts as moved, even on the same spot there, and the undo never puts a token back on another scene. A token the player cannot see now answers "that is not here" before its position is looked at, so a refusal never tells a player that a hidden creature moved. The server builds that restore when the undo arrives, guarded by the token's value then, and marks it `deps` like every other, so it is stored and replays like any restore. `canApply` lets a player's restore touch only tokens the player may move now, and only their position, elevation, facing and path. One that leaves a token on its spot, changing only its elevation or facing, takes an owner's rights instead, since owners may change those on a locked token too. So an owner can take back such a change on a locked token, and a player who moves a token they do not own still needs it unlocked. A redo moves the tokens again under the same rule. On a phone, the token action bar offers "Undo move" on the tokens a player just moved, while this device knows that move is the newest change on the player's stack. The offers follow the ops the device sends, as a short stack of its own latest moves (at most 20): a move, or a change of elevation or facing alone, goes on top. Once the server answers the bar's undo of the top one, the one below is offered, so a drag and three height taps take four presses to walk back, height first. The bar's "Doesn't count" (section 9.2) goes on top the same way, as a mark: while it is on top the bar offers no "Undo move", and pressing the lit "Doesn't count" takes the mark back with an undo of its op, which offers the move again. A drawing op, any other undo or redo (Ctrl+Z and the Draw tool's Undo included), or the server refusing a move or the bar's undo ends them all. Another device's undo or drawing is not seen, so the button names its move: the undo carries `of`, the `n` of the move's op, and the server takes back the newest step only when that op made it, and otherwise answers "could not undo that move: it is no longer your newest change". The DM's moves keep the whole-value restore above.

## 8. Ops and permissions

### 8.1 Ops catalog

Every op is a bounded zod schema in `shared/protocol.ts`. A permission rule in `shared/permissions.ts` sits next to each. "Owner" means the player owns the token or character.

| Group | Ops | Who |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Room | `room.update` (name, settings), `room.setPlayerScene`, `view.follow`, `view.blackout` | DM |
| Members | `member.update` (own name and color) | self |
| Scenes | `scene.create`, `scene.update`, `scene.delete`, `scene.reorder`, `scene.setMap`, `scene.setGrid`. v1 wave 3: `scene.update` also sets `autoWalls`, `folder` (null takes the scene out of its folder), `thumb` and `phases` (the whole list, section 14.11), and `scene.create` may carry `autoWalls` and `folder`. `scene.setPhase` switches the map to a phase. `scene.duplicate { sceneId, newSceneId, name, include: { creatures, characters, fog, drawings } }` copies a scene in one op (section 14.10). v1 wave 4: `scene.create` and `scene.update` also set `blockMovement` and `vision` (turning vision on turns fog on unless the same patch names `fogEnabled`), and a phase may carry a `light` level, which `scene.setPhase` writes into `vision.light`. A Universal VTT import's `scene.create` also carries `gridFromFile: true`, which no patch sets or clears (docs/design-v1-grid-detect.md section 6). | DM |
| Tokens | `token.create`, `token.update` (patch), `token.delete` | DM. When `playersCreateCharacters` is on, a player may `token.create` a plain PC token of a character they own, at the character's size, on the player scene, inside its bounds, one per character there. The player names it, as owners may rename a PC's token anyway. Companions (docs/design-v1-combat-flow.md 3.6 and 4.4): with no setting, a player may `token.create` a plain, unhurt companion of a character they own beside its token on the player scene, and a companion's owners may rename it, edit its owner stats, change its kind between creature and effect and its turn, mark its own Reaction (`companionReaction`) and `token.delete` it. |
| Movement | `token.move` (x, y, elevation, rotation), `token.moveMany`. v1 wave 4: `token.move` may carry `squeeze: true` and `teleport: 'wall' \| 'ledge'` (section 9.8). | DM. Owners. On visible, unlocked tokens, every player when `playersMoveAny` is `'all'`, or the players it lists. The canvas offers a drag by the same rule (`movesAnyToken`, client/canvas/dragRights.ts), tested against `canApply`. Since v1 wave 4, on a scene with `blockMovement` a player's leg must be clear of walls and ledges ("A wall is in the way." or "A ledge is in the way.", `forbidden`), and a `token.moveMany` with any stopped leg is refused whole. A teleport skips that test and nothing else, and must keep the token inside the scene ("a teleport lands inside the map"). A `squeeze` is refused with `invalid` unless the squeeze checks hold, for the DM too. The DM's moves are never stopped. |
| Path | `token.countLeg` (whether the last leg of the active creature's path this turn counts, section 9.2) | DM. Owners, a locked token included, as for its elevation and facing. Not players who only may move it. |
| Several tokens (v1 wave 5) | `token.updateMany { tokenIds, patch }`, 1 to 100 tokens and one patch of the fields that make sense for a group (`visibility`, `locked`, `disposition`, `hpVisibility`, `size` with `w` and `h`, `layer`, `kind`, `nameHidden`, `fogExempt`, `image`, `ownerIds`, `light`, `partyVision`). `token.deleteMany { tokenIds }`, 1 to 100, each through `token.delete`'s reducer. Each applies whole or not at all. | DM. A player may send `token.updateMany` with only the owner fields among them (`image`, `light`), on tokens they own: anything else is "owners cannot change <field>". |
| Token fields | `token.update` limited to `image`, `altImages`, `rotation`, `elevation`, `notes`, `rangeRings`, and `name` and `publicName` of a PC, and since v1 wave 4 `light` (null takes it off). `partyVision` is the DM's, and so is the actor's `monsterId` (v1 wave 5, null detaches it). | owners |
| Doors (v1 wave 4) | `door.set { sceneId, door: { edge } \| { cx, cy, id }, state: 'open' \| 'closed' \| 'locked', found? }` writes one door, on an edge or a free wall, and bumps the walls version. A door already in the asked state writes nothing. | DM, any door to any state, `found` included. A player only `open` or `closed`, on a door their projection shows (anything else, a secret door not yet found included, answers `gone`), not locked ("It's locked."), and within reach of a token of their own on the scene (section 9.8, "Too far from the door."). |
| HP | `actor.hp` (`{damage}`, `{heal}`, `{set}`, `{temp}`), `actor.deathSave`, `actor.life` (DM revive or kill), `actor.rest` (v1: `short` with the HP the Hit Point Dice gave, or `long`) | DM. Owners, except `actor.life`. Any player on visible tokens when `playersApplyDamage` is on, `damage` and `heal` only. Healing the dead and Revive's 1 HP: docs/design-v1-combat-flow.md 3.4 and 3.5. |
| Area damage (v1) | `actor.damageMany` (up to 100 `{target, amount}`, each through `actor.hp`'s damage, one undo step, section 9.3) | DM |
| Effects | `actor.effect.add`, `actor.effect.update`, `actor.effect.remove`, and since v1 wave 5 `actor.effects { add?, remove? }`, up to 100 of each, each item checked as its single op would be, applied whole or not at all | DM, owners. Only the DM sets or clears `hiddenFromPlayers`, and an owner naming a hidden effect gets `gone`. |
| Characters | `character.create`, `character.update`, `character.delete` | DM. Owners may update only `name`, `defaultImage`, `level`, `stats` (v1 wave 7, the stat block, each part replaced whole and `null` removing it), the actor's `hp`, `speeds`, `ac`, `initBonus`, `passivePerception`, `senses`, `notes` and `sheetUrl`, and on the character's own actor `srdId` and `wildShape`, a shape (docs/design-v1-pc-stats.md 3.6 and 4.1). With a stat block, `initBonus` and `passivePerception` follow it and a patch naming either is refused, the DM's too. A player's op that writes a block, a create carrying one or an update naming `stats`, spends the companion blocks' per-member budget. When `playersCreateCharacters` is on, a player may `character.create` one owned by that player alone, unhurt (HP full and above 0, no temporary HP, alive, no death saves), with no effects or DM notes and a portrait they may use, up to `LIMITS.charactersPerPlayer` (3) owned characters. The DM keeps every power over these. A player may `character.delete` a character whose `ownerIds` is that player alone, whoever made it and whatever `playersCreateCharacters` says (`playsAlone`, decided 2026-10-16). It removes what the DM's removes: every token of the character and its companions on every scene, and their rows. One the player shares is refused with `forbidden` ("someone else plays that character too. Ask the DM to delete it"), and one they do not play with "that is not yours". It goes on no undo stack (section 7.7), so it cannot be undone, and it writes a DM-only `characterDeleted` log line (section 7.3). |
| Areas | `template.create`, `template.update`, `template.delete` | DM. Players create their own and edit or delete only their own. |
| Drawings | `drawing.create`, `drawing.delete`, `drawing.clear` (own or all), kept rulers included | DM. Authors on their own. Since v1 wave 5 a player's `drawing.create` of any kind but `ruler` needs `playersDraw` to allow them, else `forbidden`, "The DM has not let you draw." Erasing and clearing their own stay allowed. |
| Fog | `fog.draft` (add or remove draft ops), `fog.publish`, `fog.discard`, `fog.live` (apply directly), `fog.reset` (all hidden or all revealed), `scene.update` for `fogEnabled` | DM |
| Builder | `build.paint`, `build.erase`, `build.rect`, `build.poly`, `build.edge`, `build.label`, `build.clear`. v1 wave 3: `build.walls { sceneId, put?, remove? }` for free walls (at most 500 per op and 48 KiB serialized, removals named by chunk and id), `build.stamps { sceneId, put?, remove? }` and `build.lights { sceneId, put?, remove? }` (at most 100 each per op). Removals apply first, and a put replaces the entity of its id. `build.edge` validates an edge as an edge, so the scene's east and south borders take walls and doors: a west edge `x,y,w` needs `0 <= x <= width` and `0 <= y < height`, a north edge `x,y,n` needs `0 <= x < width` and `0 <= y <= height`. That only loosens the old cell check, so every stored op still applies. v1 wave 4: `build.height { sceneId, cells, height }` paints ground from -100 to 200 ft in whole feet, 0 clearing it, and `build.stairs { sceneId, cells, up: 'a' \| 'n' \| 'e' \| 's' \| 'w' \| null }` marks or clears stairs, each at most 2,000 cells and bumping the walls version (section 9.9). | DM |
| Encounter | `encounter.start`, `.end`, `.add`, `.remove`, `.setValue`, `.setValues`, `.reorder`, `.next`, `.prev`, `.update` (entry flags) | DM. Players may `add` and `remove` their own characters' tokens, `setValue` their own entries, `update` only `reactionUsed` on their own entries (v1), and `next` when the active entry is theirs (End Turn). A `next` with `from` moves the turn only when it names the current turn of the player's own row, in their view. Otherwise it changes nothing, and it is refused only when it names the current turn of a row the player sees and does not own (section 9.4). |
| Log | `log.chat`, `log.whisper` | anyone |
| Handouts | `handout.show`, `handout.close`. Since PDF handouts `handout.show` takes either one image (`assetId`) or `pages` in order, at most 100, each `{ assetId, width, height }`, and the reducer writes `assetId` from the first page. `handoutDoc.create { doc: { id, title, pages } }` saves a document under the id the client chose, an id in use refused as invalid, and `handoutDoc.delete { docId }` deletes one, accepted at the storage quota. Every page must be a `handout` image of the room (docs/design-v1-pdf-handouts.md 3.3). | DM |
| Custom monsters | `monster.create`, `monster.update` (the stat block lines an edit changed, its `defaultImage` included), `monster.delete`, `monster.setSrdImage` (an SRD monster's default image, or null to clear it) | DM |
| Saved encounters | `savedEncounter.create`, `savedEncounter.update` (name, monsters, or party, where null goes back to the room's characters), `savedEncounter.delete` | DM |
| Journal (v1 wave 5) | `journal.create { page }`, `journal.update { pageId, patch: { title?, folder?, access?, blocks? } }` (the whole block list when any block changed), each under 32 KiB serialized, `journal.reveal { pageId, blockId, revealed }`, `journal.delete { pageId }` (which makes every pin linked to the page DM only and unlinks it) | DM |
| Map pins (v1 wave 5) | `pin.put { pin }` (create, or replace a pin of the same id on the same scene), `pin.remove { pinId }` | DM |
| Roll requests (v1 wave 5, section 9.11) | `roll.ask { request }`, `roll.answer { rollId, target, choice?, total?, result? }`, `roll.reopen { rollId, target }`, `roll.close { rollIds }` | DM. A player only `roll.answer`, for a target they own now, with a total or a save's `result: 'fail'` and no total ("players send the total they rolled, or fail a saving throw without rolling"). Anything else about a request, a missing one included, is `gone`, so a refusal never says whom else the DM asked. |
| Dice (v1 wave 7, docs/design-v1-dice.md 4) | `dice.roll { tag, part?, to, dc? \| ac?, rolls }` (1 to 20 formulas, 100 dice after each mode, 3 KiB), `roll.dice { tag, rollId, target, choice?, mode?, cancelled?, extra?, bonus?, unanswered?, change? }`, `actor.deathRoll { tag, target, mode?, cancelled?, extra? }` | Anyone rolls `dice.roll`, the DM for any creature that exists, a player only for one they own now (one they see and do not own is `forbidden`, "You roll only for your own creatures."). `roll.dice` as `roll.answer`, a player's only on a creature still waiting ("It was answered meanwhile."), and `change`, the DM's Allow of a pending ask, only while that ask is pending ("It was settled meanwhile."). `actor.deathRoll` as `actor.deathSave`. All before any draw. |
| Undo | `undo`, `redo` | anyone, on their own stack (section 7.7). An `undo` that names a log line is the DM's alone. |

The server makes one more op itself since v1 wave 4: `sys.explore { sceneId, tiles: { tx, ty, bits }[] }`, the explore step's write (section 7.2). No client may send it, and it is on no undo stack. Since v1 wave 7 also `sys.dice { asked, batch, results, typedBonus? }`, a dice op as the server resolved it, with every die drawn, held to the permission rule of the op it carries.

DM-only actions with side effects outside the room state run over HTTP (section 10.8): kicking a member, changing the player password, replacing the DM link, DM devices, deleting the room.

### 8.2 Reference checks

`canApply` checks every identifier in a player's op, not only the target:

- `attachedTo`, `casterId`, `tokenId`, `characterId`, duration anchors, a kept ruler's `anchors`, handout recipients and whisper recipients must name entities or members the player can see now.
- An `assetId` (token `image` and `altImages`, character `defaultImage`) must be an asset the player uploaded or one already in their visible-asset set, and of the right kind (`token` for tokens and characters). Since v1 wave 5 an icon image names no asset, so it passes with only a key of the right shape, and a player making their own character may pick one as its portrait.
- The DM's asset references are checked too: an asset of this room and of the right kind, so no op names another room's file. A monster's default image is a `token` asset, as for tokens. Since v1 wave 3, a stamp from the room's library wears a `token` asset, as a token does, every map phase is a `map` asset, and a scene's `thumb` is a `thumb` asset. Since v1 wave 5 the references a `sys.restore` would add are checked the same way, so an undo never brings back an image that was deleted (section 7.7).
- A `*.create` op whose ID already exists is rejected, so a create can never overwrite a hidden entity.
- Anything that fails is answered with `gone`.

For everyone, the reducer refuses with `invalid` an area attached to a token on another scene. Moving a token to another scene moves the areas attached to it, so an area never stays behind at a stale origin.

## 9. Rules engine (`shared/rules/`)

Pure functions over plain data, no DOM or Node APIs, fully unit-tested.

### 9.1 Footprints

| Size | Side (cells) |
| --- | --- |
| Tiny | 0.5 |
| Small, Medium | 1 |
| Large | 2 |
| Huge | 3 |
| Gargantuan | 4 |

`custom` uses `w` and `h`. `occupiedCells(token)` returns the whole cells the footprint overlaps, after snapping its position to the nearest half cell (Tiny) or whole cell. A Tiny creature occupies the one cell that contains it. Unsnapped tokens therefore still give clean rules answers.

### 9.2 Distance

`distanceFt(a, b, settings)` between two creatures, following the 2024 rule "count from a square adjacent to one to the other's square":

- Horizontally, use each creature's occupied cells as half-open ranges `[x0, x1)`. Per axis, the number of steps is `b.x0 >= a.x1 ? b.x0 - a.x1 + 1 : a.x0 >= b.x1 ? a.x0 - b.x1 + 1 : 0`. Adjacent creatures are 1 step (5 ft) apart, overlapping ranges are 0, and a Tiny creature next to a Medium one is 5 ft away.
- Vertically, the same formula over each creature's elevation range converted to cells: `[floor(elevation / feetPerCell), ceil((elevation + height) / feetPerCell))`. A Medium creature on the ground and a flier at 10 ft are 2 steps (10 ft) apart.
- `five`: `max(dx, dy, dz) * feetPerCell`.
- `fiveTen`: horizontal first as `max + floor(min / 2)`, then the same rule again with the vertical steps.
- `euclid`: `hypot(dx, dy, dz) * feetPerCell`, rounded to the nearest whole foot.

The function is symmetric, and the tests check that. The ruler uses the same functions between points. A point on a token takes the token's box. A free point stands on the ground under it as the measurer saw it when placing it (`pointCellBox` with the point's `z`, section 7.6): all ground for the DM, and for a player only ground their fog shows, 0 elsewhere. So from a cell at 0 to the next cell 10 ft up a ruler reads 10 ft, or 11 ft under `euclid`. With waypoints it sums the segments, and `fiveTen` carries its diagonal counter across segments, as the DMG variant requires. Under `fiveTen`, a creature two diagonal squares away is 15 ft away, so it is outside 10 ft reach.

Movement and range (v1, `client/features/measure/movement.ts` and `rings.ts`):

- **The turn's path** (section 6). Each move of the active creature on its own turn adds a leg, whoever makes it and however: a drop on a desktop or a phone alike (the project owner chose drops over a key or a pause to mark corners), a group drag, the DM's position edit, or a change of elevation, which is a leg straight up or down. A change of facing alone is no leg, and neither is a drop where it stood. The first move of a turn starts a new path where the turn began. A move outside the creature's turn, or outside a fight, adds nothing and drops a path left from an earlier turn, and so does a move to another scene, so a path never runs across two maps. A path keeps at most 50 stops: past that, its oldest corner goes and those two legs read as one straight leg, keeping the start and the latest moves. The joined leg does not count only when neither leg counted. When the corner that goes was one players who do not own the creature could not see, the joined leg is marked `joinedUnseen`, and its `uncounted` mark stays the DM's and the owners', as it was while the corner was there (section 7.3). A device that holds no start for the turn (a player who could not see it begin) cannot tell this turn's path from another's, and leaves the active creature's path for the server's answer.
- **Movement this turn** is the sum of the path's legs that count: the steps of the snapped top-left corner, as a dragged token's distance is, with the change of elevation as vertical steps, under the room's rule, each leg rounded on its own as a ruler's are. Under `fiveTen` the diagonal count runs on along the whole path, as along a ruler's legs, and a leg that does not count takes no diagonals. A creature that walks out and back has moved the whole way, and walking back is movement too. A turn that began before paths were recorded (its start has no `seq`) reads straight from its starting spot, as it did before. The trail draws a ring where the turn began, a dashed line through every stop with a dot at each corner, faint for a leg that does not count, so the table can see which squares the creature crossed for opportunity attacks. Nothing detects them. The marker shows the feet moved and, to the DM and the creature's owners, who receive its speeds, what is left of its walk, fly and swim speeds. The 2024 rules take the distance already moved off whichever speed the creature switches to, so every speed reads against the same total. Flying comes first while the creature is above the ground and can fly. Climbing, burrowing and conditions that change a speed are left to the table.
- **Doesn't count** (the project owner: "forced movement should not count"). After a move, the token's action bar offers "Doesn't count" beside "Undo move" to the DM and the creature's owners. It marks the last leg `uncounted` (`token.countLeg`) and leaves the token where it is: a shove, Misty Step or a teleport moves a creature without using its movement. The button stays lit while the leg does not count, and a press on it lit counts it again. It undoes like a move (section 7.7). Only the last leg, since the button sits beside that move's undo.
- **Drag colors.** During a fight, a drag of the active creature reads its total as its path so far plus the leg being dragged, the leg counting on the path's diagonals under `fiveTen`. Outside a fight, a drag of any token reads from where the drag began. Within the speed it is green, within a Dash (twice the speed) yellow, past both red, and the label says the same in words. Other creatures during a fight, and creatures whose speeds this viewer does not receive, keep the dragger's color.
- **Range rings** enclose the ground cells a ruler from the token to a free point would read in range: from the footprint, with the token's elevation, under the room's rule. "Every diagonal 5 ft" draws a square, 5-10-5 an octagon, true distance a stepped circle. A flier's ring shrinks once its height counts and is gone when the ground is out of reach. A ring is only its token's spot and a distance, so it is drawn above the fog.

### 9.3 Areas of effect

Every shape is a 3D predicate `inside(p: {x, y, z}) -> boolean` in world units (cells, with z converted from feet), plus a 2D bounding box. One mechanism then serves all six shapes, elevation, and every coverage rule.

| Shape | Origin | Predicate | Origin included |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sphere | a point | `distance3(p, o) <= r` | yes |
| Cylinder | center of the bottom circle (`anchor: 'bottom'`, default) or the top circle | `hypot(p.xy - o.xy) <= r`, and `z` within the height above (bottom) or below (top) the origin | yes |
| Cone | a point on the caster's edge (snapped to a corner or edge midpoint) | with axis distance `u` along `yaw` and 3D distance `v` from the axis: `0 <= u <= L` and `v <= u / 2` (53.13°, width equals distance) | no |
| Cube | a point on one face. `faceOffset.u` places the origin across the face and `faceOffset.v` up or down it, so the origin can be anywhere on the face. Cast from a token, the default puts the cube's bottom at the caster's elevation. | an axis-aligned box in the yawed frame, `L` on each side | no |
| Line | a point on the caster's edge | box of `L` by width by width along `yaw` | no |
| Emanation | the attached token's footprint and vertical extent | distance from the footprint's 3D box `<= d` | no |

The cone is a true 3D cone, so at a vertical offset it is narrower.

**Aimed up or down (v1).** A cone or line has a `pitch`, degrees above level from -90 (straight down) to 90 (straight up), so a cone aimed upward can reach a flier. Its axis tilts by the pitch and the same predicate holds in 3D: the cone's `u` and `v` and the line's box are measured in the tilted frame, so their signed distances stay exact. Cast from a token, it still starts on the caster's edge at its middle height. A steep cone's slice through its origin height is little more than a point, so an aimed area shades, outlines and is picked by what it covers seen from above: the convex outline of its 3D shape on the map (a cone's apex and the rim of its base, a line's eight corners), sampled with the same rules and the same shortcut. No one height suits every creature: near a cone's far end the lower rim of its base reaches past the point of the axis above a cell. So above each of its cells a creature is measured at several heights through its own body (its bottom, its top, the axis height clamped into it, and steps of at most a quarter cell between), and the best of them counts, as if the slice through the creature were taken where the area covers most of it. Only a part of the area thinner than a step can be missed. Level and absent are the same, so older templates are unchanged.

**Which cells a template covers (for shading).** Each cell gets a coverage fraction from points sampled at sample centers (so a shape that only touches an edge covers 0), at the template origin's height. The answer is always the 16x16 result, whatever the template's size:

- Each shape also provides a signed distance to its surface at the slice height, or a lower bound on it: exact for circles, boxes and rounded boxes, and the standard closed form for a cone.
- A cell whose center is farther from the surface than half the cell's diagonal lies entirely inside or entirely outside, so it is classified at once with coverage 1 or 0. On a scene with walls, this shortcut also requires the cell to lie entirely inside or entirely outside the cached visibility polygon (below). Any cell the polygon's edge crosses is sampled.
- Every other cell can be cut by the boundary and is sampled at 16x16. Those cells grow with the perimeter, not the area, so the work stays bounded.

Coverage = inside samples / total samples. Then apply the room's rule:

- `half` (2024 rule, default): coverage ≥ 0.5.
- `any` (Xanathar's template method): coverage > 0.
- `center`: the cell's center point is inside.

Xanathar's "token method", where shapes are redrawn on the grid, is not in the MVP.

**Which creatures it catches.** Evaluate at the creature's own height slice: `z` is the point of the creature's vertical extent nearest the template origin's height. Then, with the room's inclusion rule:

- `anyCell` (default, Xanathar's "a creature is in the area if its miniature is in an affected square"): any of the creature's occupied cells passes the coverage rule at that slice.
- `center`: the footprint's center point is inside the shape.
- `majority`: most of the footprint's sample points are inside.

So a fireball whose sphere does not reach a flying creature's height skips that creature, even though the ground cells under it are shaded.

**Walls.** When the scene has walls (from the builder, section 12, or an import), the area is clipped by a visibility polygon. For an origin point, `walls.ts` computes the polygon of everything visible from it with a standard angular sweep over the wall segments (O(w log w) for w segments). Since v1 wave 3 the sweep takes the pieces of the scene's split wall set that block areas, so walls that cross, such as free walls from an import, never let an area through (section 9.7). A sample point counts only if it is inside both the shape and that polygon. For an emanation, the polygon is the union of the polygons from five probe points of the origin footprint (center and four corners). The sweep ignores a wall through its origin, so a probe on or within 1/4096 cell of a stored wall that blocks areas is first moved off it with the bounded step of section 9.7 (`stepOffWalls`): toward the creature the area starts from where the walls allow, to that creature's side of a wall it lies exactly on, and to its own side of a wall it is only near. A probe that cannot be stepped sees nothing, so the area covers nothing from it rather than both sides of a wall. A probe near no wall moves a millionth of a cell, as it always has, so an area that touches no wall covers what it did before. Polygons are cached per derived wall set and origin point.

**Caching.** Coverage is cached per template on a key of every input that can change it: the template's `rev`, the attached token's `rev` (footprint, position and elevation), the room settings that affect coverage, and the walls version. Since v1 wave 3 that is the derived wall set's own version, which changes whenever the scene's chunks, automatic walls rule or bounds do, so a change of automatic walls or its undo refreshes shading, and an optimistic build the server refused never leaves shading behind under a version number the next build reuses. Creatures are tested only against their own few cells. Stage 2 benchmarks the worst bounded case (a 1,000 ft line at an angle in a 200x200 walled scene) on a phone, with an 8 ms budget for one recompute. While a template is being aimed, the preview may sample boundary cells at 4x4 and refine when the finger stops. The final, placed result always uses 16x16.

**Spell presets.** `shared/srd/area-spells.json` lists SRD 5.2.1 spells that create an area: name, level, shape, size, width, height, and whether it attaches to the caster. A script extracts candidates from the SRD text with patterns such as "20-foot-radius Sphere" and "15-foot Emanation". The output is reviewed by hand and committed. The review also lists when a spell hits a creature again (`triggers`, below), and the script checks each against the spell's text.

**Damage in an area (v1).** The DM opens Damage on a placed area, from the area tool or the Areas panel (`client/features/areas/AreaDamage.tsx`). It lists the creatures the area catches now, from the DM's view, so hidden ones included, each holder once. The DM types the damage the caster rolled, once for every target as SRD 5.2.1's "Damage against Multiple Targets" says, and marks each save Fail, Pass or Skip. A pass takes half, rounded down ("Half Damage", "Round Down"). Every creature fails until marked, and the dead are skipped. Objects the area catches are listed after the creatures and skipped until the DM marks one, since most spells name creatures and a fireball can still burn a cart. One `actor.damageMany` op carries every creature's amount, and the reducer puts each through `actor.hp`'s damage, so temporary HP is spent first, 0 HP, death saves and concentration checks work as for one creature, and each writes its usual HP lines. One op applies whole or not at all, and its inverse is one undo step ("Undid damaging 3 creatures."). Only the DM may send it: players roll their own damage, and the DM confirms what an area deals. The form says the damage landed only once the server takes the op, and stays as typed on a refusal. The form hears the answer to its own op from the store (`dispatch`'s `onAnswer`): taken only when the server acknowledged it. When the connection drops before the answer, a snapshot settles the op without saying whether it applied, so the answer is unknown, and the form stays and says to check the HP rather than invite a second, doubled hit. A player never receives the list, only the HP lines the log projection already allows.

**Save and damage (v1 wave 7).** The Damage page became the group save (docs/design-v1-roll-options.md 5), on the button "Save and damage". Its rows are the creatures the area catches, as above, until the roll freezes them. A spell preset now carries its save, half or none on a pass, damage parts with their per-slot dice, a cantrip's growth, conditions on a fail with how long they last, and whether a save at the end of each turn ends them (`AreaSpell`, `shared/srd/area-spells.json`), each checked against the spell's text by the import script as `triggers` are. The fill takes the DC from the caster's spell save DC and, for a character's own spell, its damage at the chosen slot. Monsters roll their saves on the server, a creature a player plays is asked through a roll request, and Apply sends one `actor.outcome` with each creature's amount, after its parts are halved and its resistances, immunities and vulnerabilities applied part by part, its conditions and any Legendary Resistance used. That op applies whole or not at all, is one undo step, and carries its plan's id as its receipt's tag, so an Apply whose answer was lost is settled by the receipt and never applied twice. `actor.damageMany` stays for replay and gains no new caller.

**Reminders (v1).** An area can remind the table when it hits a creature again (`triggers`), as the log line "Goblin 2 ended its turn in Spirit Guardians":

- `enter`: during a turn of a fight, a creature comes to be inside, by its own move (`token.move`, `token.moveMany`, a `token.update` of its place, size or scene) or by the area moving onto it (`template.update`, or the token it follows or surrounds moving). The 2024 spells that move count both ("whenever the Emanation enters a creature's space"). A move is one straight leg from where it was dropped from to where it lands, and it is swept every half cell, so a creature that crosses an area and stops beyond it, or an area moved right across one, entered it on the way. A token sent to another scene enters only where it lands. A reminder found partway along a leg is marked by what players could see at that point (the creature, and the token the area follows), so a crossing under fog, or an aura swept over someone from the dark, stays the DM's though the move ends in the open. Placing an area is not entering it: what it does when it appears is its cast, dealt with Damage above. An undo is not a move.
- `turnStart` and `turnEnd`: the creature whose turn `encounter.next` starts or ends is inside. A turn that the removal of the acting row passes on starts the same way, with its own `turn` line. Previous and an undo start no turn, so they remind no one.
- How often: an area marked `oncePerTurn` reminds about a creature once a turn, whichever trigger comes first, since its spell hits "only once per turn". Any other reminds once a turn for each trigger, since Web and Sleet Storm hit "the first time" a creature enters on a turn and again when it starts a turn there, and a creature that enters Grease and ends its turn there saves twice. A second entry in the same turn is not reminded again, though Grease and Spike Growth count every one: the DM remembers it. A turn is everything since the last `turn` line.
- Objects and the dead are never reminded. The area's own origin creature is left out unless the area includes it, as for who it catches.

`shared/reducer/areaTriggers.ts` writes them around the reducer: before `encounter.next` for the turn that ends, after it for the one that starts, and after a move. They are ordinary log events, so the projection decides who reads them (section 7.3). Lines are written in template and token id order, so a replay writes the same ones. Only areas with `triggers` do any work, and only during a turn, so rooms from before it replay unchanged. Walls come from the same identity-keyed wall sets as everywhere else (section 9.7), so a room never reads another room's walls, or its own from before a build.

A preset sets `triggers` where SRD 5.2.1 says so: on entering and at the end of a turn for Black Tentacles, Cloudkill, Conjure Animals (a 10 ft emanation that follows the pack and leaves it out, since its saves are for the creatures near it), Conjure Celestial, Conjure Woodland Beings, Grease, Incendiary Cloud, Insect Plague, Moonbeam, Spirit Guardians and Symbol, on entering and at the start for Sleet Storm, Web and Zone of Truth, at the start for Stinking Cloud, at the end for Gust of Wind, and on entering for Spike Growth. It sets `oncePerTurn` where the spell's text says "only once per turn": Black Tentacles, Cloudkill, Conjure Animals, Conjure Celestial, Conjure Woodland Beings, Incendiary Cloud, Insect Plague, Moonbeam, Spirit Guardians and Symbol. The area tool's More options turn each on or off on any area, once a turn included. The DM always gets the reminder as a toast too. Of the players, the one whose creature it is and the one whose area it is get one, because one saves and the other rolls the damage. Everyone else who may read it finds it in the log.

### 9.4 Initiative

- Order: `value` descending, then `tieOrder` ascending. `tieOrder` is set by drag-to-reorder. A new tie defaults to PCs before monsters, then higher `initBonus`, then name. In 2024 the DM decides ties, so the default is only a starting point.
- Values: players type their own results from physical dice or the D&D Beyond app. The DM types monster rolls too, or lets auto initiative (below) roll them. `encounter.setValues` lets the DM enter several at once. Identical monsters share a `groupKey` (the SRD ID, or the base name without the number), so the DM can type one value for the group.
- `monsterInitScore`: when on, `encounter.add` fills a monster entry with its actor's `initScore` (10 + Dex modifier). That is a fixed number, not a roll, and the DM can overwrite it.
- Advantage reminders: the tracker marks entries that roll with advantage (Invisible) or disadvantage (Surprised, Incapacitated) from their current effects, and the DM can toggle `advantage` by hand. They remind whoever rolls the dice, and auto initiative rolls by them.
- **Auto initiative** (the project owner's decision 14 in section 17, `client/features/encounter/autoRoll.ts`) rolls monster initiative on the DM's device, modeled on D&D Beyond's map combat tracker:
  - **The roll happens on the server** (v1 wave 7, docs/design-v1-roll-options.md 7). The DM's device sends one `roll.dice` input per shared roll, its d20 formula with the modifier and any extra die from the row's preset, labelled "Initiative" or "Initiative, shared by 4", naming the group's first token as its creature, secret to the DM and batched under one tag. When the tag's lines land, each row takes its group's total, clamped as before, and the totals go with `encounter.setValues`, in batches of 100 under one undo group (section 7.7). So players receive what typed values would give them: each visible monster's total, as if the DM typed it, and at most the bare secret line of section 7.3, never the dice or modifiers. A hidden row's value reaches only DMs, as a typed one does. Before wave 7 the DM's device rolled each d20 with `crypto.getRandomValues` and sent only the totals.
  - **Which rows roll.** Monsters and NPCs the DM runs: a non-PC token with its own actor and no player owner. Player characters and a creature a player owns (a familiar, a sidekick) never roll, since their players roll for them. A DM's view holds one scene's tokens, so a row whose token is on another scene cannot be rolled there, and the view cannot tell what it is. The tracker counts every such row as a creature on another scene, player characters included, and asks the DM to view that scene to roll any monsters there. It never guesses from the `groupKey`: Ungroup clears it from monsters, and a player's familiar has one. A dead monster keeps its value. By default only rows still waiting for a value roll, such as a monster added mid-fight. A switch re-rolls every monster.
  - **The modifier** is the actor's `initBonus`, which every actor has: a token from a 2024 stat block takes its printed "Initiative +X", and a 2014 paste its Dexterity modifier. A hand-made token has what the DM entered, +0 until then, and its row's options say "no initiative bonus set" while it is that default (no challenge rating, which every stat block token carries, and +0).
  - **Advantage** comes from the row's reminder above: two d20s, keeping the higher or the lower.
  - **How rolls are shared.** The mode is remembered per room on the DM's device:
    - By creature, the default and D&D Beyond's behavior: monsters with one `groupKey` share one roll, and every other monster rolls its own. A boss is its own creature, so it rolls alone while its identical followers share a roll. A member whose modifier or reminder differs from its group's (an Invisible goblin) rolls on its own, so every total is its own d20 plus its own modifier. A monster added to a running fight whose identical group already has a value takes that value instead of rolling alone, as D&D Beyond adds it to its group (the owner's decision after the code review). The DM can still change its value or ungroup it. When the group's members hold different values, the DM has split them, so there is no one value to take and it rolls. The per-row roll in a row's options always rolls.
    - One each: every monster rolls its own.
    - All at once: one d20 for every monster, so they all act together. It adds the modifier most of them have, the higher on a tie, and keeps a reminder only when every monster shares it.
  - **Grouping** is the rows' `groupKey`. A row's options offer D&D Beyond's Ungroup, which clears the key on every row of the group, and "Group N identical", which restores it. Both are `encounter.update` patches, DM only, one undo step, and stripped from players' projections like the key itself.
  - **What the DM sees.** A card in the tracker lists each shared roll's dice, modifier and total, and who took it, until the DM dismisses it. The card belongs to its fight: it holds while at least one of its rows is still in the encounter with the total it rolled, and goes for good once none is, when the fight ends, another starts or an undo takes the roll back. The always-visible initiative strip clears it, so this happens with the tracker closed too. While the roll's ops wait for the server the card stays, since a DM's view that lacks some rows' tokens cannot predict them (section 7.4). A row's options also roll that one monster, D&D Beyond's per-creature roll for a monster added mid-fight.
  - **Ties** follow the rule above. D&D Beyond documents no tie rule of its own. Rows that tie on everything the rule compares, such as a group of goblins, keep the tracker's order.
  - **Initiative scores.** With `monsterInitScore` on, monster rows enter with their score, so they are not waiting for a value and auto initiative leaves them be. The score wins unless the DM turns on the re-roll switch, which rolls over it, as D&D Beyond's Auto Initiative replaces its static scores.
- `next` and `prev`:
