The quota in section 10.1 counts every byte the room owns: assets, the snapshot, op rows, `room_log` rows and `client_watermarks` rows, and since v1 wave 7 `roll_receipts` rows. A `dice` line from a 3 KiB op stays under about 4.5 KB, near the longest whisper the log holds, so a roll moves no worst case of the log, a snapshot or the export.

**Room the party's sight and exploring take** (v1 wave 4, addendum 2.4). Sight is derived, so the size guard (`server/rooms/sizeGuard.ts`) reserves it instead of measuring it: on a scene with vision on, the projected-scene check measures the projection without sight and adds the scene's tile count times the most one sight tile entry can serialize to under that scene's own key (`sightEntryMaxBytes`), about 235 KB for a 200 x 200 scene with a UUID scene ID. The explore step is never refused (section 7.2), so the explored layer's growth is reserved in advance too: only the player scene explores, and only while it has vision and fog on, so only it has a reserve, the most every tile inside its bounds can serialize to less what the layer already holds there, about 137 KB for a full 200 x 200 layer with a UUID scene ID. Every op's room size check, and the player scene's projected size check, add that reserve, so exploring only ever spends reserved space and a room never passes 8 MiB. Every op that makes another scene the exploring one is checked with the new reserve in place of the old (moving players, turning vision or fog on for the player scene, their undo, deleting the player scene, a room import), and refused when it does not fit: "The room is too full for the party to explore that scene. Turn vision off there, or delete unused scenes to free space." Turning vision off removes the reserve, so the move then goes through.

**Room-level data in every DM's scene** (v1 wave 5, docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 2.4). Journal pages and roll requests are room level, so an op on them touches no scene, yet it grows every DM's projection of every scene, one nobody is viewing included. So the size guard splits a DM's projected scene into the room-level part every DM projection shares (custom monsters, the journal, roll requests, characters, the log with every whisper in memory counted, since a whisper reaches only the DMs it names) and each scene's own part. The room-level part is measured exactly on every op, from sizes kept per collection and per entity object. Each scene's own part is measured once, from one pass over the stored entities grouped by scene, the first time the guard checks the room, and kept with its growth bound as before. Every op is checked against the room-level part plus each scene's part, so a room with 1 MiB of monsters and 1 MiB of journal has about 1 MiB left for its largest scene. A scene already over the limit, as a room from before whispers counted may be, refuses only an op that grows it, on the scene or in the room-level part.

With these limits, a projected scene snapshot is at most about 2.5 MB uncompressed: tokens about 500 KB, drawings about 800 KB, build chunks about 850 KB, fog about 115 KB, the rest small. A typical scene is 100 to 300 KB. The 3 MiB check enforces the bound. v1 wave 3 adds up to about 300 KB of free walls and about 100 KB of stamps per scene, and v1 wave 4 up to about 235 KB of sight tiles, reserved as above, so a scene at every limit at once would now pass 3 MiB, and the check refuses the op that would cross it. Every reducer and rules call has bounded work. The per-connection held map stores 128-bit hashes, and values only where the room already holds them (section 7.3), so it costs at most a few hundred KB per socket, and the 400-socket server cap bounds the total to about 100 MB in the worst case.

### 10.6 Outbound requests and SSRF

The app container has no route to the internet. It reaches out only through the `egress` squid container, which listens on two ports:

- `3128` **strict:** allows `serpapi.com` (the API and its thumbnails), since v1 wave 5 `api.openverse.org` (the second image search and its access token, section 11.3), since v1 wave 7 `character-service.dndbeyond.com` (a public D&D Beyond character a member imports, docs/design-v1-pc-stats.md 6.3, which an operator turns off by removing the host), and, when configured, `challenges.cloudflare.com` for Turnstile verification. Nothing else.
- `3129` **public images:** allows any host on port 443, for URL imports and the import's fallback to a Google search thumbnail. It denies every private, loopback, link-local, CGNAT, multicast and reserved range, and denies `CONNECT` to ports other than 443.

The app calls each port with an explicit `undici` `ProxyAgent`, never through environment proxy variables. So the choice of port is visible at each call site, and no fetch can silently go direct. TLS verification is never disabled.

In development without squid, `fetchPublicImage` connects directly through an undici `Agent` with a `connect.lookup` that resolves DNS and rejects non-unicast addresses with `ipaddr.js`. IP-literal hosts are rejected before the lookup, because undici skips the lookup for them.

`fetchPublicImage(url)` also enforces:

- `https:` only, port 443, no userinfo, URL at most 2 KB.
- `redirect: 'manual'`, re-validating each hop, at most 3 hops.
- A 10 s timeout and `Accept-Encoding: identity`.
- A 10 MiB cap checked against `Content-Length` and again while streaming.
- Magic bytes via `file-type`: JPEG, PNG, WebP, GIF and AVIF only. SVG is always refused.

The bytes then go through the same sharp pipeline as uploads.

### 10.7 HTTP hardening

- CSRF: non-GET requests must carry `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`. If the header is absent, an exact `Origin` match is required. JSON bodies must be `application/json`. Uploads must be `image/*`. Imports must be `application/zip`. No CORS headers are ever sent. No GET changes state.
- Headers on the SPA:
  - `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; img-src 'self' blob: data: https://serpapi.com https://encrypted-tbn0.gstatic.com https://encrypted-tbn1.gstatic.com https://encrypted-tbn2.gstatic.com https://encrypted-tbn3.gstatic.com https://api.openverse.org/v1/images/; connect-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'self'`. The Openverse source (v1 wave 5) is a path, which narrows what the page may ask for: after a redirect a browser checks only the scheme, host and port (CSP3's "Paths and Redirects"), so the server's own thumbnail rule is the real check (section 11.3). Turnstile adds `https://challenges.cloudflare.com` to `script-src` and `frame-src` when enabled. WebKit has matched `wss:` with `connect-src 'self'` since 2022.
  - `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Permissions-Policy: camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=()`.
  - React sets styles through the DOM style API, which CSP `style-src` does not restrict, so no `'unsafe-inline'` is needed.
- `sheetUrl` must be `https:` and at most 500 characters. Links open with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`.
- `GET /healthz` returns only `ok` or `degraded`, with no room information.
- Node `server.keepAliveTimeout` is 95 s and `headersTimeout` 96 s. cloudflared keeps idle origin connections for 90 s, so a shorter Node timeout gives sporadic 502s on uploads.
- Body limits: 64 KiB for JSON, 32 MiB for map uploads, 10 MiB for token and handout uploads, 512 KiB for scene thumbnails (v1 wave 3), 95 MB for room import (under Cloudflare's 100 MB request limit).

### 10.8 HTTP API

Every non-GET route below applies the CSRF rule in 10.7. "Grant" means a valid, current-epoch grant for the room in the path.

| Route | Who | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `POST /api/rooms` | anyone, limited | create a room (10.1) |
| `POST /api/rooms/import` | anyone, limited as a creation | import a room export (16.4) |
| `GET /api/rooms/:id` | anyone | room name only, for the join page |
| `POST /api/rooms/:id/join`, `/join/complete` | anyone with the password, limited | player join (10.2) |
| `POST /api/rooms/:id/dm` | anyone with the key, limited | DM claim, or the DM members to choose from (10.2) |
| `POST /api/rooms/:id/dm/invite` | DM grant | mint a device invite |
| `POST /api/rooms/:id/dm/redeem`, `POST /api/dm/redeem` | anyone with an invite, limited | redeem a device invite (the second form, for a bare token, finds the room from the invite) |
| `GET /api/rooms/:id/dm/devices`, `DELETE /api/rooms/:id/dm/devices/:sessionId` | DM grant | list and revoke DM devices |
| `POST /api/rooms/:id/dm/replace-link` | DM grant | replace the DM link |
| `DELETE /api/rooms/:id/dm/members/:memberId` | DM grant | remove a member's DM role |
| `POST /api/rooms/:id/password` | DM grant | change the player password |
| `GET /api/rooms/:id/player-link` | DM grant | the player link from the sealed password, or null (10.2) |
| `DELETE /api/rooms/:id/members/:memberId/grants` | DM grant | kick |
| `DELETE /api/rooms/:id/members/:memberId` | DM grant | kick, then remove the member from the roster |
| `DELETE /api/rooms/:id` | DM grant, with the room name typed to confirm | delete the room |
| `GET /api/rooms/:id/me` | any grant | the caller's member and role, and the room's name |
| `GET /api/rooms/:id/log?before=` | any grant | older log pages, projected |
| `GET /api/rooms/:id/receipts/:tag` | any grant, limited | v1 wave 7: what became of the caller's own ops of one tag, each part committed with its lines projected for the caller or refused with its words, read by key. Another member's tag answers nothing (docs/design-v1-dice.md 1.2) |
| `GET /api/rooms/:id/log/sessions` | any grant, limited | v1 wave 5: the caller's play sessions in their projected log (14.16) |
| `GET /api/rooms/:id/log/export?from=&to=&tz=` | any grant, limited | v1 wave 5: one session or the whole projected log as Markdown (14.16) |
| `POST /api/rooms/:id/xcard` | any grant, limited | v1 wave 5: tap the X-card, unlogged and touching no session row (14.16) |
| `POST /api/rooms/:id/ddb-import { link }` | any grant, limited | v1 wave 7: a public D&D Beyond character read into a stat block's fill. The server builds the one address it fetches from the link's digits, on the strict egress port, 10 s and 5 MiB, with hourly budgets per session, address and server and two slots, one a room. It answers `{ fill }` or `private`, `not_found`, `fetch_failed`, `unreadable`, `busy` or a `quota_*` code, and keeps no copy (docs/design-v1-pc-stats.md 6.3) |
| `POST /api/rooms/:id/display/code` | DM grant | v1 wave 5: mint a table display code (10.2) |
| `POST /api/display/redeem` | anyone with a code, limited | v1 wave 5: pair this device as a room's table display (10.2) |
| `GET /api/rooms/:id/display/me`, `DELETE /api/display/session` | a display session | v1 wave 5: the display's room, and Leave (10.2) |
| `GET /api/rooms/:id/display/assets/:assetId/:variant` | a display session | v1 wave 5: an image in the display's projected visible set (11.2) |
| `POST /api/rooms/:id/assets/delete` | DM grant | v1 wave 5: delete library images nothing uses, at most 500 (16.3) |
| `POST /api/admin/login`, `DELETE /api/admin/session` | anyone with the admin password, limited | v1 wave 5, only with `ADMIN_PASSWORD_HASH`: admin sign in and out (15.8) |
| `GET /api/admin/storage`, `GET /api/admin/rooms` | admin session | v1 wave 5: storage use, and every room without its contents (15.8) |
| `POST /api/admin/rooms/:id/pin`, `DELETE /api/admin/rooms/:id` | admin session, the room ID typed to confirm a delete | v1 wave 5: pin or unpin, and delete a room through the DM's own delete path (15.8) |
| `PUT /api/rooms/:id/assets?kind=` | any grant (maps, handouts and scene thumbnails DM only) | upload (11.1) |
| `GET /api/rooms/:id/assets` | any grant | list the library (11.2), since v1 wave 5 with `inUse` and `bytes` for a DM |
| `POST /api/rooms/:id/assets/import` | any grant | import an image from a URL (11.3) |
| `POST /api/rooms/:id/image-search` | any grant, limited | search (11.3) |
| `GET /api/assets/:assetId/:variant` | any grant for the asset's room | read an image (11.2) |
| `GET /api/rooms/:id/export` | DM grant | export (16.4) |
| `GET /healthz` | anyone | `ok` or `degraded` |

## 11. Assets and image search

### 11.1 Upload and processing

- `PUT /api/rooms/:id/assets?kind=map|token|handout|thumb` with a raw `image/*` body. DMs upload maps, handouts and scene thumbnails (`thumb`, v1 wave 3, section 14.10). Members upload token images.
- Quota is reserved before processing starts: the request's `Content-Length` (or the size cap) is reserved against the room quota, and temporary bytes count against that reservation. After processing, if the variants together are larger than the reservation, the difference is reserved atomically before the asset row is written. If that fails, the files are deleted and the answer is `413`. Concurrent uploads therefore cannot overshoot the quota.
- Before reading any body bytes, an upload or import takes one of 4 server-wide intake slots. With none free, the answer is `503` with `Retry-After` and the body is never read. The slot is held through streaming, processing and cleanup.
  - No IP may hold more than 1 slot, no room more than 2, and imports at most 1 server-wide. One slot is kept free for pinned rooms, so a pinned room can always upload. For a pinned room the per-IP share is counted per session instead, so the DM's map upload never blocks the players' token uploads at the same table.
  - A body that arrives slower than 32 KiB per second after its first 10 s is aborted. Each upload has a deadline: 60 s for tokens and handouts, 180 s for maps, and 600 s for an import. So nobody can hold a slot by trickling bytes.
- The body streams to a temporary file on the asset volume while counting bytes and hashing with SHA-256. Requests over the size limit get `413`. Temporary data on the volume is therefore bounded: at most 4 bodies at their size caps, plus each one's processed variants, plus at most one retained import zip (95 MB). The free-disk guard is checked again before each job and each import entry.
- Processing runs in a queue with one sharp job at a time (`sharp.concurrency(1)`, `sharp.cache(false)`). The queue holds at most 8 jobs. Beyond that the answer is `503` with `Retry-After`. Room imports go to the back of the queue, so they never delay a live upload for long.
  - Loaders are restricted at boot with `sharp.block` and `sharp.unblock` to JPEG, PNG, WebP, GIF and AVIF. SVG and everything else is blocked.
  - `limitInputPixels: 100_000_000` and `failOn: 'warning'`.
  - `autoOrient()` runs before any resize.
- Variants, all WebP:
  - **Map:** `full` at most 8192 px on the long side, `mobile` at most 4096, `thumb` at most 512.
  - **Token:** `full` at most 512, `thumb` 128.
  - **Handout:** `full` at most 2048, `thumb` 512.
  - **Scene thumbnail** (kind `thumb`, v1 wave 3): one variant, `full`, at most 320, from an upload of at most 512 KiB with a 30 s deadline. The kind is DM only to upload, and players never read one: no projection gives them its ID (section 7.3).
- Files are named by content, kind and pipeline version: `/data/assets/<first two hex chars>/<sha256>-<kind>-<variant>-v<pipeline>.webp`. The same image uploaded as a map and as a token gives different files. A change to the processing recipe bumps the pipeline version, so an old file is never served under a new recipe.
- Files are shared between rooms that upload the same bytes. A file is deleted only when no `assets` row in any room still refers to it. Since v1 wave 5 a deleted file first waits 48 hours in a trash folder on the same volume, so a backup running meanwhile can still copy it (section 15.7).
- An identical upload to the same room reuses the row and adds the uploader to `asset_uploaders`, so every member who uploaded those bytes may use them. The response carries nothing that reveals reuse: it never names the first uploader or the upload time, and asset IDs are random. A reused upload does answer faster, because it skips processing. That only tells a player something about a token image they already hold byte for byte, so response times are not equalized. A player's library lists what they added by when they added it (`asset_uploaders.added_at`), not when the room first held the bytes, so a reused upload never lists among older images.
- The client honors `503` with `Retry-After` from uploads, URL imports and searches: it keeps retrying, following `Retry-After`, for up to 60 s in total, showing "Waiting for the server…", and only then shows an error. At an in-person session start, several phones uploading at once simply queue.
- Before uploading, the client downscales token images to at most 1024 px and handout images to at most 2048 px on the long side (each the size of its largest server variant or more), and re-encodes them with a canvas (`createImageBitmap` with `imageOrientation: 'from-image'`, so phone photos keep their orientation). A 3 to 8 MB phone photo becomes a few hundred KB, which spares the remote player's mobile link and keeps intake slots short. Maps are uploaded at full size, because their resolution matters.
  - The client checks once at startup that the browser honors `imageOrientation`, by drawing a small built-in image with a known EXIF rotation. If it does not, the client skips the downscale and uploads the original, and the server's `autoOrient()` handles it. The server runs `autoOrient()` on every upload either way, because the client step is a convenience and is never trusted.
- The response is the asset row: id, kind, canonical width and height, variant list. A map upload's response also suggests a `cellPx` when the file name contains a hint such as `30x20` (Owlbear's trick).
- **A handout upload's answer says who made the image** (docs/design-v1-pdf-handouts.md 3.9): `created` is true when this request inserted the row, and false when it reused one the room had, from an earlier upload or a concurrent one that won the insert. Only a handout upload carries it. Only a DM makes one, and a DM's library already lists every handout image, so it reveals nothing. A token upload's answer still never reveals reuse. The PDF upload job deletes only the pages it made.
- **A PDF's pages are ordinary handout uploads** (docs/design-v1-pdf-handouts.md 4): the DM's browser draws each page in a worker of its own, at most 2048 px on the long side, and uploads it as WebP, or JPEG where the browser cannot encode WebP. The server never sees the PDF, and each page takes the handout path above, with the handouts' own hourly budget (section 10.3).

### 11.2 Listing and serving

- **What a player may see:**
  - Assets the player uploaded (any row listing them in `asset_uploaders`), of a kind a player may upload, which is `token` alone. An uploader row on a map, handout or thumbnail, such as a hand-made room import could once write, gives a player nothing: those reach a player through the projection or not at all (v1 wave 3).
  - Assets referenced in the player's current projection: the player scene's map (the active phase's image only, v1 wave 3), visible tokens' images, the other images of the tokens they own (a non-owner's projection carries none), visible characters' images, the library images of the stamps in their projection (v1 wave 3), and handouts shown to them, every page of each (`handoutPages`). Other phases' images, scene thumbnails and the pages of a document not shown to them are never in it.
  - The visible-asset set is computed from the projection and cached per `(room seq, member)`.
- **What a DM may see:** every asset of the room, every phase's image and scene thumbnail included.
- A `thumb` asset is the DM's alone whatever names it: the listing leaves thumbnails out for a player, and a player's read of one answers 404 like any asset they may not see (v1 wave 3).
- `GET /api/rooms/:id/assets` lists exactly the assets the caller may see. A player's library shows their own uploads and the images on tokens they can see. Prep maps, unshown handouts, hidden monster art and monsters' default images (section 7.3) never appear in it. Since v1 wave 5 a DM's listing marks each row `inUse`, from the committed state (`referencedAssetIds`), with its `bytes`, so the DM's library can mark images "Not in use" and delete them (section 16.3).
- **An asset belongs to its room by its row,** never by what the state refers to. The quota sums the room's rows (section 10.5), room deletion removes them and then any file no row in any room still names (section 11.1), and an export carries every row (section 16.4). So an image used only as a monster's default image counts, goes and travels like any other.
- `GET /api/assets/:assetId/:variant` applies the same rule. `:variant` must be one of the asset row's variants before any file path is built.
- **A table display** (v1 wave 5) holds no room cookie, so it reads images from `GET /api/rooms/:id/display/assets/:assetId/:variant` through its display session, allowed exactly for its projected visible set: the map showing, the images of tokens it can see, stamps' library images and handouts shown to everyone, every page of each. The uploader rule never applies to it, since it uploads nothing, and the library listing refuses it like any ordinary route. The headers are the asset route's.
- Headers:
  - `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable`, because a variant URL never changes content.
  - `Content-Type: image/webp`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`.
  - `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`.
  - `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`.
- `private` also keeps Cloudflare from caching an access-controlled image at its edge.
- Phones load the `mobile` map variant by default. A device setting can switch to `full`: since v1 wave 5, Map detail under Menu, This device (section 14.17).

### 11.3 Image search (SerpApi)

- `POST /api/rooms/:id/image-search` with `{q}` (members only). It is a POST because it spends quota and writes the cache.
- The query is trimmed and lowercased as the cache key. Results are cached in `search_cache` for 7 days. A repeated search costs nothing.
- Quotas:
  - **Global, from SerpApi itself:** before a search, the server reads the Account API (`https://serpapi.com/account.json`, which costs no search) for `plan_searches_left` and `this_hour_searches`, cached for one minute and decremented locally for each search in between. It refuses when fewer than 10 searches are left or when this hour's count reaches 45. SerpApi counts searches on its own cycle, so the provider's own numbers are the reliable source.
  - **Per room:** 60 per calendar month in `search_usage`. A room younger than 24 hours gets 5.
  - **Per IP:** 15 per day.
  - A search reserves its count before calling SerpApi, so concurrent searches cannot overshoot.
  - When a quota is spent, the picker says so and offers upload, paste and URL.
- The server calls `https://serpapi.com/search.json?engine=google_images&safe=active&q=...&api_key=...` through the strict egress port. The key comes from `SERPAPI_KEY` and is never sent to the client. Without a key, Openverse answers instead (below), so since v1 wave 5 the search tab always shows.
- The response keeps up to 40 results: `thumbnail`, `original`, `title`, `source`, `original_width` and `original_height`. A result is kept only when its thumbnail is on a thumbnail host (`shared/searchThumbnails.ts`): serpapi.com, or Google's `encrypted-tbn0` to `encrypted-tbn3.gstatic.com`. SerpApi hosted thumbnails on serpapi.com until 2026, then started returning Google's own thumbnail URLs, which made every search come back empty until those hosts were allowed. The search call waits up to 30 s, because SerpApi has taken 19 s.
- The client shows the thumbnails directly from their host (allowed by CSP `img-src`). That host, SerpApi or now usually Google, sees the IPs of players who search. Proxying thumbnails through the server is a small change if a server owner prefers it.
- Picking one calls `POST /api/rooms/:id/assets/import` with `{url: original, fallback: thumbnail, kind: 'token'}`.
  - The server fetches the original through the public egress port.
  - If that fails (hotlink block, too large, wrong type), it fetches the thumbnail instead: a serpapi.com thumbnail through the strict port, a Google thumbnail through the public port (an ordinary public host). The fallback must be a thumbnail-host URL that appeared in a cached result.
  - Either way the bytes go through the sharp pipeline and become a normal asset.
- "Paste image URL" uses the same import endpoint without a fallback.

**Openverse** (v1 wave 5, docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 3.13, `server/assets/openverse.ts`) is the second source, for openly licensed images.

- **When.** The route tries SerpApi first, and turns to Openverse when SerpApi cannot answer: no `SERPAPI_KEY`, the account under its floor of 10, this hour's 45 reached, or the room's or the address's SerpApi quota spent. The picker can also search it on purpose ("Search Openverse instead"). A picker whose day's quota is spent switches to the Icons tab (section 11.5).
- **The call.** `GET https://api.openverse.org/v1/images/?q=…&page_size=<20 or 40>&license_type=modification&mature=false&extension=jpg,png,webp,gif` through the strict egress port. `license_type=modification` leaves out no-derivatives licenses, since cropping an image into a token adapts it. Anonymous by default, 20 results. With `OPENVERSE_CLIENT_ID` and `OPENVERSE_CLIENT_SECRET` it gets a token from `/v1/auth_tokens/token/`, keeps it until 5 minutes before it expires (or half its life, if shorter), fetches it again once on a 401, and asks for 40.
- **Quotas:** server-wide budgets under Openverse's own, kept in `search_usage` and in memory (anonymous 150 a day and 15 a minute, registered 5,000 and 80), then 20 a day per member per room, since the table shares one address (section 10.3), 60 a day per room, and 150 a day per address as a flood cap. A search reserves its counts before calling. A 429 from Openverse answers "Openverse is busy. Try again in a minute." and holds off for its `Retry-After`, else 60 s.
- **Results** are cached for 7 days under their own key, apart from SerpApi's, and searches in flight are shared. A result keeps its thumbnail only when it is exactly `https://api.openverse.org/v1/images/<uuid>/thumb/` (`shared/searchThumbnails.ts` is now a host and path rule per source), an https original, and a credit: the title, creator, license and version, landing page and attribution (at most 400 characters). Thumbnails load from Openverse in the viewer's browser, as Google's do (decision 6), and one that fails to load shows a plain tile, "Openverse could not show this preview".
- **Credit.** Picking a result runs the import above. The server then stores, on the new asset row (`assets.credit`), the credit of the cached result whose original or thumbnail it fetched, with the original's width and height, never a credit the client sends. The library and the token panel show it, linked to its page with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`, followed by what was changed, as Creative Commons licenses ask of an adaptation: "Goblins and a Troll by Dunechaser, CC BY-NC-SA 2.0. Resized and converted to WebP." "Converted to WebP" is always said, "Resized" only when the stored size differs from the original's, and the token panel adds "Cropped." only where the crop shows less than the whole image.
- **The picker** says where results come from: "From Openverse: openly licensed photos and artwork. RaccoonVTT is not endorsed by Openverse.", as Openverse's terms ask, and each tile's title names its license.

### 11.4 Token crop

The token stores `image: {assetId, crop: {cx, cy, zoom}}`. The client renders the image clipped to a circle (or a square for objects) with that crop, and a ring in the disposition color. No per-token image is generated on the server, so changing a crop is a tiny op.

### 11.5 Token icons (v1 wave 5)

docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 3.12. A token's image can be one of 4,180 game-icons.net icons on a colored disk, picked with no upload, no search, no quota and no outside service (section 6, `ImageRef`).

- **The set.** `node scripts/game-icons.ts` fetches the `game-icons/icons` repository at the pinned commit `82d948812bfe3f269ef8f731dcdb07b08160edc4` with git, checks its tree hash, checks each file against the shape `scripts/icons.ts` checks, skips the repository's `badges` folder, drops the black background square, and writes each icon as one path at `public/game-icons/82d94881/<author>/<name>.svg`. It also writes `index.json` there (each author's name, link, license and icon names) and `shared/gameIcons.ts`. 4,180 icons by 36 authors, CC BY 3.0 but for the 125 CC0 icons of Viscious Speed, Zeromancer and "Various artists". The script fails on an author folder whose license it cannot name. The files are committed, as the stamps are, about 7 MB.
- **Serving.** One wildcard route serves `/game-icons/` with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`: the commit in the path means a URL's content never changes. A key the build lacks answers 404, not the app's page. A route per file had made boot about 0.4 s slower.
- **Keys** are `<author>/<name>`, checked by `/^[a-z0-9-]{1,40}\/[a-z0-9-]{1,60}$/`, never a path. The author is part of the key because 47 names exist under two authors. A key the client does not know draws as initials, so a renamed icon never breaks a token.
- **Color.** The picker offers 14 disk colors, the 10 member colors and 4 neutrals, Charcoal by default, and the protocol takes any `#rrggbb` or `#rrggbbaa`. The glyph is white or near-black, whichever reads better on the disk, worked out on the device and never stored.
- **Works offline** is read as needing no outside service (wave 5 question 15, which the project owner decided on 2026-10-14). The icon index, about 60 KB, loads with the panels the room preloads once connected (section 14.1), so the Icons tab searches offline, a pick queues like any offline edit, and the token draws its initials on its disk until the icon's file arrives. Every icon's art offline would take a service worker (section 14.5).
- **Search** runs over the words of the names and the author ("goblin", "dragon head", "skull lorc"). The site's own tags state no license, so the app does not copy them.
- **Drawing.** The canvas fetches each icon's file once, reads its path, and draws it scaled from 512 onto the disk, clipped as images are (a circle, or a square for an object), so it stays sharp at any zoom. In HTML (the tracker, the initiative strip, the token panel) the glyph is a CSS mask over a box of its color, as condition icons are.
- **The picker.** The image picker's first tab is Icons: the search, a windowed grid of 56 px tiles that load lazily, the colors, a preview on a token disk, and the icon's credit, "Wolf head by Lorc, CC BY 3.0". Section 20 has the credits.

## 12. Map builder

The builder edits a scene's build chunks and labels, since v1 wave 3 its stamps, which render on their own Konva layer above the map image (if any) and below tokens, and since v1 wave 4 its heights, stair marks and lights.

```ts
type TerrainId = 'stone' | 'wood' | 'grass' | 'dirt' | 'cave' | 'water' | 'difficult' | 'rubble' | 'pit' | 'pillar' | 'stairs'
type EdgeFeature =
  | { kind: 'wall' } | { kind: 'none' }   // 'none' removes an automatic wall
  | { kind: 'door', state: 'open' | 'closed' | 'locked', secret: boolean, discovered: boolean }
  | { kind: 'window' }
type WallFeature = Exclude<EdgeFeature, { kind: 'none' }>   // what a free wall carries (v1 wave 3)
// cells key "x,y"; edges key "x,y,n" or "x,y,w" (north or west edge of cell x,y), in scene coordinates
```

- **Chunks.** Cells, edges and free walls are stored in 16x16-cell chunks, so a brush stroke touches one or two small entities. Each scene keeps a walls version, bumped on every build change. It only ever goes up, through undo too (section 7.7). Before v1 wave 3 the rules caches were keyed on it. Since then they follow the identity of the scene's chunks (section 9.7).
- **Automatic walls** (the project owner's decision on the addendum's question 17, v1 wave 3). A scene's `autoWalls` rule decides which painted cells get a wall toward an unpainted neighbor, unless an edge feature overrides it. A cell holds one terrain, so the overlay terrains (water, difficult ground, rubble, pit, pillar, stairs) replace the floor in their cell rather than sitting on it. `shared/rules/autoWalls.ts` is the one predicate, and wall derivation and the secret door projection (section 7.3) both ask it, reading a neighbor chunk's cells across a chunk border, so they can never disagree.
  - `'all'`, "Around every painted cell": a wall between any painted cell and an unpainted one, the MVP's rule. Blank mats get it, new or existing, and so does every scene from before wave 3, which reads an absent field as `'all'`. It is the rule that keeps a stair corridor, a pillar against a room's edge or a water channel walled.
  - `'floor'`, "Around floor only": only floor terrains (stone, wood, grass, dirt, cave) make automatic walls. A scene made from a map image gets it from wave 3 on, since the art is the floor and painted water or stairs are notes on it. Compared with `'all'`, only the edges between an overlay and an unpainted cell change. A blank mat that later gets its first image keeps its rule, since its build was drawn under it.
  - `'off'`: no automatic walls. A Universal VTT import gets it, since the file brings its walls.
  - The scene settings show "Automatic walls" with the three choices and a note saying which edges each one walls. A change, by an edit or by an undo or redo, bumps the walls version and sets `global` (section 7.2).
  - Pillar, pit and rubble are drawn as obstacles on a floor. `walls.ts` derives the effective wall segments under the scene's rule (section 9.7).
- **Tools:**
  - Paint brush (1 to 3 cells).
  - Rectangle room.
  - Polygon room: tap points, close the shape, and cells whose centers are inside it become floor.
  - Erase, with the brush or a dragged rectangle ("Erase area"). An erase takes everything in the cells, not only the floor: the walls, doors, windows and "no wall" marks on all four sides of each cell, and every label whose text covers part of one. Since v1 wave 3 it also takes the free walls whose midpoint, and the stamps and lights whose center, lie in an erased cell, and since v1 wave 4 the heights and stair marks of erased cells. A token that stood on that ground lands on what is left, its elevation the ground under it, in the same undo step, while a flier above or a token inside the ground keeps its elevation. A point on a grid line belongs to the cell after it.
    - An edge on the scene's east or south border stays when the cell beside it is erased. The erase never took one, and v1 wave 3, which lets the builder draw them, kept that rule: changing it would change what a stored erase did on a scene shrunk since its edges were drawn, and replay must give the same state. Remove and Clear take a border edge.
  - Door: tap an edge to place it, tap again to step it through closed, open and locked. Drag along a grid line for a wide door. Secret doors have their own choice, hidden (players see a wall) or found (players see a door): a tap places one in that state or switches it, and a tap on one already in that state takes it off. So no tap ever shows players a hidden door by passing through other states. "Remove" takes a wall, door, window or "no wall" mark off an edge.
  - Wall, window and "no wall" on edges: tap an edge, or drag along a grid line.
  - **Border edges** (v1 wave 3). Edges are validated as edges (section 8.1), so walls and doors go on the scene's east and south borders too, which an import's walls need.
  - **Free walls** (v1 wave 3). The DM does not draw free walls by hand: they come from a Universal VTT import (section 14.9), and the DM fixes an import with edge walls, the eraser and the door states. Door mode steps a free door through closed, open and locked, keeping its id, and Remove takes a free wall, door or window off. A tap reaches a free wall within 22 px (half a 44 px target) and goes to it when it is at least as close as the nearest grid edge. A hidden secret free door is left alone with "That is a hidden secret door. Players see a wall there." The other edge choices work on grid edges only.
  - **Lights** (v1 wave 3). Every light draws as a small sun for the DM in every tool (grey while off), and never for players. The builder's Lights mode selects a light with a tap and rings its reach on the overlay: a solid ring at the bright radius and a dashed one at the dim radius's end, with "<label>: N ft bright, dim to M ft", where M is the outer ring's own radius (a light stores its dim band as feet beyond bright, so a Lamp reads "15 ft bright, dim to 45 ft"). In wave 3 lights came only from an import, and Erase and Clear take them.
  - **Lights by hand** (v1 wave 4, `client/features/lights/`). In Lights mode a tap on the map places the light chosen in "Light to place" (Candle, Torch, Lamp, Hooded Lantern, Light or Daylight, Torch by default): lit, casting shadows, named after its preset, snapped to the 1/64 lattice inside the scene. At 100 lights the client says "This scene already has 100 lights, the most it can hold." without sending. A press within 22 px of a sun selects it and rings its reach, a drag moves it with one `build.lights` on release, and a tap opens its pane (a card beside it on a wide screen, a bottom sheet on a phone, loaded for the DM alone): the presets, "Bright … ft, dim for … ft beyond" up to 120 ft each, "Lit" or "Out", "Walls cast shadows", a Name, Delete with a second tap, and Done. Each choice is one op and one undo step. Choosing a preset renames the light only while its name is still a preset's. While the pane is open a tap on empty map closes it rather than placing. The hint reads "Tap the map to place the light chosen. Tap a light to change it, or drag it to move it. Erase and Clear build take lights too.", or on a scene with none "This scene has no lights yet. Tap the map to place the light chosen."
  - **Heights and stairs** (v1 wave 4, `client/features/builder/heights/`, addendum 4.2). The mode "Heights and stairs" paints with the brush (1, 2 or 3 squares) or a dragged Area. Paint "Height" takes a stepper from 20 ft down to 100 ft up in 5 ft steps (10 ft up at first), shown as "10 ft up", "10 ft down" or "Ground (clears)" at 0, which clears heights. Its − and + repeat while held: one step on the press, one 400 ms into the hold and one every 80 ms after, until the end of the range. The value's live announcement waits for the press to end and then speaks once. Enter repeats by the keyboard's own repeat (docs/design-v1-heights-readout.md section 5). The model allows -100 to 200 ft. Paint "Stairs" marks stairs without painting terrain, so a stair goes over a map image: "Stairs point" offers Auto (toward the one higher neighbor), ↑ → ↓ ← for the cells the rule cannot settle, and ✕ to remove marks, Stairs terrain staying a stair. The hint says what a stroke does ("Drag to paint the height. Tokens standing there rise or sink with it.") and, for stairs, whether they get walls under the scene's automatic walls rule ("Here they get walls toward unpainted squares." under Around every painted cell, "Over the map image they get no walls." under Around floor only). In this mode each raised region shows its height as a pill ("↑10 ft", at most 300, largest first, never overlapping), and a stair the rule cannot point shows "?" once a cell is at least 8 px across. Cells already as the stroke would leave them are not sent, a stroke is one undo group, and Area paints on release, at most 2,000 cells per op.
  - **Stamps** (v1 wave 3, addendum 3.5). Stamp mode places pictures on the board (`client/features/builder/stamps/`):
    - A picker (a bottom sheet on a phone, a card from 600 px up, a modal dialog that gives focus back to the button that chooses a stamp) shows the shipped art as 56 px tiles with names and a search over them, and under "This room's images" the room's `token` assets and the images already on tokens and characters in view. A tap on the map with no stamp chosen opens it once that tap's click has passed (or after 500 ms), so on a phone the click never lands on a tile.
    - A tap places the chosen stamp centered on the tap, snapped to half cells, at its catalog footprint for shipped art, or one cell on its long side for a room image. The new stamp is selected.
    - A selected stamp has a rotate handle (a 44 px target) that turns in 15° steps, and a corner handle that resizes it keeping its shape, from 0.25 to 20 cells. The corner handle stands 16 px out along both sides, so its 22 px reach never touches the stamp, and a press inside the selected stamp's box always moves it: on a phone a barrel is a few pixels across. One Snap switch, on by default, turns off both the 15° steps and the half-cell snap of a drag, and Stamp mode's hint says what it does. A drag moves a stamp.
    - The options bar offers a tint (None and 8 swatches, multiplied into the art as a cast), Flip, Bring to front, Duplicate (half a cell right and down), "DM only", Delete (red, a second tap to confirm, "Delete?" in the phone's two-row bar) and Done.
    - Below the wide layout (900 px), where the bar spans the screen, a selected stamp's bar keeps to two rows over the map. "Tint" opens a small sheet with the swatches, "More" opens one with Flip, Bring to front, Duplicate and "DM only", the stamp to place shows only its picture, and Clear build and the hint come back with Done. Each sheet is a modal dialog with a light dim, so the stamp stays in view as each choice applies.
    - Each gesture and each bar button is one `build.stamps` op, so the builder's Undo and Redo take back one at a time.
  - Label: type and tap to place. A drag on a label moves it. A tap on a label's text, in Label mode or with the Select tool, outlines it and opens the DM's label pane (text, size, Delete with a second tap, Done), which applies as one `build.label` op on Done. A tap beside a label places a new one.
  - Undo and redo buttons in the builder's bar.
  - Clear build removes every floor, wall, door, height, stair, label, light and stamp on the scene, free walls and border edges included, and lands the tokens that stood on raised ground at ground level.
  - Adjacent rooms merge, because they are just cells.
- **Ops:** brush strokes send one `build.paint` op per 150 ms of stroke with the cells added since the last one. A preview shows locally in the meantime. A finger's stroke sends nothing for its first 300 ms, so a pinch whose second finger lands late drops the stroke whole instead of leaving a stray cell. Every build op changes at most 2,000 cells. The ops of one stroke, or of one room sent in bands, share an undo group, so one undo takes the whole gesture back (section 7.7).
- **Rendering:**
  - Cells merge into one path per terrain, filled with small tiling textures from `public/textures/`. The textures are openly licensed and credited (section 20). So a large dungeon is about a dozen draw calls.
  - Walls draw as one path of thick near-black lines with a pale rim, so a wall shows on the floor and on a dark scene background alike. Doors draw as standard symbols, and an open door swings into the floor side of its edge, never into rock. Labels draw as text, sized in cells by the DM, so they scale with the map like its art, with none of the floor or ceiling that token labels have (section 14.2).
  - Free walls (v1 wave 3) draw from the stored walls in the same pass and colors as the edge walls, with round caps. Doors and windows on them draw their usual symbols turned to the segment.
  - Stamps (v1 wave 3) draw after the floor and before the walls, doors, windows and labels, so players see them as part of the map, below walls, labels and tokens. Each distinct art, tint and size bucket (the next power of two of the on-screen long side) is drawn once into an offscreen canvas and reused, at most 300 copies kept, so 500 stamps cost 500 image draws on a redraw. A key the client does not know, or an image that fails to load, draws as a dashed box the size of the stamp. On a DM's board a DM-only stamp draws at half opacity inside that dashed outline, as hidden tokens are dimmed. The map export and thumbnails draw it as it is.
  - Automatic walls draw by the scene's rule.
  - Raised ground (v1 wave 4) draws over the floor and the map image, below stamps and walls. Higher cells are lighter and lower ones darker, 4% per 5 ft and at most 20%. Each raised region casts a soft shadow down and to the right onto lower ground, 0.08 cell per 5 ft of difference and at most 0.5, in three fading bands, each lower neighbor by its own difference, with a pale rim on its north and west edges and a dark line on its south and east ones. Edges a directed stair joins draw neither. A directed stair draws five treads across its up direction, lighter toward the top, with risers and a chevron at the high end, on a pale slab over floor, and translucent with a pale halo over a map image. A stair with no direction draws as before.
  - The DM also sees lock marks, an "S" on secret doors (free ones with a dashed outline), and a dashed mark on each "no wall" edge.
  - The layer re-renders only when the build or the camera changes. It is never bitmap-cached, for the same canvas-size reason as the map.
- **Export as image (v1):** the scene's settings draw the background, the map image, an optional grid, the build and, since v1 wave 3, the stamps on an offscreen canvas, with the board layer's own drawing code, and save it as a PNG. Nothing from the token or overlay layers is drawn, and lights never are. The player view holds exactly what a player's projection holds for the scene (section 7.3), without the fog: hidden secret doors, free ones included, are drawn as the walls players see under the scene's automatic walls rule, and DM-only stamps and stamps fully under the fog are left out, with a count of them. Since v1 wave 4 the export draws raised ground and stairs, and the player view judges labels and stamps by the fog players get, the DM's layer and the explored layer merged (section 13.6). The DM view holds everything. The scale is lowered to keep the canvas within 16,777,216 pixels, the iOS Safari limit. The file is named like `crypt-30x20-70px.png`, which section 11.1's upload reads back as the scale. Phones save it through the share sheet, other devices through a download link.
- **Players** see walls, doors, terrain, stamps, and since v1 wave 4 raised ground and stairs. Undiscovered secret doors arrive as the walls players see there, and locked doors as closed (section 7.3). They never receive lights.
- **Uses:** walls clip areas of effect in the MVP, and since v1 wave 3 free walls clip them too. Since v1 wave 4 movement blocking (section 9.8) and line of sight (section 9.10) read the same derived wall set (section 9.7), with its raised edges. A Universal VTT import (v1 wave 3, section 14.9) writes its walls and doors into the edges where they align with cell edges, and as free walls stored in the chunks everywhere else.

## 13. Fog of war

### 13.1 Representation

- Fog is a bitmap with 4x4 sub-cells per grid cell (1.25 ft resolution). 1 means revealed, 0 means fogged.
- It is stored in 16x16-cell tiles of 4,096 bits each (512 bytes raw), sent as base64. A tile that is entirely fogged is simply absent.
- The bitmap is the fog. There is no list of shapes to compact and no claim of approximation to prove. A 200x200-cell scene at its worst is 13 x 13 = 169 tiles, about 115 KB as base64.
- Fog ops rasterize their shape into the bitmap in the shared reducer, with integer math, so the server and every client get identical bits. A sub-cell flips when its center is inside the shape.

```ts
type FogOp = { id, mode: 'reveal' | 'hide', shape: FogShape }
type FogShape =
  | { kind: 'rect', x, y, w, h }
  | { kind: 'poly', points: number[] }                    // at most 200 vertices
  | { kind: 'stroke', points: number[], width: number }   // brush, simplified, at most 500 points
  | { kind: 'cells', cells: string[] }                    // "reveal room" from the builder, at most 2,000
  | { kind: 'all' }
```

### 13.2 DM workflow

- By default fog edits go to the scene's draft, which is DM only.
- "Reveal" publishes the draft: the reducer rasterizes every draft op into the committed tiles in order, then empties the draft.
- "Live mode" makes each edit publish at once.
- "Reveal room" takes the room under the tap from the builder cells (a flood fill bounded by walls).
- New scenes: fog starts on and fully fogged for scenes with a map or a build, and off for a blank battle mat. Moving players to a scene with fog off shows the DM a one-line warning first.
- A blank battle mat that gets its first map image has become a map scene, so its fog turns on with the image, fully fogged, as if the scene had been made from it. A scene that already had a map or a build keeps the fog the DM chose.
- Adding or replacing the map image of the scene the players are on asks first, because they see it the moment it uploads. That step offers to start a first image under fog, checked by default. The fog goes out before the image, so players never see the map uncovered.
- The publish button names what the draft does to the players' view: "Reveal to players" for reveals, "Fog for players" for hides, "Show players the changes" for both. Brush widths read in feet at the room's scale.

### 13.3 Rendering and fail-closed gating

- The client draws the committed bitmap into an offscreen canvas at 4 pixels per cell, and Konva draws that canvas as one image node, stretched to the scene with nearest-neighbor scaling. Every pixel of a fogged sub-cell is therefore fully opaque. A soft edge is drawn only on the revealed side: revealed pixels next to fog are darkened slightly, so the fog never turns partly transparent. Redrawing it costs one image draw, so it shares the overlay layer with rulers and pings at no real cost.
- Players see opaque fog. The DM sees committed fog at 50% opacity, with the draft outlined.
- Stacking on the overlay layer, bottom to top: other creatures' drag previews, the party's sight image on a vision scene (v1 wave 4, section 14.12), the fog image, tokens whose projection says `aboveFog` (party tokens and exempt tokens, drawn here so the party always sees each other), then rulers, pings, the laser and AoE previews.
- **The gate, which fails closed:**
  - A player client renders nothing from a scene (map, build, tokens, drawings, templates) until that scene's snapshot has been applied.
  - If `fogEnabled` is true, or the scene's fog fields are missing or invalid, the gate treats the scene as enabled. Any tile that is missing or fails to decode counts as fully fogged.
  - If `view.blackout` is on in the snapshot, the player sees black before the first draw. During a blackout a player's whole room is the pause screen: no map, panels, initiative strip, handouts or toasts, and toasts that arrive meanwhile are dropped. Only the connection banner stays, and since v1 wave 5 the X-card, which a player there can also send (section 14.16). The DM's room is unchanged.
  - The pause screen is not the only guard: the server sends players none of the table while the blackout lasts (section 7.3), so there is nothing behind the black to find.
  - The map image request starts only after the snapshot is applied, so it cannot paint before the fog.

### 13.4 Tokens under fog

On a scene with `fogEnabled` true, a token is withheld from player projections like a hidden token when all of these hold: no player owns it, `fogExempt` is not set, and its occupied cells are all fully fogged in the committed bitmap (every sub-cell 0). Revealing the area brings it back, and a monster that walks into the fog disappears for players. Its attached templates and initiative entry follow it (section 7.3). On a scene with fog off, nothing is withheld for fog.

Labels follow the same rule. A label stores only its center, size and text, so its box is estimated as an erase estimates it (`rules/labels.ts`): about 0.6 of its size wide per character, plus a thin halo, and its size tall. The fog and an erase therefore agree on which cells a label covers. Only the builder's tap and outline measure the text on a canvas instead, so a tap picks exactly the label drawn under the finger. A label whose box is fully fogged is withheld from players, and revealing any cell of it brings it back. Stamps (v1 wave 3) follow the same rule over the cells under their rotated bounding box. Fog changes are global, so labels and stamps are re-checked with the tokens.

- The check reads at most 16 sub-cells per occupied cell, so it is cheap.
- Fog changes and `fogEnabled` changes set the `global` flag, so every token in the scene is re-checked.
- Tokens owned by players are never withheld, and they draw above the fog (13.3), so the party always sees each other. Since v1 wave 4 so are the tokens the DM marked `partyVision`.
- `fogExempt` is set by the DM. `fogExemptAuto` is set when a hidden combatant is revealed on its turn and cleared when its fight ends (section 9.4). It lifted the creature above the fog until the project owner decided on 2026-10-14 that a revealed creature follows the normal fog and sight rules. The reducer still writes it, so stored ops replay to the state they made, and only the seen marks of ops stored before op version 4 read it (section 16.2).
- Since v1 wave 4 the fog these rules read is the fog players get, the DM's layer and the explored layer merged, on every scene, one whose vision was turned off after it was explored included. On a scene with vision on, a token follows sight instead (section 13.6).

### 13.5 Trust

Players download the whole map image. A player who opens devtools can see unrevealed parts of the map art. The project owner accepted this. Hidden tokens, tokens, labels and stamps under fog, DM-only stamps, lights, the map's other phases, DM notes and the fog draft are never sent, so the normal UI, a reload and devtools all keep those secret. Since v1 wave 4 neither are creatures out of the party's sight, other players' token lights, the DM's own fog layer apart from the explored ground, or the teleport lines: players get only sight's result, which the server alone works out. Since v1 wave 5 neither are journal pages not shared with them, unrevealed secret blocks, pins that are DM only, under the fog or linked to a page they cannot read, other players' roll targets, a hidden DC or result, or the room monster a token came from. A table display holds only what a player who owns nothing holds. During a blackout players are sent none of the table at all, the map included (section 7.3).

### 13.6 Party vision and explored ground (v1 wave 4)

Addendum 4.3.1 and 4.3.3 have the reasoning. Section 9.10 has the rules of sight.

- **The setting.** A scene's Vision settings (section 14.12) turn party vision on: "Players see only what their characters see". Turning it on turns the scene's fog on too, unless the same patch names `fogEnabled`, since only fog remembers explored ground. With fog off on a vision scene nothing is remembered: players see the whole map dimmed and only the party's sight clear. The scene's light level is Bright, Dim or Dark, and a map phase may carry one, which switching to it writes into `vision.light`, keeping `on` (section 14.11). So does the phase list making a phase the showing one, and a level set on the showing phase applies at once.
- **Two fog layers.** The DM's fog stays exactly as it was: the committed tiles that the DM's fog ops write and the DM's undo puts back. What the party sees goes into a second layer, `exploredTiles`, in the fog tile's shape, which only the server writes, by the explore step's `sys.explore` (section 7.2). After every batch that holds ops, on the player scene only, while vision and fog are on and no blackout is, every sub-cell the party now sees that the explored layer lacks is added, ground the DM's layer already reveals included. So a DM previewing another vision scene explores nothing, and nothing is explored behind a blackout. `sys.explore` only adds bits, and the reducer refuses a tile outside the scene.
- **Players get one fog**, each tile the two layers merged (section 7.3). So the fail-closed gate, labels and stamps under fog, the map export's player view and Fit map all work as before on the merged fog. A DM receives both layers, and the DM's fog image draws explored ground like revealed ground, with a dotted line around ground only the party explored (section 14.12).
- **Undo keeps what was explored** (the project owner, 2026-10-14). A DM's fog undo puts back the DM's layer, guarded as before. Explore never writes that layer, so it never changes what a fog step's guard expects, and anything the party explored after the edit stays explored: the DM reveals a room, the party walks through it and leaves, the DM undoes the reveal, and players keep the room.
- **Hides and resets clear exploration.** A hide also clears the explored bits under its shapes, so players forget the area, except where the party is looking now, which the same batch explores again. `fog.reset` to hidden clears the scene's whole explored layer. Those clears happen outside any undo step and set `global`, so every token, label and stamp is projected again, room-wide. Undoing a hide or a reset brings back the DM's fog, not the exploration it cleared (the owner's decision in the addendum's round 6). On vision scenes the fog tool's hint says "Undo brings back your fog, not what the party explored. Reveal it again by hand."
- **Tokens on a vision scene follow sight.** A creature reaches players only while the party's sight covers its footprint (section 9.10), or when it is a party member or the DM exempted it (`fogExempt`). A creature revealed on its turn gets no exception (section 9.4). Outside the party's sight, explored ground stays drawn, dimmed, with no creature on it, and never-explored ground stays under opaque fog. Initiative rows follow their creatures, as rows under fog do, the addendum's recommendation for question 14, which the project owner kept on 2026-10-14.
- **One rule for every "seen" mark.** `seenByNonOwners`, `inSightOfNonOwners` and `rowSeenByNonOwners` use sight on vision scenes, after the blackout check, so log lines, a turn's starting spot and path stops are marked by what the party could see then. On the server sight is worked out. On a client the reducer reads the tiles the server sent, so its optimistic marks can be wrong for one batch, and the server's replace them.
- **Doors on explored ground** show their live state, as all map knowledge does (addendum question 16, decided by the project owner on 2026-10-14): a door a creature out of sight opens swings open on players' screens, in the dimmed ground they explored.
- **Scenes.** Explored tiles belong to their scene. Deleting a scene removes both layers, and a restore that removes a scene removes its explored tiles as bookkeeping, never counting them as work that depends on it.

## 14. Client

### 14.1 Structure

- `main.tsx` mounts `App`. A tiny router: `/` is Home (create a room, join by ID, or enter a DM link or invite), `/r/:roomId` is Join or Room. React Router is not needed for two routes. Since v1 wave 5 there are three more: `/display`, where a TV enters a table display code, `/display/<room id>`, the display itself (section 14.14), and `/admin` (section 15.8), a lazy chunk a room never loads.
- **Home remembers this device's rooms** (`lib/knownRooms.ts`). Entering a room records its ID, name and who this device is there in local storage, at most 8, newest first. Home lists them and checks each with `GET /me` when it opens: signed in (with the role and member name), signed out (the room page then offers the join page, in DM words for a former DM device), or gone (`/me` refused and `GET /api/rooms/:id` answering 404), which leaves the list. Offline, the rooms are offered unchecked. The list grants nothing: the session cookie does. The installed app's `start_url` stays `/`, and "Leave room" goes there, so a player never retypes a room ID. "Forget" removes a room from the list. After this device deletes a room, Home says so once. The names shown are the rooms' names as created, which a rename in room settings does not change.
- `state/store.ts` is a vanilla Zustand store with `confirmed`, `pending`, `view`, `ephemeral` (drags, rulers, previews, pings by member) and `local` (camera, selected tool, selection, panels). The socket module writes into it outside React. Components subscribe with narrow selectors such as `s => s.view.tokens[id]`.
- `state/socket.ts` implements section 7.5.
- Panels and heavy tools (builder, image picker, monster browser with its custom monster editor, fog tools) load lazily, so a player's first load stays small. Once the room is connected, the panels and tool options the role may use load in the background (`lib/retryableLazy.ts`), so one opened later offline, or after a redeploy removed the old chunks, still opens. A DM-only module never loads for a player. The DM's height marks and explored outline (v1 wave 4) load the same way and draw nothing until they arrive (`lib/quietLazy.tsx`), which also puts Konva's core in a chunk of its own, so no chunk passes the build's 600 kB warning. Since v1 wave 5 a room and a table display share the canvas and most of the room's code, which the bundler moves out of `LiveRoom` into a shared chunk, named `room` in `vite.config.ts` so the build's size report reads the same from one build to the next. Opening a room preloads it and the rest of the route's chunks at once, so the split adds no round trip. A canvas layer that failed to load draws nothing where it stands, since a notice there would cover the map, and reports to one notice over the map instead (`pages/QuietLoadNotice.tsx`) with a panel's Retry and its reload that waits for queued edits. The note after a stopped move, the door pill and the token bar's wave 4 parts are in the room's chunk, since a player cannot do without them.
- Every lazy part sits inside an error boundary (`components/LoadBoundary.tsx`), so a load that fails shows a notice in that panel, tool bar or page, and the socket, the queue and the rest of the room keep running. The page itself tells the cases apart (`lib/chunkLoad.ts`): offline says the panel cannot open while offline, with Retry, and retries when the network returns. A fresh copy of the page that loads other scripts, or a missing chunk, means the site was updated, and offers a reload. Chrome remembers a failed module for the life of the page, so a retry that fails online offers a reload too.
- A reload the app offers never drops edits. The queue lives only in this page's memory (7.4), and persisting it would need the `clientId` and its numbering to outlive the page, which a duplicated tab would then share. So the reload waits until `pending` is empty and the connection is up, saying so, and then opens the same panel or tool again. "Reload now and lose them" is there for a server that never answers.
- **Anything slow says so.** Every wait that can pass about 300 ms on a slow phone or network shows that it is working, then the result or a plain error. There is one way to show it (`components/Loading.tsx`): a spinner and a short sentence, shown only once the wait has lasted 200 ms, so a quick answer never flashes it. The spinner turns by a CSS animation, which runs on the compositor and keeps turning while the page is busy, and holds still for reduced motion. Nothing animates while nothing loads. A button that starts slow work says so in its label at once ("Uploading…") and ignores a second tap until it is answered. Before the app's script arrives, the page itself says "Loading RaccoonVTT…".

### 14.2 Canvas

At most four Konva layers, each a canvas plus a hit canvas:

1. `map`: background, map image, grid lines. One `sceneFunc` shape draws the grid. `listening(false)`.
2. `board`: builder, drawings, area templates and their coverage shading.
3. `tokens`: tokens, condition chips, elevation badges, HP bars, turn marker, selection, and since v1 wave 4 the shadows of tokens off the ground (section 14.13).
4. `overlay`: in stacking order, other creatures' drag previews, the party's sight on a vision scene (v1 wave 4), the fog image, party and `fogExempt` tokens, rulers, pings, laser, AoE previews (section 13.3).

- `Konva.pixelRatio = Math.min(devicePixelRatio, 2)`.
- No layer redraws unless its inputs changed. There is no animation loop at rest. Animations (pings, remote drag smoothing, turn marker pulse) run a `requestAnimationFrame` loop only while one is active, then stop.
- Images are decoded before Konva draws them. Token images use `img.decode()`. A map is decoded into an `ImageBitmap` (`createImageBitmap`) off the main thread, because Chrome decodes an `<img>` again, on the main thread, the first time a canvas draws it: measured at 470 ms for an 8000 px map on a laptop and 490 ms for the 4096 px phone copy at a phone's speed, with the page frozen, against 55 to 115 ms for a bitmap. A bitmap is closed once nothing draws it. A browser without `createImageBitmap` keeps the `<img>`. The map is never `cache()`d, because a cached 8192 px map exceeds iOS canvas limits.
- **Board text and marks scale with the map** (the project owner, 2026-10-14, `lib/boardScale.ts`). A token's ring, outlines, condition chips, elevation badge, padlock, HP bar and label, and the board's area names, range labels, ping and laser names, area previews and Height mode's pills and stair marks (v1 design pass), are sized in cells, pinned to their old fixed size at 40 px a cell, so they grow and shrink with the tokens. Each text element is clamped on its own: never under 11 px (names, HP and state words, chip letters and counters, badges), and never over 16 px for names and 15 px for HP. A chip holding letters or a counter grows to fit them at the floor. A chip showing only an icon shrinks to 12 px. Strokes keep a floor of 1.5 px, and the selection and turn outlines never get thinner than their old 2 and 3 px. Font sizes round to half pixels and text widths are cached by that exact size, so placement measures what is drawn. Stay a fixed size on screen: the readouts of a ruler or a move in progress (ruler segments and totals, the drag distance, the move marker), pings' rings (which grow with a close zoom up to twice their size but never shrink), touch targets, handles and the bars over the map. Map labels and drawn text are map content, sized by their author in cells with no floor or ceiling. Height mode's pills draw under the token labels, chips and plates, and a pill with no spot clear of every placed label at this zoom is left out until a zoom gives it room (`features/builder/heights/HeightLayer.tsx`, docs/design-v1-visual.md 3.4).
- **Token labels** (`lib/boardText.ts`, `canvas/useBoardText.ts`) are placed in one pass at every zoom, most important first: the selected token, the acting creature, PCs and the viewer's own tokens, then the rest, and within a rank a creature that is Bloodied or Dying, then the bigger creature, then the shorter label, then the token id. A label never covers another token's body (tiny tokens included), chips, badge or HP bar, the move marker, an area's name, a label the DM wrote on the map (which keeps its place) or a label placed before it. It tries below its token, then above (the acting creature also tries its sides). Ordinary HP gives way first, then the name. A Bloodied or Dying creature keeps its word with a cut name, then the word alone, before its name alone. A name is whole on one line, whole on two balanced lines (a word wider than a line breaks with a hyphen after a letter only when the whole name then fits, and a duplicate's number is never broken: it rides with the last word or moves to the next line whole), or cut once on one line keeping its duplicate number and at least four letters ("Kobold… 4"), never a shorter fragment. A label keeps the side it had on the last layout while that side is free, so a pinch does not shuffle labels. Below 14 px on screen a token has no chips, badge or HP bar, and only the acting creature and the viewer's own tokens try for their names.
- What a label gives up stays one tap away. A picture token whose name gave way shows its duplicate number in a badge in its bottom right corner (a body without a picture already shows its initials with the number). A creature this viewer may know is Bloodied, from the word, the bar or the numbers `hpDisplay` gives them, has an amber notch cut into the right of its ring, a shape as well as a color, shown at every zoom and in proportion on small tokens (left out only on one under 8 px), and never on a token whose HP is hidden from the viewer. Hovering a token on a desktop, or selecting one with no action bar open, shows its whole label on a dark plate over everything, at 14 px or more. A hover places nothing again, so labels under a crowd hold still while the mouse moves over it (the project owner, 2026-10-14). The action bar's header wraps the whole name and is the guaranteed full reveal. A bar too tall for above or below its token moves beside it when the screen is wide enough, and otherwise shows "Scroll for more" while anything is out of view.

### 14.3 Gestures

- Pointer Events only, with `touch-action: none`, `overscroll-behavior: none` and `-webkit-touch-callout: none` on the stage. The page itself never zooms while a room is open: the room adds `user-scalable=no` to the viewport (Android), prevents `gesturestart` and `gesturechange` (iOS), and its root has `touch-action: pan-x pan-y`. Home and the join page still zoom. A handout has its own zoom: pinch, double tap, double click or the wheel, with Reset.
- One pointer goes to the current tool. Two pointers pan and pinch-zoom: track pointers in a map, keep a baseline distance and midpoint, re-baseline when going from two pointers to one, and call `stopDrag()` on any token drag when a second finger lands. Three or more pointers are ignored.
- Tap selects. Tapping a token opens its action bar. Double-tap gives alternates: keep a ruler, edit text, select a locked item. Long-press is never required, because iOS Safari does not fire `contextmenu`. It is offered as a shortcut (500 ms, cancelled by 8 px of movement). Desktop right-click opens the same menu.
- Konva `dragDistance` is 8 px on touch, so a tap never nudges a token.
- Mouse wheel zooms around the cursor. A drag with the middle or right button, or the left with Space held, pans with any tool, so a desktop can move the map while building or drawing. Space counts only while nothing has focus, or the map's own focus target does, so it never takes a key press from a field or a button. A right-click that does not move still opens a token's menu. Ctrl+Z or Cmd+Z undoes and Ctrl+Shift+Z or Cmd+Shift+Z redoes (section 7.7). Other keyboard shortcuts came in v1 wave 5 (section 14.17).
- A scene without a saved view opens fitted to the part of the canvas the top bar, the toolbar and the tool's options leave clear. While the camera is still that fit, it follows the canvas size and moves clear of a bar that grows over the scene, such as a tool's options wrapping onto another row. A bar that shrinks leaves it be.
- A scene zooms out only until it fills half the screen (never below 4 px per cell), and a pan or zoom always leaves at least 64 px of the scene on screen in the part the bars leave clear. "Fit map" in the top bar fits the scene to that same clear part, and its fit follows the bars the same way. For a player under fog it fits what is revealed and their own tokens instead.
- Escape closes a phone's sheet, then the token action bar. Android's Back (and the browser's) closes an open sheet or handout, and on a phone the token action bar, before it leaves the room: while one is open, one history entry for the same URL sits on top (`lib/backClose.ts`), and leaving the room from a sheet replaces that entry. Each Back closes one thing: handouts shown one after another take one Back each, and a sheet under a handout takes the next.

### 14.4 Layout

- **Phone portrait:** full-screen map. A top bar holds the room name, connection dot, a chip (DM, or the player's name with a dot and border in their color) and Fit map, for a DM on a phone or a tablet Undo and Redo, and for a player Find my token. During a fight the initiative strip is a band of its own under the bar (v1): the acting creature's picture and name large, its turn timer, and for the DM the next few small. Players see only who acts, or "Another creature's turn". On a player's own turn the band lights up, reads "Your turn" under the name and holds End Turn at its end. Sheets, toasts and the token action bar keep clear of the band. From 900 px up the strip sits in the top bar as an outlined chip, lit on the player's own turn, with End Turn beside it. Below 1100 px the player's chip there shows only its color dot, named in its label and tooltip, so the room name keeps its room. Nothing about the turn covers the map. A bottom toolbar holds tools, each icon with its short name under it, and a chevron at the edge that hides more scrolls to them. Panels open as bottom sheets: token, tracker, scenes, library, log. The Log's button (the Panels button on phones, the Log tab from 900 px up) shows a count of unread chat and whispers, and a whisper to you also shows a toast while the Log is closed.
- A tool's options wrap onto more rows instead of scrolling sideways, up to half the screen, past which the bar scrolls.
- **Find my token** (`features/find/findToken.ts`): each press finds the next token the player owns on the scene, directly or through a character, after the one found last: player characters first, then other creatures, then objects, each in the order they were made. The camera zooms so the token and 4 cells around it fit between the bars, and centers the token in the part of the map an open sheet leaves in view, measured when pressed, at that same zoom. It is a plain camera rather than a fit, so the bars changing never move it back. With no token there, a toast says "You have no token on this scene." The DM has no such button: the DM's phone top bar is full, and tracker rows and following the turn already bring creatures into view.
- **Tab title** (`lib/title.ts`): `RaccoonVTT · <room name>` while a room is open, following a rename, and `RaccoonVTT` on every other page. "View as a player" leaves it to the DM's page, since two pages setting it would race.
- **Phone landscape:** the same, with sheets docked on the right.
- **Tablet and desktop** (width at least 900 px): a docked side panel with tabs, and the map beside it.
- **Since v1 wave 5** the Menu panel holds the X-card at its top and This device beside Members, Room settings and About. The DM's party bar sits atop the side panel, above the tabs, from 900 px up, and is the Party panel on a phone. The Journal button counts unread pages as the Log's counts chat, and on a phone the Panels button adds the two. The DM also gets the Outliner panel and the Pin tool.
- **Since v1 wave 6** every sheet that rises from a phone's foot closes with a swipe down, and the panel sheet fills the screen with a swipe up (docs/design-v1-sheet-swipe.md).
- Respects `env(safe-area-inset-*)`, uses `100dvh`, and uses 16 px minimum input font size so iOS does not zoom. Touch targets are at least 44 px.
- Inline styles with tokens from `client/lib/theme.ts`, per the project's convention. `globals.css` carries only the base document, the focus rings, `::selection`, reduced motion and the few other rules inline styles cannot express. The focus rings follow the input modality (`lib/inputModality.ts`, docs/design-v1-visual.md 2.1): they show after keys, and after a tap none shows except on a text field, which keeps its focus look. A key press brings them back.
- **Destructive buttons** (the project owner, 2026-10-13): every button that deletes, removes, clears, discards, ends or signs out (delete a scene, token, character, custom monster, label or area, remove an image or one of a token's other images, remove a kept ruler, clear a token's range rings, kick, remove, sign out a device, replace the DM link, change the password, clear the build or drawings, discard a fog draft, fog or reveal all, end the fight, remove a row from it, close a handout, forget a room on Home, delete the room) shows red text on the usual neutral border and background at rest: `Button`'s `danger` variant, or `destructiveStyle()` (`components/Button.tsx`) spread into a feature's own button. The text takes `theme.color.dangerText`, a lighter red than `danger`, which reads at under 4.5:1 on a raised surface. The red outline and a faint red tint come only with the armed state of a button that asks for a second tap ("Tap again to delete", a confirm box's confirm button, the room's delete once its name is typed, other images' Remove one while a tap on an image removes it), so the tap that acts looks unlike the tap that asks. Focus rings and 44 px targets are unchanged.
- Dark theme by default. In a room the accent (filled buttons, chosen options, the focus ring, the selected tool) is the viewing member's own color (the project owner, 2026-10-14), with dark or light text on it by its luminance (`lib/accent.ts`). A color that would not read at 4.5:1 on the dark surfaces, or would pass for the cream turn highlight or for muted text, moves to the nearest OKLCH lightness that fits, keeping its hue and as much of its chroma as sRGB holds. Before a room is joined, and for a member with no color, it is the crest yellow `#f7df00`, also a new room's DM color. The old DM default `#f38400`, which rooms made before stored for their DM and which nobody picked, also reads as the crest yellow for the accent, with the stored color unchanged. Colors that mean something never follow it: danger, warning, success, statuses, disposition rings, area and token colors, the ruler's speed bands, and the pale cream `#ffe8a3` that marks the active turn. The crest orange stays in the logo only.
- "Your turn" notices: when a player's turn arrives, "Your turn" in the strip flares as a filled pill for a few seconds, then settles into the strip's plain text for the rest of the turn. Android vibrates. iOS Safari has no Vibration API, so iPhones get the notice and a short sound when sound is on.
- There is no "Up next" notice for players. Their view lacks hidden rows and rows under fog (section 7.3), so a guess from it can be wrong. The server could say for sure, but then a missing notice, where the visible order says "you", would tell the player that something unseen acts first, a turn before it does.
- Token decorations (condition chips, the elevation badge, a padlock on a locked token the viewer could otherwise drag, the Bloodied notch, the number badge, the HP bar and the label) draw in passes of their own above every token body and the fog, so a token in the row below or the fog edge never covers a visible token's name: marks, then labels, then the hover or selection plate. A token being dragged on this device draws its own, so they travel with it. The badge moves past the chips on a token too small for both.
