  8. It rebuilds `backup_runs` from the completion records, finishes this run's row, deletes rows older than 90 days, and releases the lock. The rows are only a log for the admin page, and the dumps leave the table out, so a restored older dump can never make retention forget newer backups.
- **Deleted image files wait in a trash folder.** The dump and the image copy happen minutes apart, and the app's file locks cannot hold off another container. So the app never unlinks an image file at once: every deletion (a room's, cleanup's, the library's) moves the file into `trash/<UTC day>/` on the image volume, and the daily cleanup job deletes a day's folder 48 hours after that day ends. A file a dump names is therefore in place or in the trash while the copy runs.
- **Restore runs as the app's user.** The `restore` service, behind the `restore` profile, is the same image as UID 1000 (`node`) with no capabilities, `raccoon-assets` writable at `/data/assets` and the backups volume read-only, so every folder and file it creates is the app's. `backup.sh restore <dump name>` refuses, writing nothing, while the lock is held ("A backup is running. Wait for it to finish, then restore."), before the backup service has made its lock file, for a dump with no completion record, while any client is connected to the database, and when the image volume's root is not UID 1000's ("The image volume is not the app's yet. Start the stack once with docker compose up -d, then stop raccoon and restore."). It restores with `pg_restore --clean --if-exists --no-owner --single-transaction --exit-on-error`, copies the listed images without overwriting, checks each, and ends by saying to start raccoon, wait until it is healthy, then run `backup.sh once`, which rebuilds the log from the completion records.
- **The dumps** hold everything the database does but the backup log, the sealed player passwords included, which are useless without `ROOM_SECRET_KEY`. They are as private as the database volume and on the same disk, so README says how to copy `raccoon-backups` off the server (wave 5 question 7, which the project owner decided on 2026-10-14).
- **The check.** `pnpm test:backup` (`scripts/backup-check.ts`) runs the script in the pinned image through the development compose file's `backup` profile, under a compose project of its own with its own Postgres: a good run restored and compared, a deletion between the dump and the copy, restores of an older dump, a full volume, interrupted copies and dumps, overlapping runs and restores, a fresh database and a fresh image volume, and every list line against `variantFileName` and `variantPath`. It needs Docker, so it is a check before release.

### 15.8 Admin page (v1 wave 5)

docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 3.18, `server/http/admin.ts`, `server/auth/adminSessions.ts` and `client/pages/Admin.tsx`.

- **Turning it on.** The page exists only when the server has `ADMIN_PASSWORD_HASH`, an argon2id hash. Without it `/admin` shows a not-found page and every `/api/admin/*` route answers 404. `docker compose -f docker-compose.server.yml run --rm --no-deps raccoon node scripts/admin.ts hash-password` asks for the password twice without echoing it, refuses one under 16 characters or on the common-password list, can generate one, and prints the single-quoted line for `.env`. Compose passes the variable to `raccoon` only.
- **Login.** One password field, with the Turnstile check whenever Turnstile is configured. `POST /api/admin/login` verifies through the shared argon2 queue, under the limits in section 10.3. Success makes an admin session: 32 random bytes in a cookie of its own, `__Host-rvtt-admin` (`rvtt-admin` in development), `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict`, `Path=/`, with only its `sha256` in `admin_sessions`, a fixed 12 hours, and a fingerprint of the password hash it was made under, so a changed `ADMIN_PASSWORD_HASH` ends every admin session. Admin sessions are not room sessions and hold no grants. Every admin route checks the cookie, the CSRF rule and the request limit, and answers with `Cache-Control: no-store`.
- **The page,** "Server admin", a lazy chunk. Storage: the image volume's used and free space, the database's size, the images (each file once, counted from the rows), what waits for cleanup (unused thumbnails and the trash), and the last backup run and the last good one, with a warning when no good backup has run, the last run did not end well, or none was good in 36 hours. Rooms: name, ID, size (the quota's own sum), images, whether it is loaded and its sockets, pinned, read-only, last active and created, sorted by last activity or size. Actions: Pin or Unpin, which reaches a loaded room at once (`RoomManager.setPinned`), and Delete, which asks for the room ID typed to confirm and runs the DM's own delete path, so the room unloads, its sockets close with `4009`, and its files go when no other room names them. The page never shows a room's contents, DM link, player password or members, and cannot enter a room.
- **Logging.** Admin logins, failures, pins and deletions are logged with the address, which is the server owner's own information about their own server.

## 16. Persistence

### 16.1 Schema

```sql
create table if not exists rooms (
  id text primary key,                         -- Crockford base32, 8 chars
  name text not null,
  player_pw_hash text not null,                -- argon2id PHC string
  player_epoch int not null default 1,
  dm_key_hash bytea not null,                  -- sha256 of the DM key
  dm_epoch int not null default 1,
  created_ip text,
  created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  last_active_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  read_only boolean not null default false,
  pinned boolean not null default false         -- never unloaded to make space (admin pin)
);
-- AES-256-GCM of the player password under ROOM_SECRET_KEY (section 10.2).
-- Null for a room whose password was set before the column existed.
alter table rooms add column if not exists player_pw_sealed bytea;
create table if not exists room_snapshots (
  room_id text primary key references rooms on delete cascade,
  seq bigint not null,
  state jsonb not null,
  schema_version int not null,
  written_at timestamptz not null default now()
);
create table if not exists room_ops (
  room_id text not null references rooms on delete cascade,
  seq bigint not null,
  at timestamptz not null,                     -- the envelope time the reducer saw
  actor_member_id text not null,
  actor_role text not null,
  op jsonb not null,
  op_version int not null,
  primary key (room_id, seq)
);
create table if not exists client_watermarks (
  room_id text not null references rooms on delete cascade,
  session_id uuid not null,
  client_id text not null,
  n bigint not null,
  updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  primary key (room_id, session_id, client_id)
);
create table if not exists room_log (
  room_id text not null references rooms on delete cascade,
  id text not null,                            -- "<seq>:<index>", so replay inserts are idempotent
  at timestamptz not null,
  entry jsonb not null,
  primary key (room_id, id)
);
create table if not exists sessions (
  id uuid primary key,
  token_hash bytea not null unique,
  user_agent text,
  created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  last_seen_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  expires_at timestamptz not null
);
create table if not exists grants (
  session_id uuid not null references sessions on delete cascade,
  room_id text not null references rooms on delete cascade,
  member_id text not null,
  role text not null check (role in ('dm', 'player')),
  epoch int not null,
  created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  primary key (session_id, room_id)
);
create table if not exists dm_invites (
  token_hash bytea primary key,
  room_id text not null references rooms on delete cascade,
  created_by_session uuid not null,             -- revoking that DM device deletes its unused invites
  dm_epoch int not null,
  expires_at timestamptz not null,
  used_at timestamptz
);
create table if not exists assets (
  id uuid primary key,                         -- random UUIDv4, never time-ordered
  room_id text not null references rooms on delete cascade,
  sha256 bytea not null,
  kind text not null check (kind in ('map', 'token', 'handout')),
  pipeline int not null,
  width int not null, height int not null,     -- canonical (full variant) size
  bytes bigint not null,
  variants text[] not null,
  created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  unique (room_id, sha256, kind)
);
create index if not exists assets_file on assets (sha256, kind, pipeline);
create table if not exists asset_uploaders (
  asset_id uuid not null references assets on delete cascade,
  member_id text not null,
  primary key (asset_id, member_id)
);
create table if not exists search_cache (q text primary key, results jsonb not null, fetched_at timestamptz not null);
create table if not exists search_usage (bucket text primary key, count int not null);
  -- 'r:<room>:2026-10', 'ip:<ip>:2026-10-12'; the global count comes from SerpApi's Account API
create table if not exists rate_events (key text not null, at timestamptz not null);
create index if not exists rate_events_key_at on rate_events (key, at);
-- Scene thumbnails (v1 wave 3) are assets of their own kind. The kind check is replaced here, only while
-- it lacks the new kind, since an existing database never re-runs a create. It accepts every older row.
do $$
begin
  if not exists (
    select 1 from pg_constraint
    where conrelid = 'assets'::regclass and conname = 'assets_kind_check' and pg_get_constraintdef(oid) like '%''thumb''%'
  ) then
    alter table assets drop constraint if exists assets_kind_check,
      add constraint assets_kind_check check (kind in ('map', 'token', 'handout', 'thumb'));
  end if;
end $$;
-- v1 wave 5 (docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 2.3), each step idempotent as the thumb kind's was.
-- A session's purpose: 'display' for a table display's (section 10.2), 'room' for every other row.
alter table sessions add column if not exists purpose text not null default 'room' check (purpose in ('room', 'display'));
-- grants.role gains 'display', replaced as assets_kind_check is above, only while it lacks it.
create table if not exists display_codes (
  token_hash bytea primary key,                -- sha256 of 10 Crockford base32 characters
  room_id text not null references rooms on delete cascade,
  created_by_session uuid not null,            -- the DM device that minted it
  player_epoch int not null,                   -- a password change voids it
  expires_at timestamptz not null,
  used_at timestamptz
);
create index if not exists display_codes_room on display_codes (room_id, expires_at);
create table if not exists admin_sessions (    -- section 15.8: no grants, 12 hours, never extended
  token_hash bytea primary key,
  password_fingerprint bytea not null,         -- a changed ADMIN_PASSWORD_HASH ends every admin session
  created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  expires_at timestamptz not null
);
create table if not exists backup_runs (       -- section 15.7: a log only, rebuilt from the completion records
  id text primary key,                         -- the run id in its file names
  started_at timestamptz not null,
  finished_at timestamptz,
  outcome text not null check (outcome in ('running', 'good', 'partial', 'failed', 'gone')),
  dump text, dump_bytes bigint, images_copied int, error text
);
create index if not exists backup_runs_started on backup_runs (started_at);
alter table assets add column if not exists unused_since timestamptz;   -- section 16.3: a thumbnail no longer named
alter table assets add column if not exists credit jsonb;               -- section 11.3: an Openverse image's credit
create table if not exists roll_receipts (     -- v1 wave 7, docs/design-v1-dice.md 1.2: the outcome of every admitted op that rolls or applies
  room_id text not null references rooms on delete cascade,
  member_id text not null,
  tag text not null,                           -- the op's client id, part a group's batch
  part int not null,
  state text not null check (state in ('committed', 'refused')),
  seq bigint,                                  -- a committed op's seq, which names its log lines
  code text, message text,                     -- a refusal's words
  at timestamptz not null,
  primary key (room_id, member_id, tag, part)
);
create index if not exists roll_receipts_room_at on roll_receipts (room_id, at);
```

### 16.2 Loading, snapshots and replay

- **Load a room:** read `room_snapshots`, run its migrations, then replay `room_ops` with a higher `seq` through `applyOp` using each row's stored envelope (`seq`, `at`, actor). A replay that fails validation stops loading, logs loudly, and serves the room read-only rather than guessing.
- **The manager loads each room once**, behind a promise, so concurrent connects share one load. Unloading waits until the room has no sockets and no batch in flight. A connect that arrives during an unload waits for it to finish, then loads again.
- **Snapshots** contain only committed state: they are built from the committed state, after a batch commits, with that batch's last `seq`. They are written after 200 ops or 30 s after the last change, and always on unload and shutdown.
- **Pruning:** after a snapshot commits, ops at or below its `seq` are deleted when they are also older than one hour. Watermark rows unused for 7 days are deleted daily. Since v1 wave 7, also daily, roll receipts older than 7 days, and of those older than 24 hours all but each room's newest 10,000. A receipt younger than 24 hours is never deleted, and the room actor loads those with the room, so a restart keeps the duplicate rule.
- **Versioning:** `schema_version` on snapshots and `op_version` on ops. Schema version 2 added `monsters`: a version 1 snapshot or backup gets an empty collection. Schema version 3 added `srdImages` the same way. Schema version 4 turned the switch `playersMoveAnyToken` into `playersMoveAny`: on became `'all'`, off `'none'`. Op version 2 does the same to the settings of a stored `room.update`, and to the room meta a stored `sys.restore` puts back. Such a restore also loses its guard on the meta, a hash of the meta's older shape that no replayed state can match: it matched when the restore was applied, or it would not be stored. Version 1 ops also ran before turns recorded where they began (`Encounter.turnStart`). Replayed now, a turn advanced, a removal that passes the turn on, or a turn taken back leaves the encounter and its history holding a spot they did not hold then, so a stored restore of either loses its guard the same way. Op version 3 came with paths (section 9.2): a version 2 move of the active creature now leaves its token holding a path it did not hold then, a move of any other token drops one, and a turn advanced, passed on or taken back leaves the encounter and its history holding a turn's `seq`. So a stored restore of a token, the encounter or its history loses its guard the same way, and puts back what it put back then. Schema version 5 added `savedEncounters`, empty in older snapshots and backups. A character's `level` came with it as an optional field, absent in older rooms, so it needed no step of its own. The HP log's `left` and `revert` and an area's reminders (v1 wave 2) change only the log lines an older op writes on replay, and no restore guards a log line, so they needed no step either. Schema version 7 (v1 wave 4) added `exploredTiles`, empty in older snapshots and backups. Every other wave 4 addition is an optional field or a new op (a scene's `blockMovement` and `vision`, a chunk's heights and stair marks, a token's squeeze flag, light and `partyVision`, a phase's light), and movement blocking is a permission rule that replay does not run, so stored moves replay as they were made and the op version stayed at 3. Schema version 6 (v1 wave 3) added `stamps` and `lights`, empty in older snapshots and backups. Every other wave 3 addition is an optional field whose absence reads as before (a chunk's free walls, a scene's automatic walls rule, folder, thumbnail and phases), so no existing room changes its behavior. The op version stayed at 3: no protocol rule tightened, and edge validation loosened (section 8.1), which older ops still pass. A value guard hashes the stored value, and older values simply lack the new fields, so stored restores still match on replay. The optional `from` on `encounter.next` and `encounter.prev` (section 9.4, after wave 3) left it at 3 too: an op without it applies as before, so stored ops replay unchanged. Schema version 8 (v1 wave 5) added `journal`, `pins` and `rolls`, empty in older snapshots and backups. Every other addition of that wave is an optional field (`playersDraw`, `Actor.monsterId`), a new variant (an icon `ImageRef`) or a new op, and the drawing permission is a permission rule that replay does not run, so stored drawings replay as they were made and that wave left the op version at 3. Schema version 9 (PDF handouts, docs/design-v1-pdf-handouts.md 3.8) added `handoutDocs`, empty in older snapshots and backups. A handout's `pages` is optional, and `handout.show` with one image keeps its schema branch, so stored ops replay unchanged and the op version stayed at 5. An older build refuses a version 9 snapshot, since migrations never run down, so going back means restoring the backup taken before the update (README, Updating). Schema version 10 (companion stat blocks, docs/design-v1-companion-stat-blocks.md 3.6) added `companionBlocks`, empty in older snapshots and backups, and an older build refuses it the same way. Op version 7 and schema version 11 are v1 wave 7's one bump, made by its first build, the dice: every wave 7 addition is an optional field, a new log line or a new op, so both steps return what they are given, and an older build refuses either, so going back is the backup's restore (README, Updating). Migrations in `shared/migrate.ts` are pure functions with tests. A deploy writes snapshots on shutdown, so a version change normally replays no old ops at all. Replay runs a stored op's migrations, then checks it with the current protocol schema. So a change that tightens a protocol rule bumps `op_version` and adds a step that brings older ops within the new rule, or a crash right after the deploy would leave rooms read-only. An op with no migration path stops the replay like any other failure. A change that makes the reducer refuse more must not refuse ops stored before it either: those keep the old rule, as a `sys.restore` without the `deps` mark does (section 7.7). Op version 4 came with the project owner's decision that a creature revealed on its turn follows the fog and sight rules (sections 9.4 and 13.4). Its ops have the same shape, so the step from 3 changes nothing, but the `seen` marks the reducer writes changed (a turn's start, path stops, log lines and a copied ruler's visibility), and so did whether a player's guarded Next or Previous finds the acting row in their view (section 9.4). Replay therefore hands the reducer each row's stored version (`Env.opVersion`), and a version 3 op counts the revealed creature as seen wherever it stood, as it did when applied. So it replays to the very state it made, and a later stored restore's guard on its token or the encounter still matches. Live ops carry no stored version and take today's rule. Op version 5 came the same way with the combat flow's healing rules (docs/design-v1-combat-flow.md 3.4 and 3.5): healing brings the dead back while the room's `healRevives` is on, and Revive leaves at least 1 HP, read through `healRevivesDead` and `reviveToOne`. Op version 6 came with two rules at one boundary: a resize is not movement, so it records an uncounted stop and the old size on every earlier stop (docs/design-v1-token-window.md 8.7, read through `resizeStop`), and an actor links one stat block, so setting any of `srdId`, `monsterId` and `blockId` clears the other two (docs/design-v1-companion-stat-blocks.md 3.2, read through `oneLinkPerActor`). Neither changed an op's shape, so each step returns the op as it is, and a version 4 or 5 op replays to the state it made then. Op version 9 and schema version 13 came with a Universal VTT import's mark on its scene (`gridFromFile`, docs/design-v1-grid-detect.md section 6). It is an optional field whose absence reads as before, which `scene.create` alone takes, so both steps return what they are given. The numbers move because an older build's strict schemas refuse the field: such a build refuses the room cleanly, and going back is the backup's restore (README, Updating). Op version 10 and schema version 14 rename the dragon turtle's SRD id from `dragon-tur-t-le` to `dragon-turtle`. The SRD import had taken monster names from the SRD's index of stat blocks, whose kerning gaps split that one name, and now takes them from the stat blocks. The snapshot step renames the `srdImages` key and the image's `id`, and every field that names an SRD monster: an actor's `srdId` (tokens and characters), a saved encounter's monsters, a companion block's `fromSrd`, and the shapes an HP log line's `shapeTemp` names. The op step renames the same fields in stored ops, in live ops as the server admits them too, and the key and `id` of a stored restore's SRD image. An initiative row's `groupKey` keeps the spelling it was stored with, in the room, in stored ops and in live ones: it is the SRD id or else a monster's name, and a monster named `dragon-turtle` gave its row that very key before, so a renamed key could not be read back for an older guard. The tracker reads the old id as the new one wherever it groups or compares rows (`rowGroup`), and a version 9 op that keys a new row by its token's SRD id keys it by the old spelling, as its build did (`storedSrdId`). A stored restore keeps its guards, whose hashes were taken over the old spelling, so replay hands the reducer the stored version and a version 9 guard also matches the entity spelled the old way (`oldSrdIdGuards`). It still refuses a change that really happened since. Older log rows in `room_log` keep the old spelling, since nothing reads them back as an id. An older build refuses either version, so going back is the backup's restore (README, Updating).
- **Log rows** are written in the batch transaction with the ops that produced them, with `on conflict do nothing`, so they are as durable as the ops and a replay never duplicates them.

### 16.3 Retention

- Sessions expire 180 days after last use and are then deleted. Since v1 wave 5 a table display's session ends 30 days after its last socket closed (section 10.2), and an admin session 12 hours after sign-in (section 15.8).
- Rooms are never deleted automatically. The DM can delete a room, and the admin CLI lists rooms with their last activity and size, as since v1 wave 5 the admin page does, which can also delete one (section 15.8).
- Asset garbage collection (v1): the plan was to delete asset rows not referenced by the room state for 30 days, then any file no row refers to. v1 wave 5 built it narrower (docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 3.19, wave 5 question 2, which the project owner decided on 2026-10-14), since every picker lists a room's images as its library, and an image nothing uses now may be one the DM keeps for later:
  - **Automatically,** in a job of the server process (`server/assets/cleanup.ts`), first 10 minutes after boot and then every 24 hours. A scene thumbnail the room's state stops naming is marked (`assets.unused_since`), cleared when named again, and deleted after 1 day, row then file: a restore keeps the current thumbnail (section 7.7), so nothing can need an old one back. On the first pass after each start and every 7th pass after, a walk deletes files named like an asset variant whose hash, kind and pipeline no row names and that are more than a day old, so an upload between its file and its row is never caught, and never touches `tmp`, `import` or `trash`. The trash's day folders go 48 hours after their day (section 15.7). The job does not load a room that is not loaded: it reads its snapshot and every op stored after it as text, and any appearance of an asset's ID counts as a use, which can only keep too much. A room whose replay failed is skipped.
  - **By the DM.** The DM's library marks each image the state does not use "Not in use", with Delete on it and "Delete N not in use (12 MB)", each on a second tap: "No token, scene, stamp or handout uses these. A deleted image is gone for good, from undo too." Thumbnails are never offered. `POST /api/rooms/:id/assets/delete {ids}` (DM grant, at most 500) deletes them all, or none when the state names any of them then, answering `409 asset_in_use` with the ids in use, and `409 read_only` for a room whose replay failed.
  - **The deletion barrier.** An op can name an asset at any moment, so a row is deleted only by one step on the room's actor queue. The step first commits the batch being staged, so nothing it relies on can still be abandoned, computes the used set from the committed state (`referencedAssetIds`, every reference a room state holds), deletes the requested rows that are unused in one statement awaited inside the step, and records the deleted ids under a new value of the room's deletion generation, a counter apart from the seq. An op reads that generation and registers the asset ids its fact lookup reads in one synchronous step before the lookup's first `await`. When it reaches the queue, a fact for an id deleted since is dropped, so permissions answer `gone` and no reference to a missing row is ever stored. A deletion keeps only those of its ids that an older outstanding lookup asked for, so the record stays bounded however long a lookup stalls. For a room that is not loaded, the step runs under a per-room lock the manager holds and a load waits for. Files go after their rows, into the trash, only when no row in any room names them.
  - Deleting rows frees quota, and a room read-only at its quota checks its size again at once.
- `backup_runs` rows older than 90 days are deleted by the backup service (section 15.7).

### 16.4 Export and import

- `GET /api/rooms/:id/export` (DM) takes a copy of the committed state and the asset list on the actor queue, which is quick because the state is immutable, then streams the zip outside the queue, so the room keeps playing during a large export. The zip holds:
  - `room.json`: the actor's current committed state, with every scene, the room's custom monsters, its SRD monster images and its saved encounters, since v1 wave 3 the free walls, stamps, lights, map phases, folders and thumbnail references, since v1 wave 4 the explored layer, heights and stair marks, and since v1 wave 5 the journal, pins, open roll requests, `playersDraw`, `monsterId` and icon images, as they are stored (not the periodic database snapshot, and not a scene-scoped projection), with a format version. The export stays the stored state, so it carries open roll requests that the import drops. Thumbnails are `thumb` assets, so they travel and count like any other asset. Only the log is filtered: whispers the exporting DM may not read are left out. Beside the state it lists `dmMemberIds`, the members that ever held a DM grant (`room_dm_members`), which the state does not show once a co-DM is back to `'player'`.
  - `assets.json`: a versioned manifest with each asset's ID, SHA-256, kind, canonical width and height, variants, and uploader member IDs, and since v1 wave 5 an Openverse image's credit, which the import checks as bounded text and keeps.
  - `assets/<sha256>-<kind>-<variant>.webp`.
- `POST /api/rooms/import` with an `application/zip` body creates a new room with new IDs, a new DM key and a new player password from the form. It counts as a room creation for every creation limit. Before writing anything it:
  - caps the body at 95 MB, the entry count at 10,000, the manifest at 5,000 assets and 2 MiB, and the total decompressed size at 1 GiB, counting inflated bytes while reading (central-directory sizes can lie). The entry cap was 2,000 until PDF handouts: an export writes two entries for each handout page, so a room near its 2,000 pages could not come back (docs/design-v1-pdf-handouts.md 3.6);
  - validates `room.json` with the snapshot schema and the room limits, which since v1 wave 3 include the free walls' length and lattice, the stamp, light, phase and folder bounds, a scene's phases arriving with their active phase, grid reference and map, every phase passing the reducer's checks against that grid (the reference's shape, and its scaled grid within the protocol's bounds) with the map exactly the showing phase's image on it, the caps on pillars, doors and windows and drawn edges, and the split's counting pass on every scene, since no op's limits step ever saw an imported scene's walls (section 9.7), and since v1 wave 4 the height and stair codecs, the sight piece cap, and the room's size with the player scene's explored reserve, refused with the explore message when it does not fit (section 10.5);
  - treats `assets.json` as untrusted: each image's kind, dimensions and variants are recomputed through sharp, and the manifest only maps old asset IDs to files. Its uploaders are kept, under their new member IDs, only on kinds a player may upload (`token`): every imported member is a player in the new room, and an uploader row is a player's read access (section 11.2), so a hand-made manifest naming a player the uploader of a map or a thumbnail would otherwise let that player read it;
  - remaps every ID, uploaders included, and every asset reference: maps, token and character images, handouts, and monsters' default images. An SRD image keeps its key, the SRD ID, which means the same in every room. A reference to an image that did not come through is dropped, and an SRD choice with it. The turn's starting spot and a kept ruler's anchors follow their tokens' new IDs. Tokens' paths and the `seq` of each turn's start are left out: seqs count from the start again in the new room, where a later turn could take an old turn's number and its path, so the turn under way records no path until the next turn. A kept ruler on a token that did not come through is dropped, and so is a starting spot whose row or token did not. A saved encounter keeps its SRD monsters and follows the room's own to their new IDs, and a line naming one deleted before the backup is dropped. Since v1 wave 3 it also remaps a stamp's library image, every phase's image and each scene's thumbnail, and gives stamps and lights new IDs. Since v1 wave 4 the explored layer follows its scene's new ID, as the fog does. Since v1 wave 5 journal pages and pins get new IDs, a page's access follows its member's new ID (a page private to a member who did not come through becomes DM only), a pin follows its page and scene (a pin whose page did not come through becomes DM only and loses its link), `playersDraw` follows its members, and a token's `monsterId` follows its monster or is dropped when the monster did not come through. Every open roll request is dropped, since it belongs to a moment of play. Since PDF handouts every page of a handout and of a document is remapped as a `handout` image. A page that did not come through, or came as another kind, is dropped and the rest keep their order. A handout or a document left with no page is dropped, a handout's `assetId` is its first page after that, and a document gets a new ID (docs/design-v1-pdf-handouts.md 3.6). An icon image keeps its key, which means the same in every room. Free wall IDs are local to their chunk and keep. A stamp whose image did not come through is dropped, and so is a thumbnail. A phase whose image did not come through is dropped: when it was the one showing, the scene switches to the first one left on its grid reference, and when none is left the scene has neither phases nor a map. Every reference keeps its image only when the image came through as the kind an op would take there: `token` for tokens, characters, monsters, SRD choices and stamps, `map` for maps and phases, `thumb` for thumbnails and `handout` for handouts. A reference to another kind is dropped like one whose image did not come through. Only a hand-made file holds one, and it would otherwise put a thumbnail or a phase not showing into the players' visible set, which is all the asset route checks;
  - marks the members in `dmMemberIds`, and any still labeled DM, as former DMs under their new IDs, so nobody can continue as them (section 10.2). A backup without the list predates it and cannot tell a former co-DM from a player, so every one of its members is marked, and its players join as new members;
  - reserves the storage quota;
  - streams entries one at a time through sharp at the back of the processing queue, each under its kind's size cap, counting temporary bytes against the reservation and re-checking the free-disk guard before each entry.
- Exports bigger than 95 MB cannot come back through Cloudflare's upload limit. They restore with `docker compose -f docker-compose.server.yml exec raccoon node scripts/admin.ts import /data/assets/import/<file>.zip`.
- Backups: room export covers the MVP. A nightly `pg_dump` plus asset volume copy is a v1 compose service, built in v1 wave 5 (section 15.7).
- Display codes and sessions, admin sessions and backup records (v1 wave 5) are server data, never part of a room, and never exported. So are roll receipts (v1 wave 7).
- Since v1 wave 7 `dice` lines travel with the log, and rolled answers keep their dice. The import remaps a line's roller and each roll's creature (docs/design-v1-dice.md 5.3).

## 17. Decisions from the project owner

The project owner answered the open product questions after the design review:

1. **Dice:** none. The app makes no rolls at all. The DM rolls monster initiative and types it in. Decision 14 later made one exception. Superseded on 2026-10-17 (docs/design-v1-dice.md, v1 wave 7): rolls happen on the server with a cryptographic random source, so everyone sees the same result and nobody can fake one, and a roll needs a connection. Rolls are public by default, the DM's start secret, and anyone can roll one to the DM only. A player can still type a total from real dice. The Dice panel comes before Log among the tabs, a request's rolled answer reaches only the DM and that creature's players, and a rolled answer is final for its player, with the DM's one-tap approval of a reroll or an added die.
2. **Whispers:** private to the sender and the recipients. The DM does not see whispers between players.
3. **"Continue as":** accepted. Anyone with the player password can continue as any player. The stage 3 review added one exception: a member that was ever a DM cannot be continued as (section 10.2).
4. **Room password:** at least 8 characters. The generated suggestion is three hyphenated words, such as `owlbear-druid-wand`.
5. **Site-wide limit:** 20 new rooms per day, plus 5 per IP.
6. **Search thumbnails:** loaded straight from their host: serpapi.com, or Google's thumbnail servers since SerpApi moved to them.
7. **DM rulers:** private until the DM shares one.
8. **Revealed combatants:** owner's call. The automatic reveal lasts until the encounter ends, and the DM can mark any token always visible by hand.
9. **Failed join limits:** per IP only. Password joins and DM link claims have no per-room failure cap, so nobody who knows a room ID can lock its new devices out. The per-IP backoff stays, with IPv6 grouped by /64 (section 10.3). After the stage 3 review the project owner accepted the risk this leaves: someone who controls many IPv6 /64s and knows the room ID could guess a generated three-word passphrase in months, bounded only by the argon2 queue, and keep joins busy meanwhile. The project owner then chose a mitigation that keeps three-word passwords: once a room sees 20 failed password joins in an hour, joining it needs a Cloudflare Turnstile check, not a lockout (section 10.3). It works only when Turnstile is configured.
10. **Player link:** the players' room details are one join link that carries the password, not a link and a separate password. The owner put the password in the URL fragment rather than a query parameter, so it never reaches server or Cloudflare logs (section 10.2).
11. **The player link at any time:** asked "Should the DM be able to see the player join link any time?", the project owner answered "Yes, keep it viewable". The server keeps the player password encrypted with AES-256-GCM under `ROOM_SECRET_KEY`, beside the hash that checks joins, and shows it only to the room's DMs (section 10.2).
12. **One DM on several devices** (the owner's decision after the usability test): a DM link or invite opened on a new device offers "This is me" for each current DM member, so one person stays one member. The DM key already grants full DM rights. Picking a member also opens that member's whispers, which the owner accepted: whoever holds the key is trusted as the DM. A player member can never be picked (section 10.2).
13. **Nothing behind a blackout:** the project owner asked that a reload never spoil anything. So while a blackout is on, the server withholds the whole table from players, not only their screens: the scene the DM prepares behind it, its map, tokens and fog, the initiative tracker and handouts (section 7.3). Lifting the blackout sends it all at once.
14. **Auto initiative for monsters:** the project owner reversed decision 1 for monster initiative only, asking for an auto initiative option that rolls for the DM's monsters, with three modes: all monsters at once, one roll for the boss and one for the rest, and one roll per monster, modeled on D&D Beyond's VTT. Before it was built, the project owner asked that the app follow D&D Beyond wherever the research found it differed from those three modes. D&D Beyond's map tracker groups identical monsters under one shared roll, lets the DM ungroup them, and has no boss setting. So the owner made that grouping the default ("By creature"), in which a boss rolls alone because it is its own creature, in place of a boss and non-boss mode. "One each" and "All at once" are kept as the project owner described them. Players still roll their own and type them in (section 9.4). The code review then asked whether By creature, which gives goblins, wolves and their boss three rolls where the request described two, is what the DM wants, or whether to add a literal boss and non-boss mode as well. The project owner chose "By creature only (Recommended)": there is no boss mode, and the modes stay By creature, One each and All at once.
15. **The path walked** (v1 wave 2): asked about the straight-line distance moved, the project owner answered "full path walked maybe makes more sense, but this would also require an undo move or require the player in some way to be able to backtrack (tracking where they walked helps work out opportunity attacks, etc, and if they could actually get there as opposed to a straight line)". The project owner approved the plan of section 9.2: each drop is a leg, "I think we can do the mobile drop for both desktop and mobile to keep things simple, feels natural and 3 undos makes sense to me", so a drag in three drops takes three presses of "Undo move" to walk back, and "yes forced movement should not count", which the "Doesn't count" button marks.
16. **Stamp art** (v1 wave 3, the addendum's question 6, 2026-10-14): asked for one symbolic set or top-down pictures, the project owner chose the look of Prime_8's trees: pictures seen from straight above, in color, with a soft shadow. So the stamps are Prime_8's trees and stones, the Warlock's Gauntlet foliage and village elements submitted by Liosan, and Poly Haven's furniture rendered from above by a committed script, every author credited, and no throne or altar (section 20).
17. **Automatic walls** (v1 wave 3, the addendum's question 17, 2026-10-14): the project owner approved the rule as written. Blank mats keep the MVP's rule, new and existing, scenes made from a map image from wave 3 on wall floor only, Universal VTT imports start with none, every scene from before wave 3 keeps its walls, and the DM can switch any scene's rule in its settings (section 12).

18. **Party vision** (v1 wave 4, the addendum's question 1, 2026-10-14): asked whether every player sees what any party member sees or only what their own characters see, the project owner chose party vision. The visible region is the union of what every party creature sees, the same for every player, worked out once per scene on the server (section 9.10).
19. **Owners' senses and lights are trusted** (v1 wave 4, question 11, 2026-10-14): owners keep editing their senses line and set their token's light, Custom radii included, with ranges capped at the SRD's maximums, 150 ft for senses and 120 ft for each light radius, and the DM sees what owners set in the Vision settings and token panel (sections 9.10 and 14.12).
20. **Teleport here** (v1 wave 4, question 15, 2026-10-14): a stopped drop offers "Teleport here", for Misty Step and anything else that passes walls or ledges. The token lands where the player dropped it, the leg does not count, and the DM gets a note. The review then made it replace the walk to the wall, which the client takes back first, so the teleport is one uncounted leg from where the walk began (sections 9.8 and 14.13).
21. **Squeezing** (v1 wave 4, question 12, 2026-10-14): a creature may pass an opening sized for one size smaller, as the SRD's narrow opening allows, and a Large creature may stop squeezed in a 5 ft corridor, drawn full size over the walls while every rule counts it at its squeezed size. Nobody passes a wall, and the app does not charge the extra movement. After the build the owner tightened where a squeeze holds: only in a real narrow space, never beside one wall in the open (section 9.8).
22. **Undoing fog keeps what was explored** (v1 wave 4, 2026-10-14): undoing an older DM fog edit takes back only the DM's edit, and anything the party explored since stays explored (section 13.6).
23. **Hold means Ready** (2026-10-14): the "Hold and Ready markers" of features section 10 are one thing, the Ready marker wave 2 built (section 6). Nothing changed for it.
24. **Stat blocks for player characters** (v1 wave 7, 2026-10-17, docs/design-v1-pc-stats.md): asked for dice "for saves, attack rolls, ability checks, and any other rolls", the project owner added "I believe this will require adding support for pc stat blocks", typed in with helpers and imported from D&D Beyond, a share link or its JSON, through the server's outbound proxy, best effort. A character's block is seen by the DM and that character's players only, as its light card, and rolls made from it reach the log by the dice's rules. A request card always offers a roll, "Roll d20" with a typed bonus where there is no block, and the stat block view stays text: every roll is made in the Dice panel.

The project owner decided the addendum's other wave 3 questions on 2026-10-14, after every open question was listed with its recommendation: "All defaults good with me". So each keeps its recommendation: the DM may stamp images from the room's library (question 7), portals that do not block light import as windows unless the DM picks open doors (question 8), and a duplicate copies creatures but not player characters, fog or drawings unless asked (question 9).

Its other wave 4 questions keep theirs too: walking tokens use stairs both ways, even for a 5 ft rise (questions 2 and 13), a creature over a platform higher than the party's eyes is hidden from below (question 3), new scenes stop players' moves at walls and ledges while scenes from before do not until the DM turns it on (question 4), a map phase may set the light level (question 10), initiative rows hide while their creature is out of sight (question 14), and doors show their live state on explored ground (question 16). How close a player's token must be to open a door (question 5) is decided the same way: the owner chose the recommendation, the larger of 5 ft and one cell counting elevation, with a clear line to the door (section 9.8), and the project owner kept it with the other defaults.

The project owner decided the wave 5 addendum's questions (docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md section 9) with the same answer on 2026-10-14, each as recommended:

1. "Full stat blocks" means a monster or NPC token's whole block for the DM, and an SRD form's block for its owner (section 14.15).
2. Cleanup deletes on its own only replaced thumbnails and files no row names. The DM deletes the rest from the library (section 16.3).
3. Players do not write journal pages.
4. A roll's results reach only the DM and the creature's player.
5. Openverse searches leave out no-derivatives licenses.
6. "Who can draw" leaves kept rulers, pings and the laser alone.
7. Backups stay on the server, and README gives the command to copy them off.
8. The table display shows whose turn it is only when asked.
9. The X-card sits at the top of the Menu, sent with a second tap on its X.
10. The party bar is the DM's.
11. All 4,180 icons ship.
12. Both roll switches start on.
13. A table display stays signed in until Leave, or 30 days after it last connected.
14. The TV shows no roll results.
15. "Works offline" for icons means needing no outside service.

One owner decision came during the build: the X-card shows on players behind the blackout's pause screen too, and a player there can send one (section 14.16).

## 18. Testing

- **Unit (`node --test`):**
  - Rules:
    - Footprints, and distance under all three diagonal rules with elevation, including Tiny next to Medium and symmetry.
    - Every AoE shape against the research table: a 20 ft sphere covers 52 cells under `half`, and a 15 ft cone covers 4 cells pointing along an axis from an intersection.
    - Cube face offsets and cylinder anchors.
    - Inclusion rules with Tiny and Large creatures and flying heights.
    - Wall clipping.
  - The reducer and its inverse ops, with the same envelope always giving the same result.
  - Optimistic replay against redacted state: an op whose reads are missing or redacted is never replayed, for players and for a DM viewing a different scene than the encounter's tokens, and pending ops whose targets vanished are skipped.
  - Auto initiative: d20 faces and advantage averages from a seeded source, grouping in each mode, a monster added mid-fight joining its group's value, the modifier, which rows roll, the ops one press sends (batches of 100, a re-roll landing on a row's own value left out), the card going with its fight, ties and undo through the real reducer, players refused, and players' projections identical to the same values typed.
  - Initiative ordering, hidden entries revealed on their turn (including under fog, where players still do not see them), and duration ticking, including "end of next turn" applied during the anchor's own turn, a duration applied outside combat that binds when its creature joins, a removed anchor, and `prev` restoring expired effects.
  - Every HP and life-state transition in section 9.6, including damage to a stable creature and monsters with death saves.
  - AoE refinement: the same template gives the same cells at any size (no change at a sampling threshold), and the signed-distance shortcut agrees with full 16x16 sampling, with and without walls.
  - Movement and range (v1): the legs a move adds to the active creature's path and when it adds none, a path of an earlier turn counting as empty, Previous bringing a turn's path back, the oldest corners merging, each stop's `seen` mark, a mark that a leg does not count and its undo for a player, the distance along a path under each diagonal rule and with climbing, what is left of each speed and the Dash band, which drags read against a speed and from where, the cells range rings enclose against the ruler's own reading, a flier's ring, and the ring outline cut by the scene's edge. Kept rulers: double taps, stops and anchors kept and read back, and the reducer's checks on them.
  - Fog rasterization: reveal and hide in any order. Tokens under fog withheld only when fog is enabled, never when owned by a player or when `fogExempt` is set, a creature revealed on its turn withheld like any other, and `aboveFog` agreeing with that rule for owners, other players, the manual exemption and the automatic one.
  - Permissions for every op, including reference checks and every answer of `playersMoveAny`.
  - Custom monsters: every SRD 5.2.1 monster through the stat block shape and back (Markdown, light fields and tokens), and through the paste parser in each layout.
  - Monster images: which tokens are a monster's, the apply plan (no image, the monster's own, set by hand, played by a player, other scenes), its image-only ops in one undo group, and tokens placed with the default.
  - Maps (v1 wave 3): the exact orientation predicate, the split (crossing free walls, walls through one point off the lattice, collinear overlaps, any order giving the same pieces), the counting pass refusing a dense pile of free walls and a lattice of crossing walls, areas behind crossing free walls, and visibility over the split pieces against an independent ray cast for whole-scene, culled and light boxes. A test fails if the exact geometry calls a banned `Math` function. The three automatic walls rules, pillars and what each feature blocks, and wall sets cached by identity, a staged state the size guard refused included. The reducer's free walls, border edges, stamps, lights, phases (reorder, replace and remove keeping the grid on its cells), duplicate (derived IDs, nothing of the fight, rulers and areas), thumbnails and their undo. The projection's scene allowlist, stamps withheld when hidden or fogged with their images, lights for the DM alone, sorted free walls, and hidden secret doors under each automatic walls rule, across a chunk border too. The limits step refusing painting, drawn edges and automatic walls past the split's caps, and pillars, doors and windows and drawn edges past their caps, an undo included, while a room already over a cap keeps its other edits and a room import over one is refused, and a refusal toast that names the room or the scene that is full. The Universal VTT conversion on synthetic files written for it: all four border edges, positive and negative fractional origins, long walls cut on the lattice, welded ends, walls cut at portals, lights moved off walls, and ops batched under 48 KiB.
  - Walls, heights and vision (v1 wave 4, addendum section 6): a Large creature passing a 5 ft door and corridor and a stair one cell wide, a Huge one 10 ft and not 5, a Gargantuan one 15 ft and not 10, no path ever crossing a wall (a Large creature a quarter cell from a free wall refused toward and across it by every shifted path), a token on a wall stepping off either way but never along it, junctions decided on the stored segments, and a player's projected movement set equal to the server's for every secret-door and lock state. Squeezes: custom 1.01, 1.99 and 1 by 3 cores, a squeeze refused in the open, against one wall, beside a closed door or for a token with no side of 2, its undo and redo, and a squeezed creature counted at its squeezed size by areas and the ruler. Teleports: one uncounted leg from where the walk began, the fallback when the walk's undo is refused, the DM-only line, and its undo and redo in a fight and outside one. Ground and stair directions, ledges for walkers and fliers, lifts on a height paint and their undo. Sight against an independent ray cast for whole-scene, culled and light sweeps, with crossing free walls, pillars and raised edges, eyes on and near walls stepped off on their own side or failing closed, incremental equal to cold with actor changes and long free walls across chunk borders, golden bits, and no banned `Math` function. Door reach in three dimensions under each diagonal rule. The explored layer through every fog undo sequence the addendum's reviewers set, hides and resets clearing it, and its reserve at a reduced room limit across rev digit boundaries. The sight raster drawing the server's own tiles sub-cell for sub-cell, the map export's player view on the merged fog, and the fog tile decoder against the one before it.
  - DM tools and the rest (v1 wave 5, docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 3 and 6): roll requests (each skill's usual ability against the SRD table, one or two choices, a skill refused in a save, pass at exactly the DC, fail without rolling only on a save, finality only with a hidden DC and shown results, an answer replacing another on a request with no DC, reopen, and each reminder against the SRD text it quotes), journal pages (reveal and hide, a block's revealed state kept through edits, a deletion making its pins DM only and its undo putting both back, member removal), pins, every answer of `playersDraw` for each drawing kind and a player's restore, the group ops whole or refused whole with their tally phrases, `monsterId` set by each way of placing and remapped on import, icon keys and every `ImageRef` reader, the size guard with a room-level op against a scene nobody views and two DMs with different whispers, `referencedAssetIds` against every reference, the log export's sessions and text, Openverse's fallback order, budgets, token refresh, thumbnail rule and credit lines, the shortcut table across AZERTY and Cyrillic layouts with `?` and `+` typed with Shift, and the interface size's width hook and positions.
  - Encounter builder: the XP budget table against the SRD's three worked examples, each with the SRD stat blocks' own XP, mixed levels, the party from the characters' levels, the draft's caps and the patch a save sends, and placing: one token per creature with its monster's image, the biggest nearest the tap, no overlaps, numbered and grouped by the reducer, hidden when asked, rows added to a running fight, every op under one undo group, and the inverses taking it all back.
- **Projection property tests:** random states, op sequences and viewers.
  - No player projection contains a hidden entity it does not own, a token or label under fog, a DM-only field, `dmNotes`, `encounterHistory`, a fog draft, `groupKey`, a gap in `tieOrder`, another scene, an unshown handout, someone else's whisper, a custom monster, a monster's default image, a saved encounter, or the level of a character it does not own.
  - HP numbers and death save counts in log entries respect `hpVisibility`. A turn's starting spot reaches a player only for the active row they see, with its token in sight, and only one they own or could see there. A path reaches a player only for the turn under way of a row they see, without `seen` marks, and a non-owner only its seen stops, with a leg's `uncounted` mark only when both its ends were seen. A kept ruler reaches a player only when every token it stands on is in their projection, with each of those stops where its token stands now. No player gets a log event about a hidden effect, and no non-owner gets one written while the creature or its initiative row was out of their sight.
  - Applying a viewer's events to its snapshot always equals a fresh projection.
  - Since v1 wave 4 the random states and ops include vision toggles and their undo, explore writes, tiles only the explored layer holds, hides and resets clearing them, and vision turned off after exploring. On a vision scene no player projection holds a non-party creature outside the party's sight unless exempt, a Tiny creature behind a free wall in its own cell included, and sight tiles equal a fresh computation, diffed scene by scene as the server's fan-out does.
  - Since v1 wave 5 they also hold journal pages with secret blocks, reveals, access changes and deletions, pins linked to pages and pins reached through exploration, roll requests answered under both switches, icon images, and a table display among the viewers. No player projection holds a DM-only page, a page private to another player, an unrevealed secret block, a pin linked to a page the player cannot read, another player's roll target, a hidden DC or result, or a `monsterId`, and a display's projection equals that of a player who owns nothing.
  - After the client applies a batch's `ev`, its `confirmed` equals the projection of the state after that batch, no acknowledged op is still pending, and replaying the remaining pending ops never counts any op twice.
- **Server integration** against a real Postgres in Docker:
  - Room creation and its limits.
  - The two-step join, and joining as a DM member refused. A ticket from room A refused at room B, a ticket used by another session refused, and a ticket refused after a password change.
  - DM claim with session rotation (same session row, old token dead), invites (single use, expiry, epoch, and an invite from room A refused at room B), bare invite tokens.
  - Replacing the DM link leaves only the replacer with DM rights. Remove DM and device revoke delete that device's unused invites and reset `kind`. Kicks.
  - Admin `create-room` and `pin`, and loaded-room eviction that never unloads a pinned room.
  - CSRF and Origin rejection. Rate limits keyed per session at a shared IP, and the per-IP and per-room limits that new sessions cannot reset. The join challenge: no check under its threshold, a missing or rejected token refused before the hash, the count spanning IPs, and nothing asked without Turnstile. The global cap on concurrent outbound fetches.
  - Upload limits, quota reservation including variants larger than the upload, corrupt images, the file naming rule, a second uploader of identical bytes gaining access with a response identical to a fresh upload, and room deletion keeping files another room uses.
  - Export and import round trip with the asset manifest, a hostile manifest, whispers filtered from the export, custom monsters under new IDs, monsters' default images under new asset IDs and unreadable by players until a token wears them, and backups from before custom monsters and before SRD monster images.
  - Asset listing and fetch authorization.
  - Vision (v1 wave 4): a monster walking out of the party's sight never reaches a player socket until it walks back in, a monster's drag out of sight reaches a player only as a bare cancel and no frame carries a path, explore runs only on the player scene and never behind a blackout and replays as stored, and players moved between two vision scenes keep each scene's tiles apart.
  - Maps (v1 wave 3): a thumbnail is the DM's to upload and read, a scene names only a `thumb` asset, and it goes on no undo stack. Phases take their sizes from the asset rows, and players read only the active image. An export carries stamps, lights, free walls, phases and a thumbnail into a new room under new IDs.
  - DM tools and the rest (v1 wave 5): display codes (minting with only the DM's cookie, redeeming with none, once, the fifth display refused before the code is spent, their own failure bucket), a display socket's projection, its `4003` for sending and `4001` when its session ends or expires, renewal only of a live session, the display image route, and cookie substitution both ways. The X-card reaching every socket on every scene, a paused player's and a display's included, with no row in `room_ops`, `room_log`, `rate_events` or `sessions`, no cookie and no request log line. The log export: hidden rows changing no player's listing, file or remaining limit, and one member with a DM grant and a player grant getting each role's own rows in either order. Openverse with its credit stored from the cache, never the request. The deletion barrier's races and its bounded record (`server/rooms/barrier.itest.ts`), cleanup, the library delete, the admin routes with their login limits, and the CLI import leaving the server's temporary files alone. A wave 5 room through an export and an import.
  - SSRF rejections: private IPs, IP literals, redirects to private hosts.
  - WebSocket flows:
    - hello, snapshot, op, and the batch `ev` as the acknowledgment;
    - no double count of a relative op, with several ops from one client in one batch;
    - a duplicate of a committed op acked at once, and a duplicate of an op still in an uncommitted batch acked only after the commit, on the client's new socket;
    - `gap` after a `busy`, a malformed op that cannot skip a missing one, a terminal refusal that is durable before it is reported, and `resumeFrom` on every `hello`, including a client stranded by a refusal that never committed;
    - watermark dedupe after a long suspension;
    - commit failure and an unknown commit outcome, including a batch that holds only watermark updates;
    - a burst whose last op is dropped by the frame budget, recovered by the 10 s resend;
    - a DM switching scenes with `scene`, keeping its pending ops, and the snapshot budget delaying and then closing a socket that keeps asking;
    - a dropped `scene` or `hello` recovered by the 5 s snapshot deadline while heartbeats stay healthy;
    - a large-scene switch for 12 sockets at one shared IP in a pinned room stays connected, with `wait` keeping the deadline from firing;
    - a budget delay of at least 15 s during which the client stays connected, on repeated `wait` messages, until its snapshot arrives;
    - the queue cap and rate limits answering `busy`;
    - a hidden token never reaching a player socket, `eph` scoped by scene, drag frames into fog withheld from players, and previews cleared on cancel, disconnect, settlement and a drag into fog;
    - loaded-room eviction with `4010` that cannot loop under sustained contention, and `4011` when nothing qualifies;
    - kicking a DM then redeeming an invite that DM minted, refused;
    - uploads beyond the 4 intake slots refused before their bodies are read;
    - graceful restart.
- **Client:** store logic (pending replay, watermark drop, own `ev` applied once) under `node --test` (no DOM). The fail-closed fog gate gets a test that renders with missing and corrupt fog data.
- **Stage 3:**
  - A Playwright smoke run drives two browsers (DM and player) through create, join, upload, move, fog reveal and a cone preview, with screenshots saved outside the repository.
  - It checks that `permessage-deflate` is negotiated through the tunnel, and records snapshot sizes without it.
  - The maps run (v1 wave 3, `e2e/maps.e2e.ts`) and the vision run (v1 wave 4, `e2e/vision.e2e.ts`) follow it, each on its own port. The vision run lays out a dark scene over a second socket of the DM's session, then a DM on a desktop drags a goblin into the edge of the party's torchlight and back out, and a player on a phone is stopped at a closed door and opens it, is stopped at a ledge and takes the stair, teleports past a wall, and leaves explored ground behind. After each step the player's creatures and sight tiles are checked against sight worked out from the DM's state with the server's rules, and every frame the player received is checked at the end: no creature out of sight, no light, no explored layer, no drag path and no teleport line.
  - The wave 5 run (v1 wave 5, `e2e/tools.e2e.ts`) drives three browsers, a DM on a desktop, a player on a phone and a table display paired with a code: a roll asked with two choices and answered, a secret revealed, a page pinned to the map and opened from its pin, the page deleted, the display following the DM while the DM drags a hidden monster, the X-card sent from the Menu and from the pause screen, an icon token, and the log export. Every frame the player and the display received is checked at the end: neither holds the hidden monster, the display holds no roll request, and every image the display loaded came through its own route.
- **Operations** (v1 wave 5): `pnpm test:backup` runs the backup check (section 15.7). It needs Docker, so it runs before a release, like the server-box benchmarks.
- **Verification commands:** `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test`, `pnpm test:int`, `pnpm build`.

## 19. Build order

Each step ends with typecheck and tests passing. Steps after 3 can run in parallel jj workspaces with clear file ownership.

1. **Scaffold:** package, tsconfigs, Fastify skeleton serving the Vite build, `db.ts` with schema apply, docker-compose for development, `.env.example`.
2. **Shared core:** model, protocol schemas with bounds, reducer, permissions, projection, and the rules engine, with tests.
3. **Server rooms:** auth (rooms, sessions, grants, two-step join, DM key, invites, limits), RoomManager, RoomActor (commit before publish, watermarks, fan-out), persistence, WebSocket endpoint, integration tests.
4. **Client shell:** home, create, join, DM link entry, store, socket, canvas with map, grid, camera, gestures, and tokens with drag.
5. **Assets:** upload, processing, listing, serving, map scenes with grid alignment, token images with crop, URL import, SerpApi search.
6. **Tokens deep:** HP and life state, conditions, statuses, durations, elevation, stat cards, characters, SRD monster import and browser.
7. **Areas of effect:** all shapes, casting from a token, attaching, presets, coverage settings, wall clipping.
8. **Initiative tracker.**
9. **Fog, drawings, pings, laser, follow, blackout, view as player.**
10. **Map builder.**
11. **Log, whispers, handouts, undo, room export and import, settings, DM devices, room deletion.**
12. **Art and production:** source and credit the textures and icons (section 20), Dockerfile, server compose, squid, PWA icons, performance pass, deploy notes in the README.

## 20. Art, attribution and licensing

- **No AI-generated art.** None, ever. Art is never generated unless the project owner explicitly asks.
- **Third-party art in the app** must come from an openly licensed source, such as game-icons.net (CC BY 3.0) for condition and status icons and generic tokens, and CC0 sets from OpenGameArt or Kenney for builder textures.
  - Every file is listed in `CREDITS.md` with its source URL, author and license.
  - CC BY works are also credited on the About page.
- **The raccoon icon** was drawn as an SVG for the project.
- **Map stamps** (v1 wave 3, the project owner's decision on the addendum's question 6: the Prime_8 look). The 46 stamps in `public/stamps/` are pictures seen from straight above, in color, with a soft shadow, listed with their footprints in `shared/stamps.ts`:

  | Source | Author | License | Stamps |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | [Trees Top-down fake shadow](https://opengameart.org/content/trees-top-down-fake-shadow) | Prime_8 | CC0 | 10 trees |
  | [Simple stones](https://opengameart.org/content/simple-stones) | Prime_8 | CC0 | 8 rocks |
  | [Top down foliage collection](https://opengameart.org/content/top-down-foliage-collection) | Warlock's Gauntlet artists (rAum, jackFlower, DrZoliparia, Neil2D), submitted by Liosan | CC BY 3.0 | bushes, ferns, a spiky plant |
  | [Top down village elements](https://opengameart.org/content/top-down-village-elements) | the same, submitted by Liosan | CC BY 3.0 | campfires, wood piles, a broken wheel, a bucket |
  | Poly Haven 3D models, rendered from above | each model's authors, in `CREDITS.md` | CC0 | barrel, chest, crate, table, round table, bed, bookshelf, fire pit, a young pine, two mossy rocks, two stones |

  - No throne or altar ships. The DM stamps an image from the room's library for those, the addendum's question 7, which the project owner decided on 2026-10-14 as recommended. The app ships none of that art, so its licensing is the DM's, as for token images.
  - **The build script.** `node scripts/stamps.ts`, like `scripts/textures.ts` and `scripts/icons.ts`, never hand-made images. It fetches each original from where its author published it and checks it, and each file it takes from an archive, against a pinned SHA-256, so a changed upstream file fails loudly and asks for the license to be checked again. Prime_8's trees ship only as a 7-Zip archive, which `7zip-bin` 5.2.0 (MIT, wrapping a prebuilt 7za under LGPL, used only by this script and never shipped) extracts. Zips go through `yauzl`. Sprites are cropped to their opaque pixels, centered in a box of their footprint's shape, and encoded as WebP with sharp, each footprint set by hand in the script. Each Poly Haven model is rendered straight down by three.js (`three` 0.160.0) with an orthographic camera and a fixed light, in the installed Chrome run headless through playwright-core with SwiftShader (`--use-angle=swiftshader`, `--enable-unsafe-swiftshader`), so it renders in software. The soft shadow is baked under it with sharp afterwards. Images are at most 512 px on their long side, and renders 256 px per cell (less for the largest rock). The outputs are committed, so a later run need not match them bit for bit, and a re-render that changes a pixel is reviewed like any other image change.
  - **Footprints of the renders.** A model's footprint on each axis is its size in cells (1.524 m each), rounded up to the next quarter cell and at least a quarter, and the render keeps its true size, centered in that footprint. The owner chose rounding up over the addendum's nearest quarter: rounding down shrank a 0.96 by 0.52 m chest to 62% to fit 0.75 by 0.25 cells. So the crate and the chest are 0.75 by 0.5 cells, the bed 1 by 1.5, the barrel 0.5 and the table 1.25 by 0.5.
  - **Stones, not boulders.** Poly Haven calls `namaqualand_boulders_01` boulders, but both of its meshes are about a foot across, so the build names their renders Stone 1 and Stone 2, each 0.25 by 0.5 cells.
  - **Credits.** `CREDITS.md` lists every file with its source, author, license and original, and `shared/credits.ts` gives the About page one line per pack or model, CC0 included: its title linked to the source, the author, the license linked, and what changed, "Cropped and converted to WebP." for the sprites and "Rendered from above and converted to WebP." for the renders. The CC BY works link https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/3.0/. The credits test checks that the catalog equals the files in `public/stamps/`.
- **Token icons** (v1 wave 5, docs/design-v1-dm-tools.md 3.12, section 11.5). The 4,180 game-icons.net icons in `public/game-icons/` are built by `node scripts/game-icons.ts` from the pinned commit, never by hand. 4,180 rows would bury `CREDITS.md`, so for this set it names the source, the commit, the change made ("Black background square removed."), and every author with their license, icon count and link, and points to the generated `index.json` as the list of every file with its author and license. The About page has a "Token icons" group, "Icons made by Lorc, Delapouite and 34 others. Available on https://game-icons.net.", with the license links and each author's count, and the picker credits each icon where it is chosen. The credits test checks that every shipped icon has an author and a license in the index, that every author is in `CREDITS.md` and `shared/credits.ts`, and that exactly the three CC0 authors are marked CC0.
- **Openverse images** (v1 wave 5, section 11.3) are the DM's choice, as any token image is, but each keeps its credit, with what the app changed, on its asset row, shown wherever the image is.
- **The X-Card** (v1 wave 5) is John Stavropoulos's safety tool, whose text is CC BY-SA 3.0. The app writes its own words and credits him, with his link, on the About page and in `CREDITS.md`.
- **Monsters from other books.** The official sourcebooks are not openly licensed, so the app ships none of their monsters. A DM enters monsters from books the group owns in the custom monster editor (section 14.6). They stay in that room's state and its backups, and nothing in the repository, tests included, holds text from any book but SRD 5.2.1.
- **SRD 5.2.1 content** (conditions text, monster stat blocks, area spells, the XP Budget per Character table) is CC-BY-4.0. `shared/srd/NOTICE.md` and the About page carry Wizards' required notice verbatim. They also state that the material was converted to JSON and edited. The app never says "D&D" or uses Wizards' logos. "Compatible with fifth edition" is allowed.
- **The passphrase word list** is built from SRD 5.2.1 names (credited with the SRD notice) and common English nouns.
